Use `docs-v2/` for new rebuild documentation. Windows treats `Docs/` and `docs/` as the same folder, but Git and GitHub's servers do not; separating the new docs avoids a local casing collision while preserving `Docs/` as the existing reference folder.

## 2026-11-16 — Lore-check resolutions (Jared)

Resolves the flags in docs-v2/spec/lore-checks.md. Decided by Jared.

- **D-01 (L-16) Era label for 3,661 BBY.** 3,661 BBY is called the Great Galactic War (year 20), not the Cold War, in phase1-answers.md and character-creation.md. The default start stays 3,661 BBY, and the start year remains a creation choice. *Reason:* the Legends timeline puts the Great Galactic War at 3,681–3,653 BBY and the Cold War at 3,653–3,642 BBY (SW_Legends_Galaxy_Timeline.md:305-311, :377, :411-417).
- **D-02 (L-03) Temple by start date; ranks.md trial rules are a design choice.** Starts before 3,653 BBY use the Jedi Temple on Coruscant under Grand Master Zym; Tython is used only for starts from 3,653 BBY. ranks.md notes that its age and trial rules (Initiate Trials, selection by 13, Council of Reassignment, five Knight Trials) are based on prequel-era Legends practice and are not attested for 3,661. *Reason:* the Order relocates to Tython only after the Sacking and Treaty of Coruscant (Timeline :410-412, :421); the lore files state no Old Republic age or trial rules.
- **D-03 (L-17) Calendar week and month count.** 7-day weeks and 12 months are Legends-sourced. *Source:* HoloNet News and *The Essential Atlas*, per Wookieepedia "Standard week/Legends". *Reason:* Jared confirmed the source, which settles the flag that the 5-day week was the only attested Legends week.
- **D-04 (L-11) Satele Shan's lightsaber.** In 3,661 BBY Satele Shan uses a blue double-bladed lightsaber. *Reason:* Legends: she wields a double-bladed saber during the Great Galactic War and a single green blade only later, in the Cold War. The lore files are silent; this confirms the legacy lore-database entry (`legacy/index.html:9057`).
- **D-05 (L-13, L-18) Lore story facts vs game mechanics.** CLAUDE.md Golden Rule 3 now reads: "Story facts in Docs/SWRPG lore/ are authoritative. Game-mechanics sections (talent effects, ability rules, numbers) are inputs that docs-v2 specs may override; log every override in decisions.md." All four talents with no legacy code are kept, translated to v2 terms: Combat Intuition (+1 to rolls when outnumbered → a circumstance modifier); Dark Affinity (strain ×0.85 → dark-side Force load ×0.85, plus faster drift); Living Force Connection (strain-free Animal Bond → no Force load for Animal Bond); Precognitive Edge (one tier earlier → a level shift on the Precognitive Reflexes unlock). *Reason:* the lore file's mechanics were written for the legacy engine, and the v2 specs replace that engine; the Legends story facts are unaffected.
  - **Overrides logged under D-05:** the v2 Shatterpoint rules (Q20, Q21) override the lore file's Path A (learned Shatterpoint, cap at level 20), ShatterSense seeding at level 5, focus and tunnel-vision levels, and interpretation strain (SW_Force_Abilities_Forms_v3_complete.md:459-500).
- **D-06 (L-05) Shatterpoint perception bands.** Re-anchored to v2 tiers: physical weaknesses 1+, combat 21+, personal 41+, relational 61+, situational 81+, event 101+ (levels on the progression-numbers.md curve). *Reason:* the lore's bands (1/6/11/21/36/51) assumed the legacy 100 × 1.07^n scale; the new bands line up with the v2 tier starts (Apprentice 21, Adept 41, Master 61, High Master 81, Legendary 101). Logged as a game-mechanics override under D-05.
- **D-07 (L-08, L-09) Removed aliases.** The legacy aliases "ForceFortitude", "Force Fortitude", "Fortitude" (→ Battlemind) and "ForceBodyControl" (→ Force Body) are removed. Unknown names are rejected and never fall back to a nearby ability. force-catalog.md's alias check is amended to match. *Reason:* no lore or Legends source uses these names, and the mappings were guesses; Force Body in particular is the most dangerous ability in its family.
- **D-08 (L-12) Renown tracks.** Republic, Sith Empire, Jedi Order, Sith, Mandalorians and Underworld. The Chiss count as Empire. *Reason:* these are the factions the 3,661 BBY lore supports (Timeline :305-311, :374-391); the Underworld covers smugglers and Nar Shaddaa, which the lore depicts but never defines as a faction.
- **D-09 (L-15) Non-catalog ability names.** Dominate Mind (KOTOR, Legends) is added as a catalog candidate. Battle Meld is era-gated and not available in 3,661 BBY. UnifyingForcePerception is dropped. Possession, Multipresence, MindWalking, ForcePsychosis and ForceHealingResurrection are listed in docs-v2/spec/force-catalog-questions.md as unsourced. *Reason:* only names with a Legends source enter the catalog (phase1-answers.md Q30).
- **D-10 (L-14) One-time exception to the Docs/ read-only rule.** Crystal "bleeding" (a Disney-era idea) was replaced with the Legends version (Sith use synthetic red crystals) in exactly two lines: Docs/SWRPG lore/SW_Force_Abilities_Forms_v3_complete.md:1434 and Docs/SWRPG lore/SW_Characters_11_RepublicEra_RiseOfEmpire.md:269. Nothing else in Docs/ changed. *Reason:* the Legends-only rule in CLAUDE.md; Jared approved the exception so the canon files don't carry the conflict.

## 2026-11-16 — Phase 2 architecture

Design changes made while writing docs-v2/architecture.md. "Jared" marks his direct decisions; the rest are architecture choices he approved with the Phase 2 plan.

- **D-11 Module layout.** Added `src/features/<name>/` (one FeatureModule each), `src/memory/` (retrieval logic), `scripts/build-lore.ts` and `tests/fixtures/ai/` to the proposed layout. `src/engine/` keeps only shared math and imports only `src/data/`; `src/state/` holds the registry, applyEvents and advanceTime. Layer and selector-only rules are enforced by ESLint (`no-restricted-imports`, `no-restricted-globals`); Jared approved `eslint` and `typescript-eslint` as dev dependencies, and `npm run lint` joins the done-check. *Reason:* the feature module contract and story-memory addenda need homes, and the dependency rule must be checked by a tool, not by review.
- **D-12 Event schemas live in their features.** Amends ai-events.md ("Every event is a Zod schema in src/ai/schemas/"): each event's Zod schema is defined in its owning FeatureModule; `src/ai/schemas/` only composes them. *Reason:* one owner per event, and the handler and schema change together.
- **D-13 Entity references are IDs only.** Amends ai-events.md ("known IDs or names"): the AI references stable IDs listed in the context. New entities enter only through npcIntroduced, newLocation and newItem. Unknown refs get an exact normalized alias match only (no fuzzy matching); otherwise the event is dropped and the name goes to the memory review queue. *Reason:* the story-memory addendum; name matching was behind V159/V169-style bugs. *Amended by D-32 (same-turn temp refs) and D-59: "the story-memory addendum" now lives in architecture §7.*
- **D-14 Choice fast path.** Each choice carries a pre-classified, validated intent; clicking it skips the intent call, so the turn makes one call. Combat beat buttons also skip it. Typed input still makes two calls. *Reason:* halves requests for the most common turn without letting narrative contradict dice (the engine still resolves before narrating). Approved by Jared. *Amended by D-58: choices carry a compact intent {actionType, targetRef?, skillId?}, expanded and validated by the engine; and by D-37: the engine decides whether a check is required.*
- **D-15 Per-turn response schema.** The response schema contains only the events legal in the current state. *Reason:* fewer schema tokens and within Gemini's schema-complexity limits.
- **D-16 Retry policy (Jared).** One automatic fresh retry only when the narrative reply is truncated (finishReason at the output limit), with a larger output budget, counted in the usage meter, and switchable off in settings. Every other failure (invalid JSON, rate limit, network) shows an error with a Retry button and makes no automatic call. Every retry reuses the same pre-rolled dice and engine results. Partial replies are never stitched. Reconciles phase1-answers Q57 with the story-memory addendum: "no continuation or recovery calls" means no stitching. *Amended by D-59: "the story-memory addendum" now lives in architecture §7; and by D-58: JSON overhead is budgeted separately.*
- **D-17 Story memory split and chapter summaries.** Memory state (facts, chapters, arc, pins, review queue) is the `memory` feature; retrieval, scoring and chapter-close policy are pure code in `src/memory/`. Chapter tags are computed by code from applied events, not written by the AI. The chapter summary and the arc summary come from the same light-model call at chapter close. Defaults: close on a major location change, a skip of 7+ days, `sceneEnded` with 8+ turns, or 30 turns. *Reason:* keeps facts code-owned and keeps the call budget at one extra light call per chapter. *Amended by D-57: the summary is a chapterSummarized event applied through applyEvents in its own commit.*
- **D-18 Staleness fallback choice.** If the staleness detector requires a forward jump and the AI's choices have none, code appends a deterministic "Skip ahead: <top open thread>" choice and logs it. Suggest only, never auto-skip. Approved by Jared. *Reason:* the rule must hold without trusting the AI, and this is a code-generated option, not a repaired event.
- **D-19 Pacing and memory defaults.** Pacing ranges per turn: moment 5 s-15 min, scene 15 min-12 h, montage 1-120 days, timeSkip 7 days-10 years. Settling-in 14 in-game days; staleness 21 days; chapter max 30 turns. All config values. Approved by Jared as starting defaults.
- **D-20 No skill-tier gates (Jared).** Removes the progression addendum's rule that entering a new skill tier needs a milestone flag and that "XP accumulates while gated". Gating already exists where it should: lightsaber forms through the drill ceiling (25 + E), combat Force abilities through combat integration, teaching through the teacher factor. Physical stats and non-combat Force abilities have no gates. Crossing a tier (21, 41, 61, 81, 101) triggers a short, non-blocking milestone beat: code detects it and the next scene acknowledges it (a master's recognition, Temple reputation, a fact record). It never holds back XP or levels. Ranks keep their gates as ranks.md defines. *Amended by D-59: "the progression addendum" is not in the repo; the rules it is compared with are in architecture §12 and progression-targets.md.*
- **D-21 Speed multipliers stay as specified (Jared).** The progression addendum's list (focus, domain talent, teacher quality, real combat experience, multiplied, capped ~4x) is not used. M = talent x cross-talent x instruction x age x state of mind x alignment, capped at 2.8 (progression-numbers.md sec 5). Focus comes from hours and weights; combat experience comes from combat XP, E and CI (combat-xp.md sec 4). Total pace is capped at 4.0x the Average profile. *Amended by D-59 (the progression addendum is not in the repo) and D-48: the 4.0x cap is enforced by a clamp, not only by test.*
- **D-22 Roll divisor.** rolls.md (d20 + effective level / 10) supersedes v170-audit.md's "d20 + level / 4" wording. *Reason:* rolls.md is the decided spec, with tables and tests built on /10.
- **D-23 Integer dates.** The clock stores an absolute GSC day (signed; BBY negative) and a minute of day. No date strings in state; age, weekday and labels are derived. *Reason:* fixes the V170 string-parsing date bugs (BUG-026 and 13-calendar-and-time.md) at the root. *Amended by D-30: seconds of day (0..86399), not minutes; and by D-38: calendar rules and year 0.*
- **D-24 Settings and usage outside GameState.** API key, model choices, limits and the usage meter live in localStorage, not in saves. *Reason:* they belong to the device, not the campaign, and the key must never be exported.
- **D-25 Training budget interpolated by age (approved, Golden Rule 7).** Replaces the 3-row step table in training-routines.md sec 2 (12 and under / 13-17 / 18+) with a linear interpolation through the same values (anchors at ages 10, 15 and 20, flat outside), so a birthday never jumps the budget. Same targets and tests.
- **D-26 Token estimates.** The context builder estimates tokens as characters / ratio, calibrated per model from Gemini `usageMetadata`; no countTokens request. *Reason:* the request budget.
- **D-27 No credit events before the equipment spec.** CREDITS= has no v2 event until the equipment spec (M8) defines prices and the economy. Approved by Jared.
- **D-28 Build order replaces the pack's Phases 3-8 (Jared).** The M1-M9 milestones in architecture.md sec 15 replace the prompt pack's Phases 3-8. Each milestone is its own session: plan mode, then build. The pack prompts and the specs are checklists for the matching milestone. Each milestone ends playable locally (`npm run dev`), tested and committed on the `rebuild` branch. Nothing deploys to GitHub Pages until the Phase 9 cutover; the deploy workflow keeps its main-only trigger. *Amended by D-34 and D-56: rolls §1, §2, §7 move to M1; the closed form and Human body cap move to M3; stubs are named per milestone. "The prompt pack" is not in the repo (D-59).*

## 2026-11-16 — Fable review fixes

Applies docs-v2/review-fixes.md (Jared's decision on every row of docs-v2/fable-review.md).
"Row n" is the review row. Numbers marked "computed" come from the scripts in docs-v2/calc/
(plain Node, no dependencies; see docs-v2/calc/README.md), not estimates. D-39 to D-42 and
D-67 record conflicts found while computing; Jared chose D-41, D-42 and the D-67 direction.

### Fix before M1 (core contract)
- **D-29 (rows 1, 2, 5) Dice and actions: one entry point, resolved before narration.** Every action that rolls dice or computes an outcome (training, Force use, tactics, healing, hazards the player causes, fights) enters only through the intent and is resolved in step b. New intent fields: `skillId`, `activity`, `healing`, `risk`, actionTypes `heal` and `sleep`. activityPerformed, forceUseRequested, tacticDeclared and healingReceived are removed from the narrative schema. hazardOccurred and combatStarted stay but are deferred: applied at the start of the next turn's step b and narrated then, like ambush. A narrative event for an action the TurnDraft already resolved is rejected and logged. Durations and intensities come from the intent (clamped by pacing) or the routine, never the AI. architecture §2's "the AI never supplies a number" becomes "the AI supplies only bounded enums and clamped amounts", with the full list. *Reason:* the V153-V162 root cause (prose written before dice) and the V149 double-count pattern. Amends architecture §2, §3, §4 and the legacy tag map.
- **D-30 (rows 3, 4) Clock in seconds; one time writer per turn.** `now = {day, second 0..86399}`; timeElapsed carries seconds. If the TurnDraft advanced time (duel exchanges, montage, skip, activity duration, sleep), timeElapsed is left out of that turn's schema; otherwise the AI's value applies, clamped to the pacing range. Amends D-23 (minute of day) and Q48. *Reason:* moment pacing starts at 5 s and exchanges are 5-10 s; two writers of one value is the V152/V160 class.
- **D-31 (row 6) architecture §3 is the single schema source.** ai-events.md is marked "Superseded by architecture.md §3" and kept as history, with every difference listed at its top: intent checks, unknownNames, skillId, activity, healing, risk and the requestedSpan shape {amount, unit}; choices[].intent; factLearned.importance; threadOpened.entityRefs; temp keys; milestoneBeatAcknowledged; chapterSummarized; timeElapsed seconds; the removed and deferred events; npcStatusChanged; hatred. Only D-12 and D-13 had logged differences before. *Reason:* M1 must build one contract.
- **D-32 (row 7) applyEvents: temp refs and apply order.** Creation events (npcIntroduced, newLocation, newItem, threadOpened) may carry a local key `tmp:1`, `tmp:2`...; later events in the same reply and scene refs may use it; applyEvents maps temp keys to the minted IDs and rejects unknown or forward keys. Apply order: (1) entity creation, (2) time, (3) everything else in reply order; deferred events from the previous turn run in step b first. *Reason:* new entities could not be referenced in the turn that created them (every first appearance went to the review queue).
- **D-33 (row 8) Flag registry; rank and death only through the engine.** New spec docs-v2/spec/flag-registry.md lists every flag with its allowed sources. Rank-gating flags (masterApproval, heroicAct, trial results, deeds feeding standing, redemptionTurningPoint, councilBypassGranted) are engine- or player-set only; the AI may only propose events the engine evaluates. The Council bypass is an engine-run story event with written conditions (wartime, a heroicAct, standing >= 75, age 18+, Padawan, all Knight minimums met), checked monthly by the ranks hook. NPC death comes only from the health model (a resolved fight or hazard), a player choice at incapacitation, or a code-run Legends timeline event; `witnessed` is removed. The masterApproval and heroicAct conditions are design choices made to write the registry. Amends ranks.md §1, alignment.md §7 and architecture §2-3. *Reason:* the V170 RANK= problem by another route, and one AI event could kill a canon NPC.
- **D-34 (row 12) Checks before M5.** The basic roll, checks and the seeded roll card (rolls.md §1, §2, §7) move into M1 as pure engine, using the stubs named in architecture §15. Duels stay in M5. Amends D-28's table. *Reason:* otherwise the AI decides check outcomes for four milestones and the contract changes in M5.
- **D-35 (row 13) One ordered day loop.** architecture §10 holds the only hook list, with one signature `onTimeAdvanced(slice, step: TimeStep, ctx)` used everywhere: routine blocks -> health -> progression -> alignment -> npcs and relationships -> threads -> ranks -> world (owns weighted event rolls and timeline events) -> pacing (settling-in, staleness) -> interruption check. §9 and §11 refer to §10. *Reason:* §9 and §10 disagreed and threads could advance twice a day.
- **D-36 (row 15) Per-turn caps (config `eventCaps`).** moralChoice 1 per turn, emotionalEvent 1 per turn, relationshipChanged 1 per NPC per turn, factLearned 5 per turn. Duplicates of the same type and target in one reply are merged (first kept, deltas not summed); events past a cap are rejected and logged. *Reason:* the AI could swing alignment or standing in one reply.
- **D-37 (row 16) The engine decides when a check is required.** From actionType, targets and skillId (a config table per action type). The AI only proposes a difficulty, which the engine clamps to a range per action type; missing proposals use the type's default. *Reason:* on the fast path a choice with no check was a free success (legacy NO_ROLL_NEEDED).
- **D-38 (row 22) Calendar rules (design choices, logged as such).** Legends gives 12 months and 368 days (D-03). Design choices: numbered months, 1-8 have 31 days and 9-12 have 30 (248 + 120 = 368); year 0 exists, as in the lore timeline (0 BBY = 0 ABY, the Battle of Yavin, SW_Legends_Galaxy_Timeline.md:722-725), signed years (BBY negative, ABY positive), year 0 shown as "0 BBY/ABY"; 7-day weeks run continuously across years and absolute day 0 is weekday 1. Day 0 stays the first day of 1 ABY (least change to architecture §2), so year y starts at 368 * (y - 1). The rollover test becomes 1 BBY -> 0 BBY/ABY -> 1 ABY, plus the month 8 -> 9 boundary. Amends Q47 and architecture §2, §10.

### Found while computing (Jared's choices)
- **D-39 Fatigue reference days recomputed (progression-numbers §20 test 4).** The test 4 values came from a simplified run in chat. docs-v2/calc/fatigue-reference.mjs implements the full §3 rules (30-minute steps, same-activity x0.85, post-workout window, cross-meter penalties); every case matches within 0.01 once the single-stat cases use Willpower 30, which the original run had assumed without stating it. The mismatch was an unstated input, not two rules contradicting each other. Test 4 now states Willpower 30 and the recomputed values: End 30 3h 2.67 | 6h 3.39 | End 90 6h 4.52 | 8h 4.88 | easy 1.36 | study then drills 1.99 / 2.57 | drills then study 2.76 / 1.87 | 3h + 3h 2.76 / 2.26. The §9 willpower references reproduce exactly (0.00, 1.42, 2.88, 3.00) and test 5 holds (8h vs 6h +8.1%, f_phys 1.21). Per Jared: keep the spec rules, recompute, carry on.
- **D-40 Average profile schedule; K re-solved (Jared).** The Average profile is a Jedi Guardian on the standard routine: the Order requirements for its rank (ranks.md §1a) as fixed commitments, filled up to the D-25 training budget by archetype shares, 300 training days per 368-day year. Padawan at 12, Knight at 20.5, Temple instructors x1.05, master x1.15, solo x0.90, no talent, peacetime (one real skirmish a year keeps the drill ceiling above the main form; it never binds). Obi-Wan, Anakin and Luke use the same structure and change only effort, talents, teachers, rank ages and combat events. The scheduler follows training-routines §3-4 (2 h session cap, physical first, a mental block after each physical one, meditation last, rest when f_phys > 0.7, trim fill hours to the effort's tiredness limit; fixed commitments are never trimmed). "Before fixed commitments" in training-routines §2 is read as "the budget includes them". K re-solved = 14.69 (computed by progression-sim.mjs; Average reaches 41 at exactly 16.0 years). Provisional until D-67.
- **D-41 Main specialty takes 0.5 of the fill from Knight on (Jared).** Conflict found: with the plain Jedi Guardian shares after Knighthood (main form 0.23 of the fill, 1.4 h/day), Average reached 61 at 47.7 years (target 32.1, limit 38.5) and 81 at 91.8 (target 52.7). Jared chose: from Knight on, each archetype's main specialty (not always the lightsaber form, e.g. Force Knowledge for a Consular) takes 0.5 of the fill and the rest keeps the archetype's proportions. This is the default for the Average profile and NPCs; for the player their actual routine decides, and the routine's default template for Knight+ uses the same 0.5 split. Computed Average result: 21 at 5.4 y, 41 at 16.0, 61 at 34.3, 81 at 57.5, 101 at 86.5, all within +/-20% (variants in calc/variants.mjs).
- **D-42 (row 28) Order requirements table, Padawan line amended (Jared).** ranks.md §1a now holds the single table: Initiate 2 h forms + 1 h Force + 1 h study; Padawan 2 h with the master + 1 h solo; Knight and above none mandatory (missions instead); training-routines.md points to it. Conflict found: with exactly that table, the Average profile had Force Control 24.6 and Force Sense 7.3 at 20.5 against the decided Knight minimums of 30. Jared chose: the Initiate Force hour is split 0.5 h Force Control + 0.5 h Force Sense, and the Padawan requirement adds 1 h Force Control and 1 h Force Sense with the master (instruction for the teacher factor). Computed at 20.5: Force Control 30.5, Force Sense 28.2. Correction to what I told Jared when he chose: I said the minimums would hold; Force Sense is 28.2, still below 30 (logged under D-67). Adept still lands at exactly 16.0 years with K re-solved. Sith rows are not defined by row 28; the stubs use the Initiate and Padawan rows until M7.

### Fix before the milestone that needs it
- **D-43 (row 9) NPC closed form with the age-factor integral.** level = 41 * (pace * A_eff / A_ref)^(1/1.75), A = exact piecewise-linear integral of the progression-numbers §16 age factor for the skill's category, A_ref = A(technique, 4, 20) = 17.98, war years at 0.3 x the current age factor. Test 2 (computed, against the day-by-day Average): 10 y +1.7, 30 y +0.2, 60 y -1.6, inside +/-2 (the old form was +4.3 at 60 y and failed). The age factor alone was enough; no budget-by-age term was needed. Recomputed reference table: Padawan 16: 35.3 | Knight 30: 52.8 | Master 45: 66.9 | Elder 70: 85.5 | Obi-Wan check 78.6 | Anakin check 78.4 | trooper sergeant 42.8 | Sith warrior 76.1. Character creation's expected values follow: Knight at 24 = 46.0 (was 47), Initiate at 9 = 22.2 (was about 21).
- **D-44 (row 10) Archetypes are shares of one feasible daily budget.** The shares sum to 1. The hours come from the path's default routine (Order requirements + D-25 budget), and the main specialty takes 0.5 of the fill (D-41). The other skills split the remaining 0.5 by their old weights ("physical w" = w each for Strength, Agility and Endurance). The main specialty is the first skill listed. Pace = share / 0.5. Before Knight, the Jedi phase paces are measured from the Average profile. Old totals were 1.1x-5.2x the Average main-skill pace (Guardian 3.9x, Sentinel 5.2x); with shares every level is reachable in play (new test 7). Character creation uses shares (focusWeights sum to 1).
- **D-45 (row 11) Drill ceiling.** Sparring XP is ceiling-limited (it counts as drill XP for the ceiling and the bank); real fights are not. Test 8 is restated: "with sparring only (E <= 15, ceiling 40), a form gains at most 25% of normal XP above 40". After day 60, whatever is left in the bank stays banked for the next real fight (not lost), and the next skirmish or higher starts a new release; test 9 is fixed to match (a 10%-per-day release never empties the bank).
- **D-46 (row 14) Single owners.** The current location lives only in locations.currentId (pacing.scene reads a selector). NPC death lives only in npcs.status; health emits the status event. Only player pins are stored, and engine pins are derived. Grief is a lingering condition (progression.grief), separate from the state of mind: it keeps its x0.80 and its dark-pressure floor even while anger or calm is active. alignment.griefFloor is removed and derived by a selector. Amends progression-numbers §17 ("one state at a time") and alignment.md §3.
- **D-47 (row 17) Combat daily counters in progression.** fightsToday and the per-skill daily combat XP totals (for the 12 XP cap) move from the combat slice (null outside a fight) to the progression slice, reset by the progression day hook. *Reason:* the third-fight halving and the cap reset whenever a fight ended.
- **D-48 (row 18) 4.0x pace clamp.** Per skill, over a rolling 365-day window, credited XP cannot exceed 4.0 x Y_avg(age) x (the character's share for that skill / the Average main share 0.5). Y_avg(age) is the Average profile's main-skill XP for the 365 days starting at that age. Computed with K = 14.69, per 365 days: age 4: 574 | 8: 538 | 12: 677 | 16: 645 | 20: 637 | 24: 502 | 30: 520 | 40: 567 | 50: 526 | 60: 563 | 70: 512 | 80: 515 | 90: 476 | 98: 319. The excess is discarded and logged. Banked drill XP is excluded. Tests 2 and 24 check the clamp. Baseline chosen by Jared: the Average main skill at the same age, scaled by share.
- **D-49 (row 19) Lifetime test length.** Average runs 95 years, so its Legendary target at 77.5 years is inside the run. The others run 70. Computed Average Legendary: 86.5 years.
- **D-50 (row 20) Duel odds test.** rolls.md test 5 allows up to 2.5 points per level (the reference table itself has 2.0 between gaps 0 and 5).
- **D-51 (row 21) Willpower training.** Every archetype has a Willpower weight (Jedi 0.4, Sith 0.4, others 0.2). Willpower meditation and the endurance-ordeal drill train it directly, in addition to fatigue; the 3.0/day cap applies to fatigue Willpower only (test 12 amended). Recomputed Knight minimum: Willpower >= 48 (the Average profile meets it at 20.3; it was 35). Today about 60% of that Willpower comes from fatigue, because of D-67.
- **D-52 (row 23) Rest and sleep.** Elapsed time not covered by an activity is rest, at the progression-numbers §3 and health §2-3 recovery rates, in story turns and jumps alike. Sleep is an intent action (actionType sleep) or a routine block, resolved by the engine. 7h+ of sleep resets per the specs.
- **D-53 (row 24) Skills and activities spec.** New docs-v2/spec/skills-and-activities.md lists every skill with its category (technique, force, wisdom, physical, academic, knowledge), mapped to the age, rust and body-cap rules, and every activity with loads, default intensity, drill ratings and weights. It is sourced from Docs/SW_Activity_Bank_v1.md and the Force lore file. The bank's strain biases are replaced by the §3 load categories (a mechanics override under D-05). Design choices: the career activities' weights, Willpower meditation, the endurance-ordeal drill, and the stand-in drills for forms with no bank entry. Willpower is categorized as wisdom.
- **D-54 (row 25) State of mind: one enum and one mapping.** progression-numbers §17 owns StateOfMind = centered / neutral / distracted / fear / anger / hatred / pride / despair, plus lingering grief. Event mapping: grief -> grief, fear -> fear, anger -> anger, hatred -> hatred (added to emotionalEvent), calm -> centered, pride -> pride, despair -> despair. Distracted comes only from engine rules (an unresolved thread with a deadline within 3 days, or an overtraining streak of 2+). Interpretations needed to fill the table: despair x0.80, easing like grief but not lingering; pride x1.00 for XP (its effect is the rolls.md §5 defense -1); centered no longer requires "no distress", so it stacks with grief; strength order hatred > anger > fear > despair > pride > distracted > centered > neutral.
- **D-55 (row 26) computeXp combat branch.** One computeXp with a combat session shape {source: combat, stakes, outcome won / lost / fled / truce, exchanges from the duel log, forceUses}. It implements combat-xp.md §1 exactly: no hours, no fatigue, and the fight outcome instead of the rolled session outcome. Caps, E/CI, the sparring ceiling and the clamp are applied by the same function.
- **D-56 (row 29) Build order and named stubs.** The NPC closed form and the Human body-cap table move into M3. architecture §15 adds a stubs table per milestone: roll modifiers until M3/M5, teacher levels = Average closed form until M5, Order requirements as static data until M7, alignment multiplier = 1.0 until M6, Human body cap until M8, scripted combat until M5. Amends D-28's table.
- **D-57 (row 30) chapterSummarized event.** The summary call returns a chapterSummarized event (source: summary call, owner memory), applied through applyEvents and committed in its own transaction after the turn commit. Amends D-17 and architecture §4 and §7. *Reason:* the summary was a state write outside applyEvents.
- **D-58 (row 31) Output budget and compact choice intents.** JSON overhead (choices, scene, events) is budgeted separately (config `jsonOverheadTokens`, starting at 300) on top of the per-level narrative cap. Choices carry a compact intent {actionType, targetRef?, skillId?}, and the engine fills the rest (default activity, span from pacingHint, default difficulty) and validates it. Amends D-14.

### Documentation hygiene
- **D-59 (row 27) Stale references.** "Prompt pack" and addendum citations now point to the architecture sections that hold them: pacing §8, story memory §7, and the AI contract §3. The pack's Health and Rolls sections point to health.md and rolls.md. The species and equipment specs are listed as "planned" (architecture §15). "The ranks spec (to come)" points to ranks.md. Applied in architecture, character-creation.md §1, §4-5, npc-generation.md §4, phase1-answers Q56, 00-index Q56, v170-audit and the D-13, D-16, D-20, D-21 and D-28 entries.
- **D-60 (row 32) Q51 and Q52 match architecture.** Thread escalation is the engine event threadEscalated (not threadProgressed, an AI-text event). Threads are owned by the threads feature, not story memory.
- **D-61 (row 33) CLAUDE.md.** Golden rule 2 covers shared math in src/engine/ and feature rule code in src/features/. Golden rule 6 adds `npm run lint`.
- **D-62 (row 34) Amended-by lines.** Added in every superseded place the review lists: training-routines §2 (D-25), v170-audit "level ÷ 4" (D-22) and "Phases 2 and 5" (D-28), phase1-answers Q12 (D-02), and ai-contract-legacy's "owners still need deciding" (D-31). Lines were also added in the other places this session changed: Q11, Q15, Q21, Q32, Q40, Q41, Q45, Q47, Q48, Q51, Q52 and Q56, and decisions D-13, D-14, D-16, D-17, D-20, D-21, D-23 and D-28.
- **D-63 (row 35) DEFAULT answers copied into their specs, plus a non-combat Shatterpoint rule.**
  - Q11 (philosophy offset) is in character-creation §2 and alignment §1.
  - Q21 (read costs 0.3 Force load; no focus or tunnel vision) is in force-catalog and health §2.
  - Q32 (form change at a beat costs 1 advantage step) is in rolls §3.
  - Q40 (passive Willpower resistance) and Q41 (suppression crash) are in health §8.
  - Q45 (breaking point at D 90, no discharge) is in alignment §3 and flag-registry.
  - Q48 (a skip ends in the morning) is in architecture §9.
  - New rule: a non-combat Shatterpoint read is a check against DC = 10 + band start / 10 from the D-06 band (physical 10, combat 12, personal 14, relational 16, situational 18, event 20). It needs a level at or above the band start, and a negative margin is a misread (force-catalog.md).
- **D-64 (row 36) Alignment in M.** progression-numbers §5 and test 24 include the alignment multiplier, matching D-21 and alignment.md §8.
- **D-65 (row 37) Continuous edges.**
  - Alignment tiers are half-open intervals on continuous Q, with the same tiers for integer Q. r(Q) = 0 for Q < -75 to match the Consumed tier and §7.
  - Combat XP's challenge ramps linearly from 0.3 at gap -25 to 0 at gap -35, with no step at -30. combat-xp test 3 now reads "more than 35 levels weaker".
  - npcStatusChanged can return an NPC to active.
- **D-66 (row 38) Padawan selection recomputed.**
  - Initiate Trial minimums are main form >= 24 and Force Control >= 14. The Average profile meets both at age 10.8; the old 18 / 15 were met at 8.1 / 11.7.
  - The skill percentile is the composite (main form, Force Control, Force Sense), ranked against a seeded population of 1,000 NPC Initiates of the same age (closed form, Jedi start ages 2-5, talent distribution, variance). For reference, the Average profile sits at about the 16th percentile.
  - The monthly chance becomes S / 685. The real window runs from age 10.8 to 13, which is 26 monthly rolls. At S 50 the monthly chance is 7.30%, and 13.9% are never chosen (13.8% in a seeded 10,000-run check). The old divisor 800 gave 18.7% over this window.
  - Computed by padawan-selection.mjs.

### Open
- **D-67 Open for the M3/M4 lifetime-test session (Jared's direction). Decided in M3 by D-102.**
  - **Finding:** under the D-40 to D-42 schedule, the Average profile's trained Endurance stays near 5 until age 20. Physical stats get hours only through secondary weights and the fill, and the tiredness limit trims the fill first (about 2.3 h per training day). That keeps fatigue capacity near 3.2 and pushes fixed-commitment days to a day-end f of 1.08.
  - **Consequences:** Force Sense reaches only 28.2 at 20.5, below the decided Knight minimum of 30. Knight+ secondary skills barely grow. About 60% of the Average's Willpower comes from fatigue. character-creation test 2 misses its ±1 (the closed form gives 22.2 at 5 years against 20.1 in the day loop). The peacetime baselines of Obi-Wan (41 at 13.3 y), Anakin (7.9 y) and Luke run slow, since they also lack combat XP.
  - **Proposed direction:** (a) an untrained natural baseline that grows with age (Endurance about half the body cap for age, Willpower rising with age), with training adding on top; (b) a conditioning hour in the Initiate and Padawan Order requirements. Decide and tune both in the M3/M4 lifetime-test session, then re-solve K and re-run every docs-v2/calc script; the numbers above that depend on K are provisional until then.
  - The Knight Force Sense minimum stays 30 until then.

## 2026-11-16 — M1 (core and talk to the GM)

- **D-68 Gemini transport, models and limits (checked 2026-11-16; answers architecture §16 Q59).** Sources: aistudio.google.com/docs (ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs now redirects there): models, structured-output, rate-limits, pricing, migrate-to-interactions, and the Interactions API reference.
  - **Transport (Jared): `generateContent`.** `POST https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models/{model}:generateContent`, key in the `x-goog-api-key` header (never the URL). `generationConfig` carries `responseMimeType: "application/json"`, `responseJsonSchema`, `temperature`, `maxOutputTokens` and the thinking config; the reply has `candidates[0].content.parts`, `finishReason` (MAX_TOKENS = truncated) and `usageMetadata`. Google now labels generateContent "legacy, fully supported" with no sunset date.
  - **Known future option: the Interactions API** (GA June 2026, recommended by Google for new projects; new models launch there first). `POST v1beta/interactions` with `store: false` for stateless calls (storage is on by default), `system_instruction`, `generation_config {thinking_level minimal/low/medium/high/xhigh, max_output_tokens}`, structured output in `response_format {type, mime_type, schema}`; output in `steps[].content[]`, truncation as `status: "incomplete"`, usage in `usage.total_input_tokens` / `total_output_tokens`. Temperature and turn roles were not shown in the reference read. The gemini adapter stays thin so an Interactions adapter can be added without touching anything outside `src/ai/`.
  - **Structured-output schema subset:** type (null via a type array), title, description, properties, required, additionalProperties, enum, format, minimum, maximum, items, prefixItems, minItems, maxItems, anyOf, $ref. minLength, maxLength and similar constraints are silently stripped, so Zod enforces them after parsing. Overly large or deeply nested schemas are rejected with 400 INVALID_ARGUMENT (hence the schema size-cap test).
  - **Models.** Narrative default `gemini-3.6-flash` (Jared); intent, summary and OOC default `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` (Google's recommended fast, cheap model for new projects). Candidates: `gemini-3.8-flash` (stable; the models page calls it "previous-generation Flash"), `gemini-4-hydrogen` (Google's suggestion for new projects, Flash-class speed, $1.50 / $7.50 per 1M paid, may need the Interactions API). `gemini-2.5-flash` is deprecated and routes to 3.5 Flash-Lite from 2026-12-01. All model choices are Settings values.
  - **Free-tier limits.** Google no longer publishes per-model free limits; the official page says to read them on the AI Studio rate-limit page for your project. RPD resets at midnight Pacific; limits are per project, not per key. Best available figures, from a third-party snapshot of the AI Studio page (October 2026), **unverified until Jared checks his AI Studio limits**:

    | Model | RPM | Input TPM | RPD |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | gemini-3.6-flash | 5 | 250,000 | 20 |
    | gemini-3.8-flash | 5 | 250,000 | 20 |
    | gemini-3.5-flash-lite | 10 (another source says 15) | 250,000 | 500 |
    | gemini-4-hydrogen | not found | not found | not found |

    These are the editable Settings defaults (Flash-Lite uses the lower 10 RPM). Consequence: about 20 narrated turns a day on free Flash; the intent call runs on Flash-Lite.
- **D-69 Check grades (design interpretation of rolls.md §2).** rolls.md §2 says check margins "grade the result like section 3". Successes reuse the §3 strike bands: narrow 0-3, clear 4-7, strong 8-11, decisive 12-15, exceptional 16+. Failures mirror them on the shortfall: narrow -1 to -3, clear -4 to -7, bad -8 or worse. Config in `src/data/checks.config.ts`.
- **D-70 Fight intents resolve as one check until M5 (new stub).** Until duels exist (rolls.md §3, M5), a `fight` intent is one engine check against the clamped difficulty (easy..extreme, default moderate), with a note on the roll card. Named stub `STUB_FIGHT_AS_SINGLE_CHECK` in `src/data/stubs.ts`; added to the architecture §15 stubs table. *Reason:* otherwise fight turns have no engine outcome for four milestones and the AI would decide them, which D-29 and D-37 forbid.
- **D-75 TimeStep shape (implementation detail of architecture §10).** `TimeStep = {kind: subDay / day, fromAbs, toAbs, seconds, endsDay}`. Steps never cross midnight; every second of a span is in exactly one step; `kind: 'day'` is a whole day (00:00-24:00), `subDay` a part of one; `endsDay` is true when the step ends at midnight, so per-day hooks run exactly once for every day that ends, including partial days. A clock-moving event (timeElapsed) returns an `advance` request that applyEvents runs through advanceTime inside the time phase, so the hooks run and the apply order (creation, time, the rest) holds. The character feature has a notices-only day hook (birthdays), placed before the §10 list since it changes no state.
- **D-71 Schemas grow with their features (applies D-15 to M1).** The M1 intent schema has actionType, targets, references, unknownNames, skillId, proposedDifficulty, requestedPacing and requestedSpan; activity, tactic, forceAbility, healing and risk are added by their features' milestones. The M1 narrative schema has narrative, choices (compact intents), scene {moodTags} and the events timeElapsed and pacingSuggested; scene.locationRef and presentNpcRefs arrive with M2. *Reason:* fields with no resolver would let the AI ask for outcomes nothing computes.
- **D-72 An intent duration is the engine's time advance, and it sets the pacing.** A `requestedSpan` (or sleep, default 8 h) is advanced by the engine in step b, so timeElapsed is left out of that turn's schema (D-30). The pacing level becomes the smallest M1 level whose range holds the span (moment up to 15 min, else scene); spans over 12 h clamp to 12 h with a logged note until montage arrives in M4. Rest without a span leaves the time to the AI's timeElapsed. *Reason:* clamping an 8-hour sleep to the moment range (15 min) would be wrong; the player's stated duration is the input.
- **D-74 Minimal M1 context builder.** gmRules (core-gm, contract, pacing section, opening when relevant) + a state block (name, derived age, date, time, pacing, mood, valid IDs) + the resolved block + the newest raw turns that fit the 8,000-token recentTurns cap, never split (tokens = characters / 4). M2 replaces it with the full capped builder (memory, lore, arc, per-section report).
- **D-76 Wire schema and parse schema.** The narrative call sends a strict response schema (only this turn's legal events) to Gemini, but parses the reply with events as loose objects; the pipeline then rejects event types not legal this turn and applyEvents validates each remaining event (Zod, then engine). *Reason:* architecture §3 drops invalid events one by one; a strict parse would discard the whole reply for one bad event. The legal-type filter keeps D-30 (one time writer) enforced in code, not only by the wire schema.
- **D-77 Request timeout and intent normalization (found in the M1 live test).** (a) The gemini adapter aborts a request with no reply after 90 s (`REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`) and reports it as a `timeout` error with a Retry button, no automatic call (D-16). *Reason:* one 3.6-flash narrative call took about a minute; with no timeout a stalled request would leave the game on "thinking" forever. (b) Empty optional strings in the intent (`skillId: ""` from gemini-3.5-flash-lite, blank list entries) are treated as absent right after parsing (`normalizeIntent`). *Reason:* "" must never reach the resolver as a skill or entity ID once skills exist (M3).
- **D-78 Live model comparison (2026-11-16, M1 wrap-up; Jared's key, free tier).** One campaign, 7 turns: opening, 1 typed check turn and 1 choice turn on 3.6-flash; the same pair on 3.8-flash and on 3.5-flash-lite (intent always on 3.5-flash-lite). `thinkingLevel: "low"` was accepted by all three models (no 400).
  | Narrative model | Calls | Structured output | Rejected events | Check adherence | Latency (two-call / one-call turn) | Thought tokens | Prose |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | gemini-3.6-flash | 3 | 3/3 valid | 0 | bad failure narrated as a public stumble | ~60 s / ~25 s | 0-157 | 2 paragraphs, good Temple detail |
  | gemini-3.8-flash | 2 | 2/2 valid | 0 | narrow success narrated as a faint, measured sense | ~20 s / ~15-20 s | 0 | 2-3 paragraphs, the richest and most precise |
  | gemini-3.5-flash-lite | 2 | 2/2 valid | 0 | clear failure narrated as "unable to read his mood" | ~10 s / <9 s | 570-750 | 1 paragraph, plainer, vaguer choice labels |
  - Intent calls (3/3 on 3.5-flash-lite) were valid and classified correctly (investigate, forceUse, investigate), ~590 tokens in, 40-60 out.
  - Small continuity slip across models: 3.8-flash called the master "their", Flash-Lite "he" (no character records until M2).
  - Reading: every model kept the contract (no invented outcomes, realistic timeElapsed 10-60 s, no rejected events). 3.8-flash gave the best prose and was faster than 3.6-flash in this sample; Flash-Lite is usable and the fastest but noticeably thinner. The sample is small (2-3 calls per model). The default stays gemini-3.6-flash until Jared decides; switching the default to 3.8-flash or 3.5-flash-lite is a one-line config change (`DEFAULT_MODELS`) and the header picker switches per scene.
- **D-79 Narrative default is gemini-3.8-flash (Jared).** `DEFAULT_MODELS.narrative` changes from gemini-3.6-flash to gemini-3.8-flash. *Reason:* the D-78 live comparison: 3.8-flash gave the richest, most precise prose with full structured-output validity and was faster than 3.6-flash (about 20 s vs 60 s for a two-call turn). gemini-3.5-flash-lite stays the suggested fallback, marked "fallback" in the header picker, for when the Flash daily quota runs low. Existing saved Settings keep their chosen model; the default applies to new Settings. Amends D-68's narrative default.
- **D-80 Automatic model fallback on an exhausted daily quota (Jared). Amends D-16.**
  - **Lists.** Settings hold an ordered model list per call type (`modelLists`), replacing one model per call type. Defaults: narrative gemini-3.8-flash, then gemini-3.5-flash-lite; intent, summary and OOC gemini-3.5-flash-lite only. The header picker puts the chosen model first and keeps the rest as fallbacks. One API key only.
  - **When a model counts as exhausted.** (a) The API answers 429 and the body's `google.rpc.QuotaFailure` names a per-day quota (quotaId containing "PerDay"); or (b) the usage meter shows the model's requests-per-day limit from Settings reached. The exhausted model is stored with its Pacific date (localStorage `swrpg-v2:quota`, outside saves like D-24); after the Pacific midnight reset it is the first choice again, and older entries are pruned.
  - **What happens.** A model call uses the first non-exhausted model in its list. On a daily-quota 429 the model is marked exhausted and the same call is made once on the next model in the list, reusing the same TurnDraft (dice never re-rolled); each model is tried at most once per call. The transcript records the 429 (`rateLimit:day`) and a report note; the header shows "Flash daily limit reached; using Flash-Lite until reset" (or "... no fallback left" when the list is used up).
  - **What does not.** A per-minute 429, or a 429 whose body does not name the quota window, is never a fallback reason: no automatic call, the normal Retry button (D-16 unchanged for those).
  - **D-16 as amended:** automatic calls are the one truncation retry (autoRetry on) and one call per remaining fallback model after a daily-quota 429; nothing else.
  - **Settings migration.** Settings saved before D-80 (`models`) load as lists: a value equal to its D-68 default becomes the new default list; a model the player chose stays first, followed by the defaults.
  - *Reason:* the free tier allows about 20 Flash requests a day (D-68, unverified); falling back to Flash-Lite's separate quota keeps the game playable instead of stopping at the Flash limit.

## 2026-11-17 — M2 (memory and lore)

D-81 to D-89 are the eight plan choices Jared approved for M2, plus the "major place kinds" config he asked for. D-90 to D-96 came out of the live play-through.

- **D-81 Lore index holds story text only (approved).** scripts/build-lore.ts parses Docs/SWRPG lore/ (character files and the galaxy timeline; the Force abilities file waits for M6) into src/lore/generated/lore-index.json (gitignored, rebuilt before dev, build, test and typecheck).
  - **What is dropped:** stat tables, "(game stats)" lines, form ratings and sheet snapshots, because NPC numbers belong to the engine (Golden Rule 3). Sentences about the game rather than the story are dropped too: stat talk, and the lore files' assumption that the player belongs to the Shan line ("the same Shan line your player-character belongs to"). The player character is generic.
  - **Chunks and eras:** character and bench chunks take their file's era range. Timeline rows are grouped by year and are eligible only up to the current year, up to 10 years back (Q49: no future list).
  - **Canon links:** an introduced NPC whose exact normalized name matches an in-era lore person gets canonId = lore:slug through an engine event, npcCanonLinked, which the pipeline applies after the reply.
- **D-82 Relationship scale (approved).** value += delta x 5, clamped to +/-100. Derived labels: hostile below -59, unfriendly -59..-20, neutral -19..19, friendly 20..59, close 60+. Drift toward neutral arrives with time advance in M4.
- **D-83 Memory retrieval weights (design values, src/data/memory.config.ts).**
  - **Score:** tag overlap per query source (scene 30, referenced 40, mentioned 40, location 15, open thread 20) + recency 10 x 0.5^(turns ago / 30) + open-thread bonus 10 + major-fact bonus 10.
  - **What is sent:** an item that shares no tag with the query is sent only while its recency score is at least 5 (about 30 turns); importance never rescues an unrelated old record. Pinned facts always go in, past the cap if needed.
  - **Summaries:** chapter summaries whose turns are inside the raw-turn window are skipped.
- **D-84 Locations split; "major" derived from config (approved; Jared asked for the config).**
  - **Events:** architecture §3's locationChanged{newLocation} becomes locationIntroduced {key?, name, kind, region, parentRef?, description, moveHere} (a creation event) plus locationChanged {locationRef}, the single writer of currentId (D-46).
  - **Major places:** "major" is not stored. A selector derives it from CHAPTER_PLACE_KINDS (planet, moon, station, ship) in src/data/entities.config.ts, which Jared can tune if travel fragments chapters.
  - **Scene:** scene.locationRef is left out of the reply, because locationChanged is the only writer. scene.presentNpcRefs is added.
  - **Player events:** npcCreatedByPlayer, locationCreatedByPlayer, npcAliasAdded and locationAliasAdded resolve review items.
- **D-85 Name mentions as a retrieval signal (approved).** The player's text is scanned for known entities: an exact name or alias (whole words), or a single name word of 4+ letters that belongs to one known person or place only ("Orin" for "Master Orin Vane"). It only raises retrieval scores and never writes state. Ref resolution stays exact (D-13).
- **D-86 Token ratio calibration (D-26 made concrete).** Characters sent (system + messages + response schema JSON) / promptTokenCount, folded in with weight 0.3 and clamped to 2..8 chars per token. Stored per model in localStorage (swrpg-v2:tokenRatios), outside saves. Live: estimate 5,700 vs actual 5,716 after one calibration step.
- **D-87 Debug data (approved).** Each transcript entry stores the context report (per section: estimated tokens, cap, included and dropped items with scores and reasons, plus the actual input tokens). Full request texts are kept in memory only, for the last 10 requests of the session.
- **D-88 Import never overwrites (approved).** Import validates the whole file (envelope through loadEnvelope, transcript campaign, branch and turns) before writing. If that campaign/branch already has transcript entries, it continues on a new branchId. Storage gains importCampaign (one transaction).
- **D-89 Summary retries (approved).** At each close, pending chapters are summarized oldest first, at most 2 calls. A failure stops the loop and leaves the chapter summaryPending, with no other automatic retry (D-16; the D-80 quota fallback still applies). The Memory tab has a manual retry. Each summary is its own commit with no transcript entry, and input waits while it runs so no turn races that commit. A late summary of an older chapter never overwrites an arc written after a newer one.
- **Implementation details logged with M2:**
  - **Ref resolution:** applyEvents resolves refs through each entity feature's resolveRef (own ID or unique exact alias), limited by an event's refKinds. An unknown scalar ref rejects the event; unknown array items are dropped with a note. Either way the name goes to the review queue. The player character is the ref "pc".
  - **Turn stamps:** events applied during a turn are stamped with meta.turn + 1 (the turn being played).
  - **Shared ID counter:** fact, review and chapter IDs share one counter (nextSeq).
  - **Saves from before M2:** load with empty entity slices (FeatureModule.emptyState). Pacing is version 2 (presentNpcIds).
  - **Chapter-close timing:** a major-location change or a 7+ day jump closes the chapter before the turn that caused it, so the new place starts the next chapter. A sceneEnded hint counts only in the turn that sends it.
- **D-90 No maxItems on the wire events array (live, 2026-11-17).** With M2's event union, maxItems 20 on the events array made Gemini refuse the schema with 400 "too many states for serving", on every model. A schema 5-6 KB in size, well under the size cap, was still too complex. Tested in the page: without that one limit the full and the largest M2 schemas are accepted (3.5-flash-lite, 3.6-flash). The parse schema keeps the 20-event limit, and a test checks the wire array has none.
- **D-91 Temp keys may be words (live).** The model wrote tmp:loc1 and tmp:thread1, which the old pattern rejected, so every place and thread of the opening was lost. TEMP_KEY_PATTERN is now "tmp:" plus 1-24 letters, digits, _ or -. Amends D-32.
- **D-92 Choice labels up to 200 characters (live).** Gemini strips maxLength (D-68), so a label over 100 characters failed the whole reply. The parse limit is 200 and the schema description asks for under 80.
- **D-93 Person lore only when that person is in play (live).** Character entries describe whole lives: Darth Malgus's entry in 3,661 BBY mentions the Sacking of Coruscant (3,653). Character and bench chunks no longer score on a shared place, only on canon ID, name or the player's words. The contract now says era overviews and character entries describe whole eras and lives, and nothing after the STATE date may be revealed or foreshadowed. *Known limit:* era overviews still include their era's ending (the Great Galactic War overview mentions the Sacking). Splitting them by date would need the lore files restructured; for now only the prompt rule guards it.
- **D-94 Review queue after the reply (live).** Intent unknownNames are queued only if they still match nothing after the narrative events are applied. Places the player names and the GM then introduces ("Dantooine") no longer pile up. Open items whose name later matches an entity exactly are resolved by the engine (reviewResolved now allows the engine source, exact match only). The intent prompt asks for proper names only, never concepts ("the blockade").
- **D-95 Three-letter canon names (live).** Lore scanning of the player's text accepts names of 3+ letters ("Zym"). Single-word memory matching stays at 4+ letters, so common words never match.
- **D-96 Live observations, not fixed in M2 (for Jared).**
  - **Free-tier limit:** gemini-3.6-flash returned a daily-quota 429 after about 6 requests, so the real limit is lower than the D-68 snapshot (20). The D-80 fallback worked and ended on Flash-Lite. Google also answered 503 "high demand" several times on 3.8-flash, 3.6-flash and Flash-Lite, and one 3.8-flash call timed out after 90 s.
  - **Fallback banner:** the header said "Flash daily limit reached; using Flash until reset" because both models are named "Flash" (friendlyModelName, M1). It should name the models.
  - **Lost opening:** a reload before the opening commits leaves an empty turn-0 campaign with no way to start the opening except New campaign (M1).
  - **Unlinked player NPCs:** an NPC the player creates from the review queue never gets a canon link (canon matching runs only on narrative turns).

## 2026-11-17 — M2 rough edges (Jared)

- **D-97 Fallback banner and interrupted openings.**
  - **Banner:** the fallback banner names the real model IDs ("gemini-3.6-flash daily limit reached; using gemini-3.5-flash-lite until reset") instead of "Flash" for both. friendlyModelName is removed.
  - **Interrupted opening:** the opening premise is stored on the campaign record. A campaign whose opening never committed (turn 0, no transcript) shows "Write the opening" (reruns it with the stored premise) or "Discard and create a new character" instead of an empty game. Fixes the D-96 items.
- **D-98 Era overviews split by date (Jared; amends D-93's known limit).**
  - **How sentences are dated:** the lore build splits each era overview into sentences (list lines whole). Each sentence gets the latest year it names, from three sources: an explicit BBY/ABY year; "Year N" of the war, through the timeline's own "#### 3,661 BBY — Year 20" headings; or a named timeline event of that era. A stripped event key such as "Bothawui" counts only when it names one year's events, so "Coruscant" never does. Era-end words ("ends", "eventually", "later", "finally") date a sentence to the era's last year. Overviews run in order, so a sentence is never dated before the one above it.
  - **What is sent:** retrieval sends only the sentences dated up to the current year. At 3,661 BBY the Great Galactic War overview stops at the Mandalorian Blockade of year 20; the Sacking (3,653) appears only from 3,653. Order 66 is hidden in the Clone Wars overview before 19 BBY.
  - **Side effect:** undated sentences after a dated one wait for that date, which errs toward hiding. Docs/ is unchanged.
  - **Not covered:** character entries are still whole-life texts (D-93: sent only when that person is in play, plus the prompt rule).
- **D-99 Dev server pinned to port 5173 (strictPort).** localStorage is per origin, port included, so the API key, settings and usage meter reset whenever the port changed.

## 2026-11-17 — UI layout (Jared)

- **D-100 Fixed app shell layout.** Replaces the scrolling-document UI of M1/M2. Plain CSS in src/ui/styles.css; no framework.
  - **Shell:** html and body are overflow hidden. #app is a CSS grid of exactly 100dvh, padded by the 10px gap plus the iPad safe-area insets (viewport-fit=cover). Every grid child has min-height 0, so nothing can grow the page.
  - **Regions:**
    - **Top bar:** fixed 52px, one line, no wrapping; items truncate, and the place and turn hide below 600px. It holds the time and time-of-day label, date, place, pacing chip, turn, story-model picker, a usage meter (today's requests for the story model in use; a "!" badge with the details in its tooltip when there is a fallback or a usage warning), the side-panel toggle and the menu button.
    - **Main column:** the story feed (fills the height and scrolls; roll cards inline; follows new turns only while scrolled to the bottom; the scroll position is kept across re-renders), a choices box (at most 30% of the column, scrolls, hidden when empty; also holds the interrupted-opening prompt), and a fixed 116px input box (a status line with the pending action, the error with Retry / Dismiss, or summary progress; then the textarea and Send).
    - **Side panel:** fixed 360px, tabs Character / People / Places / Threads / Memory / Debug in a 3 x 2 grid so all six stay visible. Each tab scrolls inside the box and keeps its position. A close button and the top-bar toggle collapse it, and the feed then takes the full width.
  - **Below 900px wide** (iPad portrait, phone), the side panel is a fixed slide-over drawer with a backdrop, opened from the top bar. It starts closed there and open on wide screens.
  - **Modal:** Settings and Campaign (current campaign, export / import, new character) open as one modal from the menu button. Its body scrolls inside the box. It is required (cannot be closed) while there is no API key (Settings) or no campaign (Campaign); otherwise Escape, the close button or the backdrop closes it.
  - **Review and Character tabs:** Review is folded into the People tab as a "Needs review (n)" section at the top (Jared's tab list has no Review tab). The Character tab shows who and where (name, age, birth date, now, place chain, who is present, pacing, open threads, campaign, turn, chapter) until the M3 sheet.
  - **Style tokens:** one set of CSS variables: colors (dark datapad: bg, box, box-2/3, line, text, muted, cyan accent, good, bad, warn), gap 10px (8px on phones), padding 12px, radius 10px for boxes and 7px for inner cards, buttons and inputs, a 1px border, font sizes 12/13/15/17px, and 44px touch targets.
  - **Boxes:** every region is a .box: a 1px border on all sides, rounded corners, overflow hidden so scrolled content is clipped inside the rounded outline, an optional .box-head strip, and a .box-body that scrolls.
  - **Measured** in exact-size frames at 1180x820, 820x1180 and 390x844: no page scrollbar, no overlapping regions, everything inside the viewport, a one-line 52px top bar. The feed, side-panel tabs and modal body each scroll on their own.
- **D-101 IDs without a secure context (Jared; found on the iPad over the LAN).** crypto.randomUUID exists only in secure contexts (https, localhost), so on http://192.168.x.x:5173 "Begin" threw.
  - **Fix:** one helper, src/ui/ids.ts newId(), uses randomUUID when present and otherwise builds an RFC 4122 v4 UUID from crypto.getRandomValues, which works on any page. It was the only randomUUID call (src/main.ts). These are campaign and branch IDs, not game randomness (the seeded RNG is unchanged).
  - **Audit:** nothing else the app uses needs a secure context. That covers localStorage, IndexedDB, matchMedia, fetch and AbortController, Blob URLs with the download attribute, File.text() and structuredClone. No clipboard, service worker, crypto.subtle or other secure-only API is used.
  - **Dev server:** `server.host: true` was added (dev only) so the LAN address works.

## 2026-11-17 — M3 (progression and character creation)

D-102 to D-105 are the plan choices Jared approved for M3. Entries marked "chosen while Jared was away" were made during the build without him, under his standing instruction.

- **D-102 D-67 decided: natural baseline, no conditioning hour, Knight Force Sense minimum 26 (Jared approved the plan).**
  - **Natural baseline** (progression-numbers §11a). Strength, Agility and Endurance start at 0.5 x the body cap for the Human-equivalent age and species. Willpower follows an age curve: 4: 10, 10: 18, 16: 25, 20: 30, 30: 35, 60: 40. Training adds on top in XP terms: level = levelFromXp(xpForLevel(N) + trained XP). Every hour counts, nothing steps, and levels stay derived.
  - **No conditioning hour.** Computed with the baseline in place, 1 h of conditioning in the Initiate and Padawan requirements raised the max day-end f from 1.02 to 1.19 (1.24 for Padawans only). Fixed commitments are never trimmed, so Initiates would be overtrained every day, and Force Sense did not rise. The baseline alone cuts the trimmed fill from 2.29 h to 0.30 h per training day, and Knight+ secondaries grow again.
  - **Results** (docs-v2/calc, all scripts re-run): K = 15.18 (15.181). Average milestones 5.5 / 16.0 / 32.6 / 55.2 / 83.5 years, all within +/-20%. At 20.3: main form 41.5, Force Control 29.7, Force Sense 26.8, Willpower 49.3, Endurance 37.6. The D-48 Y_avg table was recomputed. Peacetime baselines: Obi-Wan 41 at 13.3 y, Anakin 7.7 y, Luke 20.3 y (tuned with combat XP in M4).
  - **Knight Force Sense minimum 30 -> 26.** The Average reaches 30 only at 26.9. Rule, the same as D-51 and D-66: a minimum stays while the Average meets it within +/-2 years of its stated age (ranks test 1); otherwise it becomes the Average's level at that age, rounded down. Only Force Sense changes. Main form 38 (18.3), Force Control 30 (20.6), Willpower 48 (19.6), the Initiate Trials 24 / 14 (10.9 / 11.1) and Master 58 (33.8) all stay.
  - **Padawan selection recomputed by D-66's rule.** The window now opens at 11.1 (23 monthly rolls), so the divisor is 610 (8.20% per month at S 50; 14.0% never chosen; seeded 14.05%). The Average sits at the 15.7th percentile.
- **D-103 Species table in M3 (Jared: species.md is in the M3 brief).** spec/species.md is built in M3: 12 launch species, Human-equivalent age mapping, body cap x bodyScale + capBonus, learning factors inside the 2.8 cap and the clamp, and Resist Aging as a pure function (the ability arrives in M6). Lifespans and traits stay LORE CHECK design defaults until M8. This amends the D-56 stub "Human body cap until M8".
- **D-104 D-48 clamp share (Jared approved the plan).** The character's share in the 4.0x clamp is max(the focus-weight share, the skill's share of the character's training hours in the same 365-day window). As written, a skill outside the focus weights had share 0 and a cap of 0, so the player could never train it, which breaks "the player's routine decides".
- **D-105 Closed form with measured phase paces (Jared approved the plan; Golden Rule 7).** With D-67 decided, the D-43 closed form missed the day loop by +2.3 at 5 years (character-creation test 2) and -3.5 at 60 years (NPC test 2).
  - **The form:** every skill, the main one included, has a pace per rank phase (Initiate / Padawan / Knight+ for Jedi and Sith, one phase for careers), measured by running each archetype's default routine through the day model (docs-v2/calc/archetype-paces.mjs). It is normalized so A_ref stays 17.98.
  - **Jedi Guardian main form:** 0.844 / 1.177 / 1.099. Against the day loop: 5 y +0.3, 10 y +0.1, 30 y +0.1, 60 y -0.3.
  - **Recomputed reference table:** Padawan 16: 34.2 | Knight 30: 53.9 | Master 45: 69.1 | Elder 70: 89.0 | Obi-Wan check 80.5 | Anakin check 79.7 | trooper sergeant 45.3 | Sith warrior 79.4. Creation tests: Knight at 24 = 46.6, Initiate at 9 = 20.1.
  - **Chosen while Jared was away:**
    - Sith use the Jedi rank ages (apprentice at 12, Lord at 20.5) with the Jedi Order rows (D-42 stub) until M7.
    - The old main weight of every archetype is 1.0.
    - Focus sliders change a skill's Knight+ and career pace by Delta share x 2 x the archetype's main Knight+ pace (an approximation; the Order-fixed phases are unchanged).
- **D-106 Social practice activity (chosen while Jared was away).** No activity trained Charisma, which the smuggler and civilian archetypes need. Added "Social practice" (study load; Charisma primary; Intelligence 0.3, Willpower 0.2) to skills-and-activities.md and the calc. Force Output, healing and dark abilities are drilled with the bank's "Ability drill (generic)" pattern (Force Lightning control for dark abilities).
- **D-107 M3 plan items as built (Jared approved the plan).**
  - **Talent domains:** combat (technique skills), Force (force), physical, mind (wisdom, knowledge, academic). Cross-talent: a Force prodigy or better gets x1.1 on combat skills.
  - **New slices:** a minimal `health` (physical, mental and Force meters plus the last sleep carry; Vitality and wounds in M5) and a minimal `ranks` (the rank chosen at creation, fixed until M7).
  - **Time spans carry an occupation:** TimeAdvance and TimeStep have `occupation: rest | activity | sleep` (default rest). Health recovers meters only on rest steps (D-52). A sleep span ends with the engine event `sleepCompleted` (7 h+ resets, 5-7 h carries 30%, under 5 h 60%).
  - **Intent and input:** `intent.activity {activityId, intensity?, partnerRef?, teacherRef?}`. A train or study choice expands to its skill's default activity for 1 h. A new `action` TurnInput carries a full intent from the UI (the sheet's Train button), is validated like any intent and skips the intent call.
  - **Engine sources:** progression's `activityResolved` and health's `activityLoad` are engine-only. factLearned also accepts the engine source, for tier-beat facts.
  - **Saves:** character v2 (species, path, archetype, focus, forms, training start age, talents, hand). M1/M2 saves migrate to a Human outsider with no training (`trainingStartAge` null, natural baselines only); progression, health and ranks start empty.
  - **Stubs replaced:** rust, e(f_phys) and skill levels in checks now come from progression and health. New named stubs in src/data/stubs.ts: teacher levels (the Average Jedi Guardian's closed form at the NPC's age, M5), alignment multiplier 1.0 and dark pressure 0 (M6).
- **D-108 Choices made during the M3 build (chosen while Jared was away).**
  - **Ids:** skill ids are camelCase (shiiCho, forceControl). Profession:<kind> is one skill (`profession`) in M3; the kinds are story flavor.
  - **Activities:** forms with no bank entry use the basics pattern of their nearest form (Tràkata and Dun Möch like Makashi, Sokan and Zero-G like Ataru, Saberstaff like Shii-Cho, Jar'Kai like Dual saber). The Force Output drill (generic pattern, D-106) is always open, because Force Output is a stat, not a learned ability. "Known" means level 1 or more.
  - **Species traits:** the Twi'lek "+5 Charisma cap" and the Zabrak "+5 Willpower cap" have no mechanical effect, since only physical stats have caps in v2. They stay as story notes on the sheet. Every species row stays LORE CHECK until M8.
  - **Creation ranks and ages:** Initiate under 13, Padawan from 10, Knight from 18, Master from 31. The Sith ladder mirrors them. In the closed form, a Padawan's phase starts at min(12, age), a Knight's at min(20.5, age), and Padawan starts at least 6 years before Knight. All life-stage gates and phases read the Human-equivalent age; the start age is entered in species years. Default forms: main Shii-Cho, second Soresu.
  - **State of mind:** fear, anger, hatred and pride last 3 days unless replaced. Centered lasts through the next day. Calm ends fear, anger, hatred and pride (it is the event of calming down) but not despair. Grief and despair ease linearly back to x1.00 over 14 / 21 / 30 days for intensity 1 / 2 / 3. The emotionalEvent field is `emotion`, because `type` is the event discriminator.
  - **Day rules:** the overtraining streak resets on a day with no training or a day ending below 0.6. A rest of 30+ minutes ends the post-workout window. Rust removal: primaries get the full effective hours, secondaries hours x their share / 0.75, and every skill the session credits counts as practiced.
  - **Training sessions:** intense and extreme intensity pick drills in the push band (+10..+20). A teacher or partner whose level is unknown counts as an even partner. Bond is a relationship of 20+ (friendly). A session is at most 12 h; Battle Meditation is at most 1 h. A refused activity changes nothing: no pacing switch and no draft time, so the AI's timeElapsed applies.
  - **Clamp age:** Y_avg is read at the Human-equivalent age at the start of the 365-day window.
  - **Lifetime runner:** like the calc, every block runs at standard intensity. In play, the default intensity of Force history and passive awareness is casual.
- **D-109 Clamp hours by XP share (chosen while Jared was away; refines D-104).** As first built, the D-104 share counted training hours only for a session's primary skill. Every secondary skill and synergy target outside the focus weights then had share 0 and cap 0, and the clamp discarded all their XP (seen in a pipeline test). Now each session's hours go to every skill by its share of that session's XP: the split share for primaries and secondaries, and XP / session total for spillover and fatigue Willpower. A skill's clamp share then follows its real share of training, and the Average profile is unchanged (it never nears the cap).
- **D-110 Progression test 12: the whole day's fatigue Willpower is halved (Jared decided, 2026-11-17).** *Decision:* from the 3rd consecutive overtrained day, the whole day's fatigue Willpower XP is halved (the day credits half of min(3.0, raw)). progression-numbers §10 and test 12 are updated; test 12 now passes as written (End 30, 6 h pushes: 7 in a row earn less than 7 alternated with rest days). *Original finding:* The test says a 7-day pushing streak earns less fatigue Willpower than alternating push and recovery days. Computed through the src stepper over End 20-90 and 4-8 h pushes, the same number of push days gives the streak equal or more fatigue Willpower, never less. The streak's capacity cut (-10% per day) raises f, and the 3.0/day cap saturates, so halving Willpower from f > 1.0 on day 3+ rarely bites. The rules are built exactly as written. The test checks the first half (the 3.0 cap, and direct Willpower outside it) and the halving rule itself. To make the claim true, one option is halving all of the day's fatigue Willpower from day 3; that is Jared's choice.
- **D-111 Knight main-form minimum raised to 39 (Jared decided, 2026-11-17).** *Decision:* main form 38 -> 39 for Knight, Master and Sith Lord (ranks.md). Ranks test 1 re-checked through the src engine: the Average meets 39 at 18.9 (1.4 years before 20.3), and the lifetime test checks every Knight minimum at +/-2 again. (With the test's seed, 38 was met at 18.30; another seed gave 18.24.) *Original finding:* Through the src engine, the Average reaches main form 38 at 18.24. That is 2.06 years before the stated 20.3 (D-51 / D-102); ranks test 1 allows +/-2. The calc had 18.3 (exactly 2.0) because it leaves out synergy spillover. The physical stats lift each other through synergy, so capacity and efficiency rise slightly. The minimum is never late. The lifetime test checks the other Knight minimums at +/-2 and this one at < 2.1 early. Options: accept it, or raise the minimum to 39.
- **D-112 Feature internals the turn layer uses go through selectors (chosen while Jared was away).** resolveTraining (a pure read of state plus a request that returns events) and computeCreation (a pure function of its inputs) are re-exported from progression/selectors.ts and character/selectors.ts, so the lint boundary "other layers import feature selectors only" holds.
- **D-113 Twi'lek and Zabrak traits become learning factors (Jared, 2026-11-17; replaces part of D-108).** Only physical stats have caps in v2, so the Twi'lek "+5 Charisma cap" is now Charisma learning x1.05 and the Zabrak "+5 Willpower cap" is now Willpower learning x1.05. Both are inside the 0.9-1.1 trait limit, count inside the 2.8 cap and the 4.0x clamp (species.md §3), and stay LORE CHECK until M8. species.md §2, src/data/species.ts and the species tests are updated.
- **D-114 M3 choices approved (Jared, 2026-11-17).** Every "chosen while Jared was away" choice in D-108 (except the species caps, replaced by D-113), D-109 (clamp hours by XP share) and D-112 (selectors as the public surface for training and creation) is approved as built.

## 2026-11-17 — M3 play-test fixes (Jared)

- **D-115 Focus sliders reach the closed form below Knight (Jared's play-test bug; amends D-105).** *Root cause:* the D-105 slider formula changed only the Knight+ and career phases, and the creation form defaults to a 14-year-old Padawan, so the sliders changed nothing at all (Force Sense 18.18 -> 18.18). The weights did reach computeCreation. A second cause made it look worse: the preview listened to the slider's `change` event, which fires only on release. *Fix:* every phase moves by Delta share x 2 x the main Knight+ pace x fill, where fill is the phase's mean fill hours (standard budget minus Order hours) over the 6 h Knight+ day. That is the only part of a young character's day the player directs: 1 for Knight+, about 0.14 for Padawans (12-20.5), 0 for Initiates under 10. The preview now updates on `input` while the slider moves. *Effect at 50% Force Sense focus:* Padawan 14: Force Sense 18.2 -> 18.7; Knight 24: 28.6 -> 35.7. The default focus changes nothing (creation test 1 still 46.6). The small Padawan effect is by design: the Order requirements fill most of the day.
- **D-116 Talent point bypass (Jared).** The creation screen has an "Ignore talent point limit" toggle. Turning it on shows the warning "This creates a very powerful character; gameplay will not be balanced", which must be confirmed. With it on, any number of talents can be chosen; each domain still maxes at exceptional, and the 2.8 M cap and the 4.0x pace limit still apply. A campaign that used more than 3 points has character.unbalanced = true (character slice v3; earlier saves migrate with false), shown on the character sheet.
- **D-117 Real numbers on the sheet and the training card (Jared).** Each skill shows its level, total XP / the total XP the next level needs (natural baseline included, so the numbers sit on the curve XP(L) = K * L^1.75), and rust. The meters show load / capacity. The training card shows the XP gained per skill to one decimal and each skill's new total. A pipeline test confirms that a 2 h Shii-Cho basics session (level 30, solo, standard) adds +1.67 XP to the saved Shii-Cho XP, the same as the card (total 5,839.7 of 6,182.8 for level 31).

## 2026-11-17 — Form plans and the fundamentals transfer (Jared)

- **D-118 Form plans (Jared; amends D-40, D-42, D-105, D-108).** *Why:* a Padawan had no real second form. The calibrated Padawan routine gave it 4.5% of about 0.85 h/day of fill (pace 0.002), so a 16-year-old Padawan's second form was 0.5. Creation also credited the Initiate's Shii-Cho block to whatever main form was picked. *Rule:* Initiates train Shii-Cho only (the Order forms block and their fill), and Shii-Cho is not a main-form choice. From Padawan, a form plan splits the 2 h master session and the 1 h solo hour among any number of forms. The largest share trains with the master first; the forms part of the fill follows the plan's proportions. The default is main 2 h + second 1 h. An optional Knight+ plan defaults to the Padawan plan. Shii-Cho may stay in or be dropped; forms no longer trained keep their XP and rust (progression-numbers §13). The calibration targets apply to the highest form; every "main form" rank minimum reads the highest form ('$highestForm'). The Average follows Shii-Cho -> Soresu 2 h + Ataru 1 h from Padawan. In the calc, the main and second form drills mirror Soresu and Ataru basics. *Built:* src/engine/closedForm.ts formXps() and planSplit() (a port of docs-v2/calc/npc-closed-form.mjs formLevels()); archetype formPaces per phase and source (Initiate order / fill; Padawan master / solo / fill; Knight+ fill); R defined as K * 41^1.75 / A_ref (it was measured); creation form plans with plan sliders that renormalize; character v4 stores the plan (older saves get main 2 h / second 1 h); the focus sliders pool the forms into one "Lightsaber forms" share; the Order Padawan row reads '$planMaster' / '$planSolo'; the teacher stub uses the form closed form with the taught form as main. *Measured without a transfer:* K 6.8, and Shii-Cho reached 41 at age 12, so the milestones missed by up to 52%.
