terrain takes tags or a place word, see below); /spar <level | at <gap> | canon:<name>> [mode] (a sparring session, played out from its first exchange: fightBegun playOut); /wound <location> <severity> (healthSynced with one wound at the middle of its W band) and /heal [all] (devHealed; all clears lasting injuries too); /set <skill> <level> (devSkillSet: trained XP above the natural baseline, rust cleared), /e <form> <value>, /ci <ability> <value> (devESet, devCISet, 0-120); /fatigue <physical|mental|force> <value> (devFatigueSet, a fraction of capacity); /skip <amount> <unit> (the same montage or time skip a montage turn runs, through resolveTurn; over 120 days a time skip); /time <time of day | year BBY|ABY [month] [day] [time]> (the clock forward through advanceTime, every hook running; never backward: the clock has one writer); /place <location> (moves there, created by name when new); /help. Wounds, heals and skips are refused during a fight (the duel owns the wounds); /set during a fight applies from the next fight. Unknown commands show the help. *Events:* normal events are reused where they exist (fightBegun gains optional minionGroups and playOut; npcIntroduced and locationChanged accept the new dev source); new dev-only events exist only where no event sets the value (devSkillSet, devESet, devCISet, devFatigueSet, devHealed, devNpcLevelsSet: a test opponent's generated profile scaled so its best form sits at the level, stored on the NPC as devLevels, an optional field, so older saves load unchanged). They take the new dev event source and refuse outside a test campaign themselves; no other source can send them. *Record:* each command is a turn record (input kind dev, no calls) with a short "[DEV]" line, shown in the feed in a code style, committed with the state like any turn, so it is in the transcript and the save; dev turns are left out of the story history sent to the AI and keep the last story choices. In dev mode a slash line never goes to the AI; outside dev mode it is ordinary typed text. A normal campaign refuses every command, applies nothing and saves nothing. *Design default (Jared to confirm):* place words for /fight terrain map to tags (forest and jungle: uneven ground and cover; swamp: slippery, uneven; cave: confined, uneven; ruins: debris, cover; desert: open, uneven; plains: open; rooftop: ledge, high ground; hangar: flat floor, crates, machinery; corridor: confined, flat floor; temple: flat floor; ice: slippery, open), since the terrain tags are mechanical and "forest" is not one. Tests: tests/turn/devCommands.test.ts (each command's events, the refusal in a normal campaign at both layers, the transcript and save, the scenario in the opening and after /scenario). Architecture §15 (Dev tools) amended.
- **D-181 extended: the narrator never ends a fight on its own say-so (Jared, 2026-11-18).** In every fight, not just spars, the narration's combatEnded is accepted only as interrupted, and only with why (the outside event: reinforcements, a collapse, an alarm); fled, surrendered and truce from the AI are refused with a reason that names where they come from. Fled and surrendered come only from the engine: the player's Disengage and Surrender beats, or an NPC's engine-rolled fate. A truce: the narration sends truceOffered {why} (not in a sparring session); the fight stays active with the offer stored on it (active.truceOffer, default null, so older saves load), the duel panel shows the offer and Accept the truce (combatAction acceptTruce, player), and any other action declines it. The ending reason keeps the why ("interrupted: Republic reinforcements pour into the hangar"). combat-narration.md, the COMBAT state line (an open offer), ai-events.md, architecture §3 and rolls.md §3 amended. Tests: tests/features/combat.test.ts; fixture tests in tests/turn/fightView.test.ts (an AI truce and an AI flee in a duel's narration are rejected with the reason and the duel goes on; an offered truce waits for the player's button).
- **D-182 extended: target selection is a setting (Jared, 2026-11-18).** targetSet (player, outside the turn, like stanceSet): no exchange, no dice, no AI call, no time, no turn record. The target stays until changed; when that opponent is out (or turns to fight an ally) the duel strikes at the next engaged one, and the panel's select shows the target actually faced. combatAction target is removed. Test: tests/features/combat.test.ts.
- **D-180 follow-up: throwables in outdoor and wrecked places (Jared, 2026-11-18).** A new terrain tag fallenTimber (fallen branches and logs: medium objects, a supply of 2-4 per fight like debris). Place words now carry throwables where the place has them: forest and jungle uneven ground, cover, fallen timber and loose rock (small, 3-6); swamp slippery, uneven, fallen timber; cave confined, uneven, loose rock; ruins debris, cover, loose rock; desert open, uneven, loose rock; rooftop ledge, high ground, machinery (vents and units); hangar unchanged (crates, machinery). None for plains (open grass), corridor and temple (indoor, kept clear), and ice (no tag fits ice chunks; a future tag if wanted). Tests: tests/turn/devCommands.test.ts (forest supply, a Force-thrown rock in a forest fight, every outdoor or wrecked place word has throwables).
- **D-184 Ice terrain; the per-bout terrain hazard roll built (Jared, 2026-11-18).** New tag iceChunks (chunks of ice: medium throwables, a supply of 2-5 per fight; *design default:* medium, like logs, since a chunk worth throwing is bigger than a rock). Slippery ground keeps footing -1 and its fall hazard and now gives -1 to take-position attempts (positionAttempt on the tag; the duel adds it to the margin checked against the position maneuver's minimum, so a position that needed exactly 2 fails). Place words ice, glacier, Ilum caves (two words, now parsed) and Hoth map to iceChunks + slippery + uneven ground. *Found while building:* the hazard roll per bout that D-144 approved ("environment accidents in every mode: a hazard roll per bout from the terrain tags") was in the terrain data but never built: no code read tag.hazard. Built as D-144 says (src/engine/terrainHazard.ts): at the start of each bout (the first at fightBegun; each later sparring bout, played out or resolved like a montage; a real fight is one bout) every fighter still in it rolls each hazardous tag; a hit is hazardDamage by the health rules (falls by the fall location weights), synced through healthSynced / npcHealthSynced like duel damage, and listed to the narrator as "Terrain accident" lines with the exchanges. The fight context stores the hazards and the position modifier (defaults, so older saves load). The lifetime runs use no terrain, so no progression number moves (npm run test:all 608/608). Not built: D-144's flat-floor accident (about 1 in 200 bouts, a minor injury), which has no tag data; say if you want it. Tests: tests/engine/terrainHazard.test.ts. rolls.md §5 amended.
- **D-185 Flat-floor accidents; hazard rolls in long fights (Jared, 2026-11-18).** *Flat floor:* the flatFloor tag gets hazard data: chance 0.005 per roll (about 1 in 200 bouts), a severity-1 fall kept in the minor band (W 1-9, even when the fighter is Spent: minorOnly), narrated as "turns an ankle, pulls a muscle or takes a bruise"; the D-144 flat-floor accident is now built. It applies to sparring bouts and real fights alike, whenever flatFloor is among the fight's tags (a fight with no tags rolls nothing; the hangar place word includes flat floor). *Long fights:* a real (non-sparring) fight rolls its terrain at its start and then once per TERRAIN_HAZARD_EVERY = 10 exchanges (combat.config), with the per-roll chances unchanged: in the duel loop (runUntilBeat), at the first exchange of each new block of 10, whatever the play mode (beats, Fight on, Resolve to end), so a 60-exchange fight rolls 6 times (at 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50); a block entered during a typed sequence or a Force action rolls at the next exchange the loop runs; a roll that takes a fighter out ends the duel there. A sparring bout still rolls once (hazardEvery null). The fight context stores the interval (null for older saves, which keep one roll a fight). *Progression confirmed unchanged:* the lifetime runs build their duel context with no terrain (no hazards, no interval), so they draw no hazard dice; a test checks a no-terrain context gives the same duel as before, and npm run test:all passes 613/613. Tests: tests/engine/terrainHazard.test.ts (the flat-floor rate and minor band, both fight kinds, the 60-exchange ice fight rolling 6 times, a bout rolling once, the no-terrain identity). rolls.md §5 amended.
- **D-186 Indoor place words have the flat floor (Jared, 2026-11-18; follow-up to D-185).** The dev place words training hall, salle, dojo, sparring ring, Temple, academy, arena and office map to flatFloor; corridor keeps confined + flatFloor (two-word names parse as one). *Not built, open for Jared:* "sparring sessions started by Order classes and routine blocks get flatFloor by default". Those are not duels: an Order class or a routine sparring block resolves as a training activity (activityResolved: hours, XP, E from the partner), with no bouts, so there is no terrain or bout roll to give them. Giving them flat-floor accidents would need a new rule on training activities (an accident chance per sparring session or per hour), and the lifetime runs use those blocks heavily, so their numbers would move. The duel spars that can lack terrain are the narrator's combatStarted with no tags, Play the next bout, and /spar with no terrain; defaulting those to flatFloor is a one-line change if wanted. *Lifetime confirmed:* their duels build a context with no terrain and their Order and routine sparring is training activity, so nothing they run rolls a hazard; npm run test:lifetime 36/36. Test: tests/engine/terrainHazard.test.ts.
- **D-187 Makashi precision and the marks of contact, recorded for M6 (Jared, 2026-11-18; not built).** Recorded under architecture §15 with the form traits (cleave, free form switch): Makashi precision (the aimed-strike penalty from -2 at Makashi 0 to 0 at 61+, linear design default, only while fighting in Makashi) and the marks of contact as learnable techniques lowering the aimed-strike penalty for their own location (cho mai hand, cho mok limb, cho sun arm, mou kei multiple limbs with a two-location aimed strike at a higher difficulty; sai cha head and shiak torso stab if the lore supports them). Both feed D-171 only. Lore check done now: Docs/SWRPG lore/ has no entry for the marks of contact or any of the six names, so their Legends definitions need a lore entry before building.
- **D-188 Lightsaber combat lore added; more M6 records (Jared, 2026-11-18; not built).** New lore file Docs/SWRPG lore/SW_Lightsaber_Combat_Marks_Zones_Techniques.md (Legends: marks of contact, target zones, stances, maneuvers; its "Game notes" are design suggestions, not lore). The D-187 lore gap is filled: the file defines cho mai, cho sun, cho mok, mou kei, sai cha and shiak. Checked against D-187: cho mai and cho sun are the weapon hand and weapon arm; shiak is a stab (tied to no zone; D-187 keeps it as the torso technique); mou kei cuts through several limbs (D-187's two-location aimed strike is the game version); the file also lists sai tok (cutting in two) and sun djem (the existing disarm). No Disney-era conflict found. Recorded for M6 under architecture §15, not built: Zone 4 (back) strikes after a won take-position or against a fleeing or surprised opponent, with a lethal location rule; riposte as a Makashi and Djem So trait; Tràkata as a high-risk tactic; shiim as the narration term for minor saber wounds.
- **D-189 Duel panel and health card layout (Jared, iPad check, 2026-11-18; UI fixes).** *Health card:* the Vitality row reused the meter class, which is the 8 px bar itself (overflow hidden), so the row was 8 px tall and its label and number spilled over the next line. The card is now a column of rows, each with its own space: Vitality as a label, a bar and current / max, the wound penalty, then a Wounds list (or "No wounds."). *Duel panel:* a pinned part under the head never scrolls: the fighter rows (each bar with current / max; a long list scrolls in its own small box), the advantage track with the facing opponent's name at the left end, the player's at the right and the value ("Advantage: +2 Kael", "Even"), the bout result, the current beat with a Quiet (no AI call) or Narrated (AI call) tag, and the main action row (Continue or Spar it out, Fight on, Narrate, Resolve to end) with the sparring controls. Below it, in its own scroll box: stance, tactic, next strike, aim, Force, form and target (side by side), then the narrator's suggestions last, then the duel log. Each duel-log entry is tagged Narrated once an AI narration covered it (exchange number at or below the fight's lastNarrated) and Quiet before; each feed combat card is tagged by its turn (quiet or narrated). A dev command's combat card is Quiet (no AI call). In dev mode the panel head shows "AI calls this fight: N" (fightCalls: the calls of the turns whose combat draft is this fight). In a fight the duel box may take 60% of the main column (was 46%). *Checked* in headless Chrome on the real App shell (memory storage, a test Knight, dev /fight): iPad 820 x 1180 and phone 390 x 844 (the phone in a 390 px iframe, since headless Chrome will not size a window under 500 px): the page height equals the viewport (no page scrolling), the main actions are inside the duel box, the health card's rows do not overlap; a 6-minion fight keeps the actions pinned. Tests: tests/turn/fightView.test.ts (the call count), tests/turn/devCommands.test.ts (dev cards are quiet).
- **D-190 Compact duel panel and a story / panel split (Jared, iPad check, 2026-11-18; UI).** *Split:* while a fight is active the story box and the duel panel share the main column 50/50 by default, with a drag handle between them (pointer drag, or arrow keys when focused); the share stays between 25% and 75% and is remembered per device in localStorage (swrpg-v2:duelSplit, read and written in try/catch; blocked storage keeps it for the page only). *Always visible:* the header (with the bout tally in a spar), the player's bar and the faced opponent's bar (each current / max), the advantage track with its value, the beat line (Quiet or Narrated, the bout result, "+N more (M fighting)" when there are other opponents), the primary action row, and the stance as one segmented setting directly under it. *More actions* (collapsed by default; open or closed is kept for the fight it was set in): the other opponents' bars, target, tactics, next strike, aim, Force, form, Stop sparring while a bout is played out, the narrator's suggestions, the duel log. Smaller padding and 30 px buttons; with More actions collapsed the panel fits its half with no scrolling (measured below). *Continue vs Fight on:* they differ, so both stay, renamed: **Next beat** (Continue: fights to the next beat, any kind, usually a quiet one with no AI call) and **To key moment** (Fight on: fights on through quiet beats to the next narrated one: a wounding hit, a landed maneuver, someone spent or down, the end; an AI call). While a sparring session is not being played out, the primary row is Spar it out (the rest at once), Play this bout and Stop sparring (every other passive action resolved the rest of the session the same way). *Button styles:* actions are filled (the primary row in the accent; Narrate and Resolve to end filled neutral; tactics, Force), settings are outline toggles with a check when selected (stance, target, next strike, aim), Surrender is an action in a warning outline. Note: next strike and aim keep their D-171 / D-182 behavior (choosing one also fights to the next beat); only their look is a setting's. *Checked* in headless Chrome on the real App shell: iPad portrait 820 x 1180 (story 474 px, panel 474 px; a 6-minion fight) and landscape 1180 x 820 with the side panel open (294 / 294): no page scrolling, the panel body does not overflow with More actions collapsed, the primary row inside the panel; with More actions open the extra rows scroll inside the panel. rolls.md's "Fight on" is the To key moment button.

## 2026-11-18 — iPad combat test fixes, Force resistance, saber throw (Jared)
- **D-191 Every skill of the rank at creation; no silent step drops; attack verbs are strikes (Jared, iPad combat test, 2026-11-18; bug fixes).** *Bug 1 (telekinesis at level 4.0, +0):* the dev test start (D-178) and normal creation gave Jedi and Sith no Force abilities at all. The measured archetype paces have no ability rows, because the routine's levitation drill trains Force Control while telekinesis is unknown. The 4.0 in the log was the d20 alone (level bonus 0.0); the CI formula did not collapse (with level 0, G = 0 and the effective level is the level). The sequence path never checked that the ability was known. *Fix:* archetypes.ts ARCHETYPE_FORCE_ABILITIES: each Order-trained ability's phase pace is a share of the parent stat's phase pace (Force Control, focus included), on top of it (no hours moved), so the closed form grows it with Force Control. Jedi core: Telekinesis 0.8, Force Push 0.6, Force Jump 0.45, Mind Trick 0.35; Guardian + Force Speed 0.5; Consular Mind Trick 0.55 + Force Barrier 0.45; Sentinel Mind Trick 0.55 + Force Speed 0.35. Sith core: Telekinesis 0.8, Push 0.6, Choke 0.5, Jump 0.45; Warrior + Force Speed 0.5; Inquisitor + Mind Trick 0.45 (design defaults). The Average Guardian Knight at 50: Telekinesis 45.9, Push 38.9, Speed 35.1, Jump 33.0, Mind Trick 28.6 (Force Control 52.1); at 28: 35.1 / 29.8 / 26.9 / 25.3 / 21.9. The dev start's CI 20 now lands on them. NPC Jedi and Sith get the same (NPC generation reuses creation). Force Speed raises the Force body-ability boost and Vitality a little; test:all passes unchanged. *The droid's "+5 natural":* a natural 20 (+5, rolls §1); its +3.7 was its Willpower / 10, the resistance roll D-192 removes for droids. *Bug 4 (the strike step never rolled):* the intent call folded "driving heavy strikes" into the aggressive stance ("push forward" is a stance word, D-179) and returned [stance, push]; the engine resolved the stance silently and went straight to the Force step. *Fix:* (1) intent.md: every attack verb is its own strike step, never folded into a stance. (2) combatWords.ts STRIKE_WORDS and FORCE_CLAUSE_WORDS: the typed text is read clause by clause; each attack clause with no Force action is a strike, inserted in the player's order when the intent left it out (combatTurn reconcileSteps; a noun such as "his strike" and a clause with a defend or evade word do not count); a Force action the text names is added the same way. (3) Every step the intent returned gets a log line: resolved, failed, or dropped with the reason (does not know the ability, nothing left to throw, an earlier step failed, past the cap, an incomplete intent step); a step that cannot be attempted is dropped and the chain goes on. *Attack words:* against minions a sequence strike step is a strike roll (attack vs defense, a hit at margin 4+; a miss by more than a tie gives the minion the build-up step for that margin), never a build-up contest. A position maneuver is only for explicit movement (push back, drive back, take ground: a new combatWords group). Against a named duelist a strike step is still a build-up exchange. *Narration:* the RESOLVED block gives each Force use the engine's reason (the roll fell short, a natural 1, a misfire, backlash, dodged, cover, the target's Force, a will that held, a saber caught it, immune, Force-null) with "Explain the failure only with this reason"; combat-narration.md forbids invented explanations, Force-blocking fields, dampeners and other mechanics not in RESOLVED. *Measured* (dev Knight at 50 vs 6 B1, forest, 600 fights each): balanced 100% won, 0.5% moderately wounded; mixed (strike-strike-Push sequences, saber throws, Pushes, beats) 100% / 0.5% (D-158: 99%+).
- **D-192 What resists the Force (Jared, 2026-11-18; Legends: almost nothing).** rolls §4 and force-catalog amended. src/data/forceResistance.config.ts; one contest in combatForce.ts (forceContest) for combat and out of it; the Force load stays in forceMeter.ts. *Groups* (every skills.ts ability): **mind** (Mind Trick): a Willpower contest; a Force user adds half their Force resistance; droids and lore-immune species cannot be affected (Force abilities lore, Mind Trick: droids, Hutts, Toydarians). **avoidable** (Force Push, Force Lightning, Telekinesis throwing an object, the saber throw): the difficulty first, then a target that can react (not surprised) may dodge with d20 + Agility / 10 (Push -5, lightning -6, a thrown object 0). Cover blocks lightning and thrown objects (the cover and crates terrain tags, for ranged fighters); a lightsaber catches lightning (form level); a Force user counters a Push or lightning with Force resistance. **grip** (Force Choke): difficulty only, unless the target is a Force user (Force resistance contest). **object** (telekinesis on an object or a body, the recall): difficulty only. **unopposed** (Force Barrier, Speed, Jump, Absorb, Battle Meditation, Force Heal Self and Others, Revitalize). *Force resistance:* Force users only (Force Control or Force Sense 5+): the best of Force Control, Force Barrier, Force Absorb; everyone else 0. *Difficulties* (DC = 10 + rating / 10, design defaults): a humanoid body 10, a heavy build (B2, droideka, beast) 30, objects small 10 / medium 20 / large 50, lightning 10. *Negation:* the ysalamiri terrain tag is a Force-null bubble (Characters_14, Thrawn): no Force action (and no load), no precognition, no insight. The Yuuzhan Vong (Characters_15 era mechanic) cannot be sensed or directly affected: Push, lightning, grip and mind tricks fail, but a thrown object (and a thrown saber) still hits; precognition and insight fail against them. Taozin are NOT in the lore files: kept in config as Force-invisible, a design default until a lore file covers them. Voxyn hunt through the Force and do not negate it. Entity species gain toydarian and yuuzhanVong. *Mind trick in a fight:* a new Force action: a minion stands down (out of the fight), a named opponent gives an opening (+2). Out of a fight a mind ability on an NPC is the same contest, shown as a check card whose DC is the target's roll. *Open for Jared:* Force lightning on the Yuuzhan Vong is treated as a direct Force effect (blocked); the lore file is silent.
- **D-193 Saber throw (Jared, 2026-11-18; lore: Saber Throw, a Telekinesis application).** A Force action and a sequence step (forceAction saberThrow; intent words such as "throw my saber"), needing a saber in hand, Telekinesis 15 and a form 10. Control: telekinesis (CI as usual) vs rating 15 (DC 11.5), difficulty only (Jared, 2026-11-18; was 20). *Corrected 2026-11-18 (combat pass 1):* the code still had rating 30 (DC 13) when this was first written, and the 70% was an estimate, not a measurement. Now set to 20 and measured (about 2,800 throws each): the average Knight at 50 (Telekinesis 45.9) controls the throw 64.6% of the time and drops the saber 35.2%; a Knight at 30 (36.2) 59.3%; a Padawan at 16 (23.6) 54.2%. Jared's target is about 70% for the average Knight: rating 15 (DC 11.5) gives exactly 70% at Telekinesis 46 (65% at 36); not changed without his word. *Set to rating 15 (Jared, 2026-11-18, after combat pass 1), measured (3,000 throws each, the dev characters' Telekinesis and CI):* the average Knight at 50 (Telekinesis 45.9) controls the throw 69.6% of the time and drops the saber 30.2%; a Knight at 30 (36.2) 64.1%; a Padawan at 16 (23.6) 55.0%. A test pins the value. Against minions, one strike on each engaged minion in the arc, up to 3 (SABER_THROW.maxTargets), each its own roll (d20 + form level / 10 vs d20 + the minion's level / 10) and margin, cut down at margin 6+ (as D-158); each strike is its own roll card. Against a duelist, one strike they can deflect; a Force user may wrench it from its arc (d20 + Force resistance / 10 beating the control total) and the saber drops. A failed control roll drops the saber nearby: the thrower fights unarmed (Agility, no form) until a recall (a Force action: telekinesis vs rating 10). Force load 0.2 (health §2 saber throw), recall 0.1. The duel panel shows Saber throw, Recall saber and Mind trick in the Force row.
- **D-194 Engine sweep follow-ups (Jared, 2026-11-18; bug fixes, UI, report).** *Backlash strain:* the mental load from Force backlash (health §2: +0.5 per Force load point over the limit) was computed in forceMeter.ts and named on the roll card but never reached the character: combat synced only the physical and Force meters. The duel fighter now carries the mental meter (from health), resolveForceAction adds the backlash through applyForceLoad, and healthSynced takes it back to health and fatigue. Test: 28 heavy Pushes give exactly the forceMeter mental load. *"Cuts down":* the note appeared on every cut-down hit, even a graze that left the minion standing (log and narrator). It now says cuts down only when the minion is out; otherwise "wounds X (severity); it fights on" or "grazes X; it fights on" (duel.ts minionHitNote, the saber throw too). A hit on a minion that stays up is a new quiet beat, minionHit ("a minion hit"), instead of minionDown. *Narrator block:* a regex lost its escape and the RESOLVED block carried only the dropped sequence steps, not the resolved and failed ones (D-191 promised every step); fixed, with a test. *Over-limit warning:* the duel panel shows a Force meter once the Force has been used; above 1.0 it is red with "Over your limit: 5% collapse risk per use", and the Force buttons carry the same risk ("Push (5% collapse)", red outline). The rule is unchanged (M6 may redesign it). *Mind trick on an immune target* (a droid, a lore-immune species, someone Force-invisible): the button is greyed out with "(immune)"; a typed attempt is dropped before any roll with the reason ("droids can't be mind-tricked") as a new quiet beat noEffect: no exchange, no time, no Force load, no AI call. *Minion level gaps (report, no tuning):* the dev Knight at 50 fights at 72.6 (Soresu) and the generated minions sit far below D-158's measured gaps: troopers median -38 (D-158: -20), elites -29 (-10), Mandalorians -30 (-10), B1 -59 (-30), militia -73 (-30). At 30 (fighting at 53.6) the gaps are close to D-158's: troopers -19, elites -10, Mandalorians -11; there, balanced play, 600 fights each: troopers 98.5 won / 21.5 wounded (D-158 Soresu at -20: 99.3 / 14.1), elites 88.7 / 56.8 (88.9 / 38.5), Mandalorians at 41% beskar 90.5 / 43.8 (mixed at 60%: 88.7 / 42.9). Win rates agree at the same gaps, so nothing is tuned. Elite wounds are 18 points above D-158's Soresu row but inside its target band (55-75): accepted as is (Jared, 2026-11-18). Generated militia fought at blaster level 0 (the civilian archetype has no blaster pace): fixed in D-195. *Sweep:* tests/sweep/combatSweep.test.ts, `npm run sweep` (not part of test or test:all): about 9,800 fights in 25 s over ranks, minion groups, terrains, play patterns, named duelists and a determinism replay; it fails on broken behaviour (a fight that never ends, bad Vitality, dead and fighting, a limb severed twice, cut down but standing, a zero level bonus on a known skill, resistance from a droid or a non-Force user, a missing step line, no CI from a Force use, a different result for the same seed) and prints the win rates and the generated gaps. tests/support/fightHarness.ts returns the end state.
- **D-195 Minion weapon-skill profiles (Jared, 2026-11-18; bug fix).** *Problem:* a minion's levels came only from its kind's archetype, and the civilian archetype trains no weapon, so militia (blaster pistol), brawlers (melee) and beasts (melee) fought at level 0 at every grade. Every other kind was trained (B1, B2, droideka, trooper, elite: soldier blaster; thug: bounty-hunter blaster; Mandalorian: Mandalorian blaster; Sith acolyte: Sith Warrior forms; grade 1 about 14, grade 5 about 50). *Rule:* every minion kind has a weapon-skill profile (npcGen.config MINION_WEAPON_PROFILE): 'archetype' where the archetype measures the kind's weapon skill (levels unchanged), else a pace for that skill over the grade's years (closed form, technique row, one phase) as a share of the soldier's Knight+ blaster pace 1.326, taken as the higher of it and the archetype's level (engine/npcGen minionWeaponLevel, used by buildMinionFighter). Design defaults: militia basic blaster training 0.25 (grades 1-5: 6.3 / 10.6 / 15.7 / 19.7 / 23.1, with their -1 accuracy: a poor shot, not level 0), brawlers street melee 0.5 (9.4 / 15.8 / 23.3 / 29.3 / 34.3), beasts natural fighting 0.6 (10.5 / 17.6 / 25.9 / 32.5 / 38.1). A test checks every kind's main weapon is trained at every grade and rises with it. *Re-measured,* 10 militia (D-158: 99+ won / 10 or less wounded at -30): dev Knight at 50 (gap -64): 100 / 0.8 (2,000 fights, balanced), 100 / 0 (mixed); Knight at 30 (gap -45): 100 / 4.0, 100 / 1.7; Padawan at 16 (gap -26, nearest D-158's -30): 100 / 10.3, 98.5 / 5.5 (600 each). npm run sweep: 9,840 fights, no issues.

## 2026-11-18 — Fixes from combat pass 1 (Jared)
- **D-196 The narrator interrupting a fight: a category, an outside event, and its own outcome (Jared, 2026-11-18; combat pass 1 blocker).** *Problem:* the narrator sent combatEnded interrupted with why "the combat is still ongoing" and the engine ended a lethal fight against six standing troopers as fled, with XP: any non-empty why passed. *Rules:* (1) from the narration, combatEnded interrupted needs a category from a fixed list (combat.config INTERRUPT_CATEGORIES): reinforcements, environmentCollapse, alarmOrAuthorities, thirdParty, storyEvent (an event already named in the scene). (2) A why that only restates the fight is refused and the fight goes on (INTERRUPT_RESTATES: "the combat is still ongoing", "the fight continues", "they keep fighting", a bare "ongoing"). (3) An interrupted fight is its own outcome, interrupted (COMBAT_OUTCOMES): never fled, never won; the summary keeps the real state ("reinforcements: ...; 6 opponents still standing"), no fate choices open, no heroicAct. Its combat XP uses the fled factor 0.8 on the exchanges actually fought (never the win factor); confirmed by Jared, 2026-11-18. A sparring session interrupted by the story still ends with its bouts so far, and now needs the category too. *Tightened after the re-check:* the narrator then ended a fight with category storyEvent and why "The droids take cover behind the terrain."; a storyEvent or thirdParty why that names the fighters (the words of their names, or opponent, enemy, attacker, foe: INTERRUPT_FIGHTER_WORDS) is refused as something the fighters do, not an outside event (a test holds the exact case; reinforcements may still be more of the same kind). combat-narration.md and ai-events.md amended; a test holds the exact case. *Amended (Jared, 2026-11-18, after the re-check): storyEvent is removed, and every interruption needs its cause in the same reply* (combat.config INTERRUPT_CAUSES): reinforcements and thirdParty need someone entering the scene (npcIntroduced, or a sceneUpdated that adds someone present who is not a fighter and was not present before the reply; there is no npcArrived event, so these two stand for it), environmentCollapse needs hazardOccurred, alarmOrAuthorities needs alarmRaised (a new AI event with no state change) or an arrival. A cause the engine cannot see is refused and the fight goes on. combatEnded is applied last in its reply (a new 'last' apply phase; handlers see the events applied so far in the reply and the state before it), so the cause may come before or after it. The fighter-words check stays for thirdParty. Tests: each category with and without its cause, a cause of the wrong kind, a sceneUpdated naming only fighters and people already present, the re-check's exact reply (no cause), and the sparring-session interruption.
- **D-197 Narration faithfulness: plain labels, an outcome list, and a check before commit (Jared, 2026-11-18; combat pass 1, the V170 failure mode).** *Problem:* the narrator added hits the log did not have (a build-up exchange logged "strike: lands" became "your blade catches him cleanly" and "a touch against his ribs"; a thrown saber "carved through" a droid it missed), turned a moderate wound into a severed arm, narrated a Force push cut by the cap, left a severe wound out, gave blaster-armed Mandalorians a gauntlet strike, and invented bacta and Force healing. *Rules:* (1) **Plain labels.** A sequence step's line is `step N <label>: DONE | FAILED | NOT REACHED (<what happened>)` (sequence.ts stepLine). Build-up exchanges never say "lands": they say gains ground, holds or loses ground, with "no hit". Only a real hit says "hits", with the severity and the location in plain words (duel.ts hitText, exchangeOutcomeText: "hits B1 battle droid 2: moderate wound to the left arm; out of the fight"). A feint or taunt line carries its dice and margin. Raw part ids (leftArm) no longer reach the narrator. (2) **The outcome list** (turn/combatFacts.ts, context.ts describeCombat). The COMBAT block gives each fighter's weapon ("Weapons (the only ones in this fight)"), one line per exchange marked HIT (target, location, severity) or NO HIT, "Steps the player asked for" with DONE / FAILED (reason) / NOT REACHED, "Hits in this passage" (or NONE), and "Did not happen" (misses, failed Force uses, steps not reached). combat-narration.md opens with an ABSOLUTE RULE: nothing outside the block happened; no injury, severing, healing, item, weapon or effect that is not in it; nothing marked NO HIT, FAILED or NOT REACHED may be described as happening; a wound is exactly the severity and place listed; a fighter uses only the weapon on the Weapons line. (3) **The check before commit** (turn/narrationCheck.ts, run in narrateTurn). Six narrow rules, each skipped in a clause or sentence with a near-miss or negation word: severing words with no sever in the block and no limb lost earlier; the player's blade landing (or a touch scored) when the player hit nobody, and the player being hit when nobody hit them; a numbered minion that was not hit and still stands described as cut, carved through or brought down; a Force step marked NOT REACHED described as done (matched on how that ability reads in prose); bacta, kolto or Force healing with no such care applied (only when the character is wounded, or on a time jump or a healing action); a wounding hit on the player or a named opponent whose location the narration never mentions (the wound left out; added beyond Jared's list because the pass's worst case was an omission). On a contradiction the narration is discarded and the call is made once more with the same TurnDraft and a CORRECTION line listing the contradictions (one extra AI call, same dice). If the retry still contradicts the block, code-written lines (the outcome list in plain sentences; for a healing contradiction, the narration with the offending sentences removed) replace the narration, and the engine log records it. Fixture tests hold each case of the report with the narrator's own words, the good narrations of the pass (no false alarms), the retry and the fallback. (4) **Montage and time skip.** RESOLVED carries "Care applied this turn: ..." (careStarted, woundsStabilized) or "none. Wounds heal only by rest and time", on a time jump, a healing action or when care was applied; the montage and time-skip prompts tell the narrator to describe only that care. *Re-check false alarm, fixed:* a sentence saying the care was NOT bacta ("treated only by standard medpacs ... rather than restorative bacta") was flagged and cost a retry; "not", "rather than", "instead of", "only by", "without the luxury of" and the like now count as healing not applied. *Known gaps (Jared, 2026-11-18):* the weapon rule is the prompt and the Weapons line only (no code check for a gauntlet strike), and a wound placed on the wrong fighter is not detected. Combat pass 2 checks for both; code checks are added only if they show up.
- **D-198 Wounds that change how a fighter fights: a severed leg ends the duel; the weapon arm is built but off (Jared, 2026-11-18; combat pass 1).** *Problem:* a duelist with a severed leg and a severe sword-arm wound kept duelling at four fifths strength and wounded the player. *Rules* (health.config WOUND_FIGHTING; duel.ts cannotStand, weaponArmState, cannotDuel): (1) **Severed leg (lore-required, adopted):** a named fighter whose leg is severed in the fight, or who has lost a leg with no prosthetic (aid none or scar tissue), cannot stand or duel and is out of the fight; the exchange notes it ("has lost a leg and cannot stand: out of the fight"). A basic prosthetic or cybernetic leg is exempt. A downed opponent opens the fate choice (spare, capture, kill: finished off); a downed player has lost the fight and the opponent's fate roll decides. Minions keep their own rule (out on a moderate wound). (2) **Weapon arm (built, switched off: weaponArm false; M6):** an unhealed severe or worse wound to the weapon arm or hand switches the weapon to the off hand at 0.4 on the fighting skill (the unadapted lost-hand penalty on forms); a severed weapon arm or hand puts the fighter out of the fight until there is a second weapon (items, M8). The weapon hand is the character's dominant hand (NPCs: right). *Not built:* surrendering or using the Force from the ground after losing a leg: the fight ends for that fighter instead (for Jared: M6, with NPC Force actions). *Measured, rolls §8 table 1* (10,000 duels per gap; win % / median exchanges; target in brackets):
  | Gap | Before | Leg rule (adopted) |
  |---|---|---|
  | 0 (50 / 34) | 49.4 / 37 | 49.6 / 27 |
  | +5 (60 / 33) | 61.2 / 35 | 60.2 / 25 |
  | +10 (68 / 34) | 68.6 / 36 | 67.5 / 26 |
  | +20 (84 / 29) | 83.3 / 31 | 82.0 / 23 |
  | +30 (92 / 24) | 91.2 / 26 | 90.1 / 19 |
  | +40 (96 / 20) | 95.4 / 23 | 94.5 / 16 |
  | +60 (99 / 13) | 98.7 / 16 | 98.5 / 12 |
  Win rates stay inside +/-3; duels are about 10 exchanges shorter (saber hits sever limbs often, D-161: 27% of wounds). With the weapon-arm rule on as well (3,000 duels): gap 0 50.0 / 22, +10 67.1 / 22, +20 79.0 / 20 (win 5 under the table), +30 88.7 / 16: it breaks the win gate at +20 and puts the even duel 8 exchanges under the 30-40 band, so it stays off for M6. *Gates moved by the leg rule (test:all now fails these four; left failing for Jared to decide: amend the gates or retune duel length):* duelTables test 1: median at gaps 0-10 is 25-27 (band 30-40, D-156) and at +20 it is 23 (29 +/-5); Anakin "never beyond 4.0x the Average pace": tier 41 ratio 3.90 (needs 4 or more); Anakin Clone Wars ages 19-22: combat 22.4% of the yearly main-form XP (band 25-35); Obi-Wan Clone Wars ages 35-38: 35.0% at age 37 (band 20-30). Shorter duels give fewer exchanges of combat XP per fight and end fights at different wounds, which moves the profile shares both ways.
- **D-199 Engine and input fixes from combat pass 1 (Jared, 2026-11-18; bug fixes).** (1) **A stance step is outside the sequence cap** (D-182: a stance is a setting): the cap counts actions only, so "press him with two quick cuts, then a Force push" (stance, strike, strike, push) fits a Knight's cap of 3. (2) **The opponent's initiative.** While the opponent holds the strike (advantage -3 or lower) the duel panel warns "Opponent has the initiative: a sequence may be cut short". The first step still resolves; if the track is still at -3 or lower and steps remain, the opponent's strike is resolved as its own exchange and the remaining steps are NOT REACHED with that as the reason ("X had the initiative and struck first (#n: ...)"). Before, the chain stopped with "the opponent seized the strike" and no strike happened. (3) **Feint regression (from D-194):** a feint or taunt rolls a contest without an exchange record, so D-194's "no new exchange = nothing attempted" turned a failed feint into "no effect". A step that was DONE or FAILED now always makes the beat (noEffect only when every step was NOT REACHED), the step line carries the dice and the margin ("step 1 feint: FAILED (seen through: A d20 6 = 9.4 vs B d20 15 = 21.0 (margin -11.6))"), and the feed card lists each step line above the roll cards. (4) **Rock throw:** a throw uses the object named, or any other throwable the fight still has; "nothing left to throw here" only when every throwable in the fight's tags is spent. (5) **Typed rest with care:** over a span of days, a rest, heal or sleep intent (or any intent with healing set) makes the montage rest and recovery days with no training (advanceByRoutine rest), and a heal intent or a healing source starts the care first: medical for the whole span (x2, D-150), a bacta tank for its 6 h, a Force healer for 1 h when there is one. The montage branch used to return before the healing code. intent.md: resting while treated is actionType rest with healing set, never train. (6) **Typed words end a sparring session** like the Stop sparring button (combatWords STOP_SPAR_WORDS: end the sparring session, stop sparring, that's enough, enough for today, I yield, solah, and the like).
- **D-200 Slugthrowers (Jared, 2026-11-18; Legends; the equipment system is M8).** Three weapon kinds: slugthrower pistol (base 13), slugthrower rifle (18), scatter gun (22) (design defaults near the blasters). Non-saber weapons (wounds by margin, D-161), ranged, fired with the blaster skill until M8. Against a lightsaber (weapons.ts; rolls §4): the blade blocks slugs (they melt on it) with the normal defense roll, but never redirects them (no Soresu or Shien redirect), and the forms' vs-blaster matchups do not apply (energy bolts only). Rapid fire (the rifle) and scatter shot (the scatter gun) give a defender holding a saber -1 on the defense (design default); the pistol does not. Mandalorians may carry one instead of the blaster pistol, rolled per Mandalorian by era (MANDALORIAN_SLUGTHROWERS, design defaults: Old Republic eras 0.15, the late Republic and the Clone Wars 0.2, the Empire and after 0.25), the kind by weight (pistol 0.5, rifle 0.3, scatter gun 0.2); named Mandalorians use their canon weapon (CanonNpc.weapon), else a seeded roll. The narrator's Weapons line names them ("a slugthrower rifle (rapid solid slugs, not bolts)").
- **D-201 The engine's reason for every miss (Jared, 2026-11-18; combat pass 1, issue 19 and R4).** *Problem:* the narrator explained misses with causes the engine never produced ("your footing slips on the scorched earth", "your grip slips on the energy matrix", "a critical misstep"). *Rule* (turn/combatFacts.ts strikeMissReason, buildUpLoss): every NO HIT line in the narrator's COMBAT block carries the reason. A strike that does not land: the defender **parried** (a saber or melee weapon against a melee attack), **deflected on the blade** (a saber against a bolt or a slug), **dodged** (no weapon to block with, or a melee weapon against a shot), or **dodged: foresaw it with precognition**. A Force use keeps its own reasons (D-191; cover is one of them: **blocked by cover**). A build-up exchange: **both held** on a tie, the loser **gave ground** on a 1-step loss, and **was outpaced** on a loss of 2 or more steps (rounded margin 6+, BUILD_UP; Jared's reading of "outpaced"). combat-narration.md: explain each miss only with that reason; nobody slips, stumbles, loses footing or grip, misjudges a step or overextends unless the block lists a terrain accident for that fighter. Tests: each reason from a block, and the prompt rule.
- **D-202 The check before commit: a wound on the wrong body location (Jared, 2026-11-18; combat pass 1 re-check, scenario 6).** *Problem:* after a week of medical care the narrator treated "your deep torso wound"; the engine's wound was the right arm. *Rule 7* (turn/narrationCheck.ts misplacedIssues): a wound described on a body location different from the block's locations for that fighter is a contradiction (woundMisplaced). The locations: the player character's wounds before or after the turn (health), every fighter's current wounds (CombatFighterView.woundParts, new), limbs lost, and this passage's hits. An arm wound may be told at the wrist or the shoulder and a hand wound up the arm; legs, torso and head stay apart. A clause counts only with a wound word (wound, gash, burn, treat, tend, pain, ...) and the fighter's possessive ("your ...", or "his / her / their / its ..." in a sentence naming the opponent; up to three describing words, never another possessive or an article, so "your blade cuts his chest" is not your chest); near misses, aims, hypotheticals and negations are skipped (a narrower list than the hit rules', which also skip "recover"). Only a fighter with a known wound is checked: an invented wound on an unhurt fighter stays the hit rules' business. Minions are not checked. On a contradiction the retry and fallback work as in D-197; outside a fight the fallback drops the offending sentence. Tests: the exact medic-week sentence, the same week told correctly (forearm, shoulder), an opponent's wound on the wrong limb, and another fighter's chest not read as the player's. This narrows D-197's known gap (a wound on the wrong fighter is still not detected in general).
- **D-203 Rule bugs from the numbers list (Jared, 2026-11-18; combat pass 1, numbers 2, 10 and 13).** (1) **The wound penalty eases as the wound heals.** Each wound's location penalty and its share of the status penalty are multiplied by the share still unhealed, 1 - healProgress (engine/wounds.ts unhealedShare): a severe arm wound at 67% healed gives a third of its penalty (pass 1: still -12%). The wound load itself (status labels, incapacitation) does not ease until the wound has healed. health.md §6 was silent and is amended. (2) **Montage XP only to abilities the character knows.** Synergy spillover skips a Force ability below level 1 (the combat rule for "knows", engine/synergy.ts); stats, forms and career skills are unchanged. A Guardian's Force Control practice no longer gives Force Barrier, Force Absorb or Battle Meditation XP. (3) **Feints and taunts roll with the form level.** The trick level is the best of Charisma, Intelligence and the level of the form (or weapon skill) the player fights with now (rolls §5 already listed it; combat/index.ts fightingFormLevel). Pass 1 R9: a Guardian's feint rolled "d20 6 = 6.0". Tests: the penalty at 2/3 healed and at fully healed, the label unchanged; spillover to known and unknown abilities; the feint's level bonus in the step line.
- **D-204 No free Force turns (Jared, 2026-11-18; combat pass 1, number 14 and R8).** *Problem:* a Force action resolved with no exchange for the other side, so the droids did nothing for two turns while an unarmed Knight used only the Force, and Force-heavy play was safer than fighting (the sweep's heavyForce pattern was never wounded). *Rule* (combatForce.ts resolveForceAction; duel.ts ExchangeExtras.duringForce): a player's Force action takes its exchange of time (5-10 s; no Force action here has a longer time of its own), and during it the opponents act: one exchange against the current target in which the player only defends. The player never strikes in it, a won build-up holds the track (it never moves toward the player, no opening from the opponent's natural 1, no cut-down), and an opponent holding the strike (-3 or lower) strikes. The exchange takes no time of its own (seconds 0: the Force action's time covers it) and costs exertion as usual. It follows every player Force action that leaves the fight going: standalone, a sequence step (so a sequence's later steps see the new track, and D-199's initiative rule still applies after it), a recall, a failed or misfired use. Unarmed after a dropped saber, the player defends with Agility. The narrator's block marks it "(while X uses the Force and only defends)". NPC Force actions are M6. The duel tables, the lifetime harness and the duel-length targets use no Force actions and are unchanged. *Measured* (npm run sweep, before -> after; win % / wounded %): dev Knight at 50, mixed pattern: 6 elites 98.7 / 13.0 -> 95.0 / 28.7 (balanced 96.7 / 28.0), 4 Mandalorians 99.7 / 5.3 -> 99.7 / 17.0, 6 troopers 100 / 3.0 -> 99.3 / 9.0, 10 B1 100 / 1.0 -> 99.7 / 4.3; heavyForce: wounded 0.0 everywhere -> 1-26%, wins about the same (67-92 -> 70-94; collapses still decide them). Padawan 16 mixed vs 6 troopers 92.5 / 32.5 -> 82.5 / 60.0 (40 fights). Mixed play is no longer safer than fighting. Tests: the R8 case (an unarmed Knight pushing three droids is attacked; the exchange takes no time; the player never strikes or gains in it), the Push opening, D-199 after a Force step.
- **D-205 Combat pass 1 issues 16-21 and R3-R7 (Jared, 2026-11-18; bug fixes, UI, narrator block).** *16, the Push card and the track:* a pattern's first-use opening (D-146) is now applied inside the Force action (ForceActionInput.firstUse), before the record is written and before the opponents' exchange (D-204); the card read -3 -> -1 while the panel showed +3. *17, old saves:* a Force record from before D-192 (no outcome, no difficulty) reads "resisted", never "undefined (DC undefined)". *18, fate choices:* each choice keeps its time, place and whether the opponent is dying; it is offered only while no fight has begun since, at the same place, and within FATE_CHOICE_EXPIRY (1 game hour, design default); a new fight clears them; an older entry without a date is not offered; fateChosen on a passed choice is refused. A dying opponent reads "is dying" with "Spare X (leave dying)". *19, the narrator's block (with Jared's adjustments):* (a) **Blade colours** (data/weapons.ts bladeColorFor; Fighter.bladeColor; CanonNpc.bladeColor): a Jedi's blade is blue or green (Guardian blue, Consular green, any other Jedi a seeded pick by name), a Sith's red, Sith acolytes red; canon figures use the lore colour: Darth Malgus and Darth Marr crimson (SW_Characters_09: "Wields a crimson blade."; no other era-9 figure has one in the lore files). The Weapons line lists it ("a lightsaber, blue blade"); a training saber lists none. (b) **Advantage now:** who has the upper hand, how much, and whether they hold the strike, "do not describe the other side pressing an advantage". (c) **Bout result:** who scored the bout's clean touch, and that the player WON or LOST the bout. (d) **The thrown object** by its terrain tag ("a fallen log", "a loose rock"; ForceActionRecord.objectTag). (e) **One target:** a Push, Choke, mind trick, throw or lightning line says "Only X is affected; the others are not" when others stand. (f) Ability names in words in the block (Force Push, Telekinesis). combat-narration.md: colours only from the Weapons line; the player character's feelings and state of mind only from the block (the Condition line); opponents may show visible expressions and body language as flavor if nothing contradicts the outcomes; the advantage, the Push target and the thrown object as listed; say who won a bout. *R3, R5:* second person throughout; never the outcome list's terms in prose ("a moderate strike", "HIT", "margin"), show the severity instead. *20, roll cards:* a build-up exchange that cuts a minion down is headed by the hit with its wound in words; the margin is shown from the player's side ("Margin for Kael Voss -10.4"), never "-0.0"; a Force use reads "roll vs DC +8.4 (then blocked by cover)"; one entry per modifier source (the stance's attack and defense net to 0 and drop out); "numbers" without "(+3)" repeated; a blank birth year is no date (no "does not fit age -22.0"); "1 rest or recovery day"; a span of days with no activity named no longer reports "nothing trained". *21, labels:* ability names, body parts ("left arm") on cards, the health card and aimed steps (R7); "won: every opponent is down", "lost: sparring session: stopped"; the bank release note as a date; minion names capitalised ("Line trooper 1"); a droid is "Disabled" or "Destroyed", never "Dying"; "(5% collapse)" keeps its case. Dev only: /fatigue during a fight reaches the duel panel (devDuelMetersSynced); a /fight place word ("terrain temple") moves the scene to "Temple (dev sandbox)". Tests: tests/ui/cards.test.ts, tests/turn/blockDetails.test.ts, the issue-16 card, fate expiry, the two dev commands.
- **D-206 Duel length restored after the severed-leg rule: pressure 2, precognition min(0.4, FS / 250) (Jared, 2026-11-19; tunables only).** *Problem:* D-198's severed-leg rule (kept, lore) shortened duels by about 10 exchanges: medians 27 / 25 / 26 at gaps 0-10 (band 30-40) and 23 at +20 (29 +/- 5). Jared: retune the duel-length tunables (pressure, exertion, precognition, beat or strike frequency) to restore the rolls §8 table 1, wins within +/-3. *Search* (a throwaway grid over the config through vi.doMock, 2,000 duels per gap, then 10,000): exertion has almost no effect (0.08 -> 0.04: medians unchanged); pressure (MISSED_STRIKE_STEP) and precognition do. Pressure 2 alone: 32 / 29 / 31 / 26 (gap 5 under the band); pressure 3 with D-156's precognition: 37 / 31 / 33 / 29 with gap 0 wins 47.5 (edge); precognition back at the original min(0.5, FS / 200) with pressure 2: 36 / 33 / 35 / 30 / 25 / 21 / 14 at 10,000 (passes). STRIKE_AT and BEAT_EVERY were not needed (BEAT_EVERY changes only how often the narrator is called, not duel length). *Adopted:* MISSED_STRIKE_STEP 2 (was 1) and PRECOG min(0.4, Force Sense / 250) (was min(0.25, FS / 400)), the setting closest to the table. *Measured, rolls §8 table 1, 10,000 duels per gap (win % / median exchanges; target in brackets):* gap 0 50.6 / 34 (50 / 34), +5 60.4 / 32 (60 / 33), +10 69.3 / 33 (68 / 34), +20 83.5 / 29 (84 / 29), +30 91.7 / 24 (92 / 24), +40 95.8 / 20 (96 / 20), +60 98.8 / 14 (99 / 13). Scenario table: straight 25.8 (23), high ground 69.5 (68), reckless 79.0 (81), surprise within +/-5 (test passes); Jedi 70 vs trooper 15: 100% won, median 2 exchanges; one level of difference moves the odds 1.06 / 0.85 / 0.36 points (limit 2.5). duelTables tests 1, 2, 3 and 5 pass. Sweep: no issues; minion fights move by 1-3 points (Knight 50 balanced, wounded %: 6 troopers 6.7 -> 5.7, elites 28.0 -> 25.7, Mandalorians 26.3 -> 24.7). rolls.md §3 amended. No gate was amended. *Lifetime suite after D-201..D-206:* Anakin's gates (the 4.0x floor, the Clone Wars share 25-35%) pass again; Obi-Wan's Clone Wars share was 25 / 24 / 32% at ages 35-37 (band 20-30), so his last war year goes from 12 to 11 gap-0 duels (tests/lifetime/profiles.ts, fight counts only, as Jared asked): 25 / 24 / 27% on the fixed seed; 5 extra seeds 26/23/22, 23/27/27, 23/32/22, 23/27/27, 24/27/24 (one year at 32 on seed 3, the same kind of spread as D-159). One lifetime test still fails, and not from the duel retune: the Average's Y_avg at age 90 is 291.7 against the table's 266 (+9.7%, limit 5%). Diagnosed: it comes from D-203 (2), spillover only to known abilities (with that line removed, all 8 Average tests and all 4 Obi-Wan tests pass; applying the 25% session cap before dropping unknown targets leaves Y_avg at exactly 291.7 and breaks three Anakin gates, so it is not the cap scaling). Y_avg is a measured progression table (D-48 clamp input, D-166), so it is left failing for Jared.
- **D-207 Y_avg re-measured after the known-abilities spillover rule; adopted (Jared, 2026-11-19).** *Why:* D-203 (2), spillover only to Force abilities the character knows, moved the Average's yearly main-form XP at age 90 from the table's 266 to 291.7 on the fixed seed (+9.7%, limit 5%). Jared kept the fix and asked for the D-166 re-measure. *Method (as D-166):* the Average with its yearly mission, training XP only (combat XP excluded), median of the fixed seed and 5 more (average-s1..s5). *Old -> new (change; range of the 6 runs):* 4: 582 -> 582 (-0.1%; 576-591), 8: 607 -> 607 (0.0%; 595-620), 12: 557 -> 557 (0.0%; 550-566), 16: 534 -> 531 (-0.6%; 506-541), 20: 551 -> 550 (-0.2%; 547-552), 24: 444 -> 450 (+1.3%; 442-457), 30: 395 -> 396 (+0.2%; 391-398), 40: 468 -> 471 (+0.6%; 466-476), 50: 453 -> 448 (-1.0%; 436-453), 60: 485 -> 487 (+0.4%; 475-492), 70: 438 -> 440 (+0.5%; 433-451), 80: 439 -> 436 (-0.7%; 424-438), 90: 266 -> 289 (+8.8%; 282-296), 98: 194 -> 197 (+1.6%; 192-201). Only age 90 moves by more than 1.3%. *4.0x clamp check* (the lifetime suite on the same code with the old and with the new table, fixed seeds): no test outcome changes except the Average's Y_avg test, which now passes (old table 35/36, new 36/36). Milestones, old -> new: Average and Luke unchanged; Obi-Wan 61 23.71 -> 23.65, 81 39.44 -> 39.41, 101 59.70 -> 59.65, war shares 25 / 24 / 27 -> 24 / 24 / 26%; Anakin 21 1.29 and 41 4.18 unchanged (the 4.0x floor holds), 61 8.42 -> 9.26, 81 14.89 -> 15.82 (limit 16.2: 1.2 x 13.5), 101 21.14 -> 22.35 (the 4.0x clamp binds him from his teens; the table fell slightly at 16 and 20, which lowers his cap). Every gate passes, but Anakin's 81 is now 0.38 y inside its limit. *Adopted:* progression.config Y_AVG; progression test 24 pins yAvg(20) = 550 (was 551).

## 2026-11-19 — Fixes from combat pass 2 (Jared's list; built overnight)
- **D-208 The dropped saber, physical exhaustion and the safety cap (Jared, combat pass 2 blocker; built overnight).** *Problem:* after a failed saber throw the Knight fought 322 exchanges bare-handed on Resolve to end and lost to six B1s; the physical meter reached 26.4 / 5.1. *Why 26.4 / 5.1 was possible:* every exchange adds EXERTION_PER_EXCHANGE (0.08) x the form's stamina multiplier to the physical meter with no ceiling; in a fight the meter only lowers the effective level through e(f) (fighterEffectiveLevel: x(1 - 0.25 x (1 - e)), never below x0.775 once e = 0.1 past 1.0), Spent is tied to Vitality alone, and progression-numbers §3 defines no physical hard limit. So an unarmed fighter who was never wounded badly enough kept fighting at 77.5% for ever, and the passive buttons (Next beat, Fight on, Resolve to end) never take a Force action, so nothing recalled the saber. *Rules:* (1) **Auto recall** (engine/sequence.ts runPassive, canAutoRecall): when the player is unarmed with their own saber on the ground, knows telekinesis and the Force reaches here (no ysalamiri), the passive buttons try a recall first (a Force action, with D-204's opponents' exchange). Next beat stops after the attempt (a Force-action beat); Fight on stops after a failed attempt (the next press retries) and fights on after a good one; Resolve to end retries until the saber is back or the fight ends. Each attempt is logged as "auto recall: step 1 saber recall: DONE | FAILED (...)". Typed play is unchanged. (2) **Physical collapse** (health.config PHYSICAL_COLLAPSE.hardLimit 2.0, duel.ts collapsedFromExhaustion): a fighter whose physical meter passes 2.0 x capacity collapses: out of the fight, no wound, logged ("collapses from exhaustion (physical X / Y, past the hard limit of 2x)"); a player's fight ends lost with reason exhausted (the opponent's fate roll applies, as for any player who is down); a minion folds; a named opponent is down (fate choices). It is checked after every exchange and in settleEnd, so nobody fights on past it. Spent at Vitality 0 is unchanged (health §3). The value is an overnight design default (progression-numbers §3 has none): 2.0 leaves the rolls §8 tables unchanged (duelTables passes; a 34-exchange duel adds about 2.7 load-hours to a capacity near 5); alternatives for Jared: 1.5 (collapse after about 95 exchanges from fresh), or a chance per exchange past 1.0 like the Force meter's 5%. (3) **Safety cap** (combat.config FIGHT_SAFETY_CAP.exchanges 200): a real fight with no outcome at exchange 200 ends as an exhausted stalemate, both sides Spent (Vitality 0); the new outcome stalemate (COMBAT_OUTCOMES) gets the fled and interrupted XP factor 0.8 (overnight decision: the most conservative existing factor for a fight nobody won). Sparring sessions keep their own budget (D-168) and are exempt. Tests: tests/engine/exhaustionAndRecall.test.ts (the recall in each mode and its preconditions; the pass-2 case through the turn: 6 fights of a Knight-30 throwing until the saber drops then Resolve to end, the recall seen, at least 5 of 6 won, every fight under the cap; the exchange that crosses the hard limit puts the fighter out, a minion too; the stalemate at the cap; a spar exempt).
- **D-209 The fallback narration is a summary; history never carries a raw log (Jared, combat pass 2 issue 2; built overnight).** *Problem:* D-197's code-written fallback was "the outcome list in plain sentences": one sentence per exchange. For the 322-exchange Resolve to end it was 39,541 characters of engine sentences in the story, and the next two narrative prompts carried about 19,000 input tokens (7,300 usual). *Rules:* (1) **The summary** (turn/narrationCheck.ts combatSummary; combat.config NARRATION_FALLBACK: maxLines 10, maxKeyEvents 5, maxLineChars 300): the count of exchanges and Force uses with the player's hits and wounds each way; the key events (severed limbs first, then the worst wounds, told in exchange order, each with the striker, the target, the severity and the location); the other wounds by fighter and location in one capped sentence (so the check's woundOmitted rule holds); the count of misses and failed attempts; the steps done or failed; the steps not reached with their reason; the end state (the outcome and the opponent's choice, or how many opponents still stand). The end-state line always survives the cap. No HIT / NO HIT, margins, reasons or exchange numbers. (2) **History** (turn/context.ts clipNarrative; context.config HISTORY_NARRATIVE_MAX_CHARS 2400): one turn's narration in the recent-turns window is clipped at a word to 2,400 characters with "(...)", so saves from before D-209 that hold a raw log cannot crowd the prompt. Ordinary narrations (600 to 2,000 characters) are untouched. *Tests:* tests/turn/fallbackSummary.test.ts (a 300-exchange passage: at most 10 sentences, the counts line and the end state, no engine wording, under 2,000 characters; it adds fewer than 500 tokens to the next prompt's history; a short passage names every wound; a 39,000-character old fallback clipped to 2,400); tests/turn/narrationCheck.test.ts (the pass-1 fallback case rewritten for the summary and still passing the check).
- **D-210 Input parsing: Force gestures, unknown abilities, repeated narration (Jared, combat pass 2 issues 3 and 9; built overnight).** (1) **Force gestures** (data/combatWords.ts FORCE_GESTURE; turn/combatTurn.ts isStrikeClause, forceActionIn): "thrust out my hand", "raise my palm", "extend both hands", "sweep my arm", "hold out my hand" and the like (a gesture verb, an optional direction, owner and describing word, then hand / hands / palm / arm / fingers / fist) are taken out of a clause before its strike and Force words are read, so a gesture is never a strike and never a Force word on its own ("fling out my hand" is not a throw); it belongs to the Force ability named in the same clause or to the intent's Force step. A thrust, cut or lunge with a weapon is still a strike. intent.md says the same. (2) **An ability the character does not know** (engine/sequence.ts UNKNOWN_PREFIX): the step line is "FAILED (you don't know Force Lightning)" (the skill's name); nothing is rolled, no exchange, no Force load. A typed Force action no longer ends in a rejected event (pass 2: no line, and the narrator repeated its previous reply): it runs as a one-step sequence, and when nothing else was attempted the beat is noEffect: quiet, no AI call, the quiet line "You don't know Force Lightning." Inside a longer sequence the step fails with its reason and the chain goes on (nothing was rolled, so nothing failed in play); the other steps resolve and are narrated. (3) **A narration that repeats the previous reply** (turn/narrationCheck.ts repeatedIssues, rule 8): a reply of 25 words or more of which at least 60% of the sentences match a sentence of the previous story reply at 80% word overlap (or the whole text overlaps 85%) is a contradiction, one issue per repeated sentence: retried once with "narrate only what RESOLVED says happened this turn, in new words"; if the retry repeats too, a fight gets the D-209 summary and other turns drop the repeated sentences. The previous reply is the last non-dev record's narration. Thresholds are design defaults (overnight). *Tests:* tests/turn/inputParsing.test.ts (the two pass-2 inputs give Force steps only; seven more gesture phrasings; real thrusts and cuts stay strikes; through the turn, Force lightning for a Jedi who does not know it is FAILED with the reason, quiet, no exchange, no reject; inside a sequence the next step still resolves; the pass-2 repeat word for word and nearly word for word; a new reply that shares a phrase is not a repeat); tests/engine/forceResistance.test.ts (an unknown telekinesis now FAILED); tests/turn/pipeline.test.ts (a fixture that returned the same reply on two turns now returns new text).
- **D-211 Narration check: slips, the outcome list's terms, kill vocabulary (Jared, combat pass 2 issues 7, 13 and 15; built overnight).** turn/narrationCheck.ts, three more rules in a fight's narration, each conservative (clause-level, with negation and near-miss exemptions). (1) **Rule 9, slips with no hazard** (slipNoHazard; D-201 enforced in code): a slip, stumble, trip, misstep, overextension, or lost or faltering footing, footwork, balance or grip, in a clause with no negation, when the block lists no terrain accident ("Terrain accident: ..." from the engine's terrain rolls). Exempt: a blade or bolt slipping past a guard, someone slipping away, "a slip of focus" (pass 1's accepted wording for a natural 1 on a Force use), keeping or regaining footing, and staggering or stumbling back when a Push or thrown object succeeded or a knockdown landed in the block. (2) **Rule 10, the outcome list's terms** (mechanicalTerms; D-205 R5 enforced): a severity word used as a label ("a moderate wound", "a severe, smoking cut", "a painful, moderate wound", "your critical strike"; minor, moderate, severe, critical before wound / strike / blow / hit / cut / graze / injury / burn / gash, and "mortal strike / blow / hit"), "margin", "advantage track" or the track moving, "quiet / narrated / next / this beat", "exchange 12" or "#12", the upper-case labels (DONE, HIT, NO HIT, NOT REACHED, FAILED), "stamina only", "the roll fell short", "short of the difficulty", "DC n". Natural phrasing passes: "a deep cut", "a grievous wound", "a mortal wound", "a fierce exchange of blows", "a beat later", "the narrow track". (3) **Kill vocabulary**: the numbered-minion rule's damage verbs gain shear, dismantle, demolish, obliterate, decapitate, wreck, smash, rend, split, tear or rip through or apart; and a new **rule 3b**: when the player hit no minion in the passage (no hit and no mind trick standing one down), a sentence about the player (you / your) that names a minion noun (the last word of a minion's name: droid, trooper, warrior; or chassis, automaton, machine) with a kill verb (shear, carve through, cut down or through, slice through or apart, cleave, bisect, destroy, fell, drop it, dismantle, demolish, obliterate, decapitate, tear through, wreck, reduce to scrap) is minionNotHit, unless the verb is only the move's aim ("to cut down the nearest droid"). An ally cutting a droid down is not the player. *Fallback:* a reply whose only issues are wording (rules 9 and 10) keeps the retry's narration: a severity label becomes natural words (minor light, moderate deep, severe grievous, critical terrible, mortal deadly), and a sentence with another engine term or an invented slip is dropped (repairWording); any factual issue still gets the D-209 summary. *Fixture changes:* three pass-1 fixtures now also flag the invented stumble ("his stumble") or the label ("a critical strike"), and three synthetic wound-location sentences say "a deep wound" instead of "a moderate wound". *Tests:* tests/turn/narrationCheckPass2.test.ts (the six pass-2 slip sentences and six allowed cases; eight term sentences, five natural ones, the retry and the repaired fallback; the exact pass-2 kill sentence, the same with a droid hit, an ally, an aim, six kill synonyms).
- **D-212 Combat rules from pass 2: the opponent's initiative, the Force-action exchange's label, the narrator's hazards, allies (Jared, combat pass 2 issues 4, 6, 8 and 11; built overnight).** (1) **The opponent's initiative** (engine/sequence.ts): when a strike or maneuver step comes up while the opponent holds the strike (advantage -3 or lower), the exchange is the opponent's strike, that step is NOT REACHED with "X had the initiative and struck first (#n: ...)", and the chain stops; the step's aim and maneuver are not set. Pass 2 had the player's step 1 marked DONE ("holds the strike: no opening to attack") with the opponent's strike standing in for it and the chain going on. A Force step at -3 still resolves (D-204's opponents' exchange follows it), and the existing rule after a step (the track at -3 with steps left) is unchanged. (2) **Build-up lines follow the real track** (turn/combatFacts.ts buildUpLead): "X gains ground" only when the track moved toward X; a won roll that moved nothing is "X presses: no gain (the track does not move)", and during the player's Force action "X holds: no gain (X only defends while using the Force)"; a tie "neither gains ground (both held)"; a move the other way (a natural 1's opening) names the side it moved toward. Pass 2 had the defending player "outpacing" droids four times from "gains ground" lines. (3) **The narrator's hazards** (features/health hazardOccurred; turn/pipeline.ts): while a fight is active, the narrator's hazardOccurred does no damage and nothing is stored pending; it is applied as an event with no state change, so it can still be the cause of an environmentCollapse interruption in the same reply (D-196; overnight decision: refusing it outright would make that interruption impossible). In a turn where a fight ended in the draft, or where the engine already resolved a hazard (a terrain accident, an intent's risk), the narrator's hazardOccurred is refused before it is applied. Outside fights the rule is unchanged except that a reply may carry one hazard (a second is a duplicate and refused). Pass 2's two cases (a hazard with nothing in the narration, a copy of the engine's terrain fall applied the next turn) are both refused now. (4) **Allies** (engine/duel.ts allDownReason; features/combat conclude; turn/combatTurn.ts targetFromText; turn/resolve.ts): an opponent engaged elsewhere already never attacked or counted; now the typed target is honoured (the opponent named in the attack clause, by full name or kind noun, else the last one named before it: "turn to the droids and cut at the nearest one" targets a droid; an opponent fighting an ally is never picked), applied as targetSet before the action. When the player's own opponents are down while an opponent still fights an ally, the fight ends for the player as won with the reason opponentsDefeated ("your opponents defeated"); that opponent is never out, gets no fate choice, stays an active NPC (the ally's duel goes on in the story), and does not count for the combat XP gap or the heroic act. A minion's last wound no longer names the end reason "dying" (allOut instead). contract.md: when the narration has an ally take on an opponent, list it in engagedElsewhereRefs or send opponentEngagement. *Not changed:* the pass-2 opening described the Master engaging the Sith but sent no engagedElsewhereRefs; that is the narrator's (the prompt line above), not a code check. *Tests:* tests/engine/combatPass2Rules.test.ts (a sequence begun at -3 on 30 seeds: step 1 NOT REACHED with the reason, one exchange struck by the opponent, no aim left; a Force step at -3 still resolves; the pass-2 cards' labels and four more cases; 60 real D-204 exchanges never "pc gains ground"; the narrator's hazard in a fight does no damage; two hazards in one reply; the typed target in six phrasings; 40 engine fights end opponentsDefeated with the ally's opponent never out or targeted; through the turn a Knight's win leaves no fate, no heroic act and the Sith active).
- **D-213 Era-correct minions (Jared, combat pass 2 issue 5; built overnight).** *Problem:* in 3,653 BBY the narrator's "four war droids" became "B1 battle droid 2-5": combatStarted offers a fixed list of minion kinds with no era check, and minions were numbered by their place among all opponents. *Rule* (data/npcGen.config.ts MinionKind.eras, minionKindInEra, minionKindForYear, MINION_ERA_FALLBACK; features/combat startFight): each kind lists the calendar years it exists in ([from, to), negative = BBY; absent = every era). A kind asked for outside its years, by the narrator or by /fight, becomes its era fallback (droids a war droid, era troopers a line trooper) with the note "no B1 battle droids in 3653 BBY: war droids instead (D-213)". Minions are numbered within their kind ("War droid 1" after a named duelist). *Lore check (Docs/SWRPG lore):* the Old Republic files support war droids (the Krath "War Droid attack" of 3,997 BBY, Timeline :176; Ulic's Mandalorian allies' Basilisk war droids, Characters_04-05 :320-334), Sith troopers (Characters_08 :265, the Dark Wars), Republic troopers and elite troopers (Characters_09 :252-263, :702), Zakuul's Skytroopers (Characters_09B :342) and the Knights of Zakuul (Characters_09B :339, Force-using elites: not a minion kind, left for the M6 catalog). New kinds (all with an existing kind's numbers; design defaults): **war droid** (any era; the B1's numbers, a droid), **clone trooper** (22-19 BBY), **stormtrooper** (19 BBY on, through the Fel Empire), **Sith trooper** (3,960-3,628 BBY), **Skytrooper** (3,636-3,628 BBY, a droid) (the last four with the line trooper's numbers). Gated existing kinds: B1 battle droid and droideka 32-19 BBY (the Naboo crisis through the Clone Wars; Jared's brief said "the Clone Wars era": the Naboo years are added because the lore files have the Trade Federation's droid army there; overnight decision, narrow to 22 BBY if Jared prefers), B2 super battle droid 22-19 BBY.
  | Kind | Old Republic (to 1,000 BBY) | Rule of Two Republic (1,000-32 BBY) | Naboo to the war (32-22 BBY) | Clone Wars (22-19 BBY) | Empire and Civil War (19 BBY-4 ABY) | New Republic, Vong war, Legacy (4 ABY on) | Out of era becomes |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | B1 battle droid | no | no | yes | yes | no | no | war droid |
  | B2 super battle droid | no | no | no | yes | no | no | war droid |
  | droideka | no | no | yes | yes | no | no | war droid |
  | clone trooper | no | no | no | yes | no | no | line trooper |
  | stormtrooper | no | no | no | no | yes | yes | line trooper |
  | Sith trooper | 3,960-3,628 BBY | no | no | no | no | no | line trooper |
  | Skytrooper | 3,636-3,628 BBY | no | no | no | no | no | war droid |
  | war droid, line trooper, elite trooper, Mandalorian warrior, militia, thug, brawler, beast, Sith acolyte | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | (every era) |
  *Tests and harness:* tests/engine/eraMinions.test.ts (20 kind-year cases, the fallbacks era-neutral, droid kinds are droids for the Force rules, the note; through the turn the pass-2 request in 3,653 BBY fields "War droid 1-4" after the Sith Duelist and in 22 BBY fields B1s). The sweep and the B1 test in forceCombat.test.ts field the Clone Wars kinds at a Clone Wars date (tests/support/dates.ts CLONE_WARS_DATE) so they measure the kinds they name; every other group keeps the neutral date (the Mandalorians' beskar odds are by era). Sweep after: 10 B1 100.0 / 0.7, 6 B2 99.7 / 2.3, 4 droidekas 99.0 / 4.7, 6 troopers 99.7 / 5.7 (Knight 50, balanced; unchanged); 4 Mandalorians 97.0 / 24.7 -> 96.3 / 25.0 at the time, from D-208's first hard limit (2.0); back to 97.0 / 24.7 with the amended 4.0. The lifetime harness builds its minions directly and is unaffected.
- **D-214 Earlier saves and death (Jared, combat pass 2 issue 10; built overnight).** *Problem:* the death card said "Load an older save from the campaign menu", and the menu had only Export and Import. *Rules* (persist/saveHistory.ts; persist/storage.ts and indexedDb.ts; turn/campaign.ts loadEarlierSave; ui/app.ts): (1) Every turn commit also keeps its snapshot in the campaign's save history, in the same transaction (snapshots store, key "save:<campaign>:<branch>:<turn>", with an index "saves:<campaign>"; no database version change). Retention (SAVE_HISTORY): the last 10 saves by commit order, plus the newest save of each in-game day for the last 7 days counted back from the newest save's day; pruned snapshots are deleted, the transcript never is. (2) The campaign menu has "Load an earlier save": each kept save with its in-game date and time, place and turn ("after death" when the character was dead then); the save the game is at now is not listed. Loading one always starts a new branch (architecture §13; health.md: death ends the campaign and an older save plays on): the old branch's transcript up to that turn is copied to the new branch id, and the new branch becomes the autosave, so resuming and playing on stay on it; the old branch is untouched. (3) The death card says "Choose Load an earlier save (in the campaign menu) to play on from before this moment on a new branch, or create a new character." and its button reads "Load an earlier save". *Overnight decisions:* the history lives in the existing snapshots store (no IndexedDB upgrade); "the last 7 days" counts in-game days back from the newest save; loading the newest kept save also branches (simpler, and a branch from the same turn is harmless). *Tests:* tests/persist/saveHistory.test.ts (60 saves over 20 days keep turns 42, 45, 48 and 51-60; a re-commit replaces; storage lists 10 with date, place and turn and deletes pruned snapshots; after death a turn-3 save plays on alive on a new branch with turns 1-3, the old branch keeps 1-5, resume loads the new branch, the next commit lands there; the death card text).
- **D-215 Dev tools for pass 3 (Jared, combat pass 2; built overnight).** (1) **Test start by archetype and species** (turn/testStart.ts; ui/app.ts): Jedi Guardian, Consular, Sentinel, Sith Warrior, Sith Inquisitor, soldier, smuggler, bounty hunter, and any species; the rank tier maps to the path (Sith: acolyte, apprentice, Sith Lord for Knight and Master; a career has its one rank); training starts at 4 (Jedi), 10 (Sith) or 16 (a career) (design defaults inside PATH_START_RANGE, capped below the age); skills and equipment come from the archetype (creation's closed form, loadoutFor: a smuggler fights with a blaster pistol and has no Force abilities or CI). Jared's plan has the test Sith use Lightning, which neither Sith archetype trains (D-191's Sith core: Telekinesis, Push, Choke, Jump): a test Sith gets Force Lightning at Force Choke's starting XP (TEST_START_GRANTS; test starts only, normal creation and NPCs unchanged; overnight decision).
