Report only, the same table with the opponent breaking late: win within 1.3 points and median within 1 exchange of the plain table at every gap (gap 0: 49.8 -> 51.1%, 34 -> 33). *Lifetime (gated seed, with D-254 and D-255; old -> new, years from the start):* Anakin 41 3.74 -> 3.86, 101 20.31 -> 22.63, war-year shares 25.5 / 27.0 / 30.0% -> 27.9 / 32.0 / 33.1% (mean 27.5 -> 31.0%, band 25-35%, passes); Obi-Wan 101 59.42 -> 59.09, war shares 23.6 / 24.4 / 24.8% -> 21.0 / 25.1 / 23.3% (passes); Luke 101 33.69 -> 33.58; every gate of the three passes. **Two Average gates fail (reported, not retuned):** (1) the Y_avg table at age 40, 407 against 471 (-13.5%, gate +/-5%), caused by D-254: on this run's dice the Average takes burns or lung damage at age 33.2, adapts to scar tissue's 70% cap, and the remaining 0.4 x 0.3 = 12% cut to capacity trains less every day from then on; with burns off the capacity list the same run gives 476 (a toggle probe on the gated seed; D-255 alone does not move it). (2) Targets test 4, the highest form at 41 at 15.897 years against 16.0 +/- 0.1 (0.003 outside), from morale: its 87 skirmishes against minions end differently, the dice take another path, and with morale off it is 15.99 with no lasting injury at all. Average 101 82.08 -> 85.03. *Lost limbs per lifetime* (20 seeds each, tests/severs, npm run severs; mean (min-max), morale off -> on): Average 0 -> 0 (0-0); Obi-Wan 4.10 (2-5) -> 4.30 (4-6); Luke 3.95 (3-5) -> 4.25 (3-5); Anakin 4.30 (4-6) -> 4.30 (4-6). Lore targets: Obi-Wan 0-1, Luke about 1, Anakin about 2, the Average 0-1. Morale does not reach them: the severs come from serious saber duels against Force-using duelists (late morale), concentrated in each profile's heaviest years (Obi-Wan 35-37, Luke 44-48, Anakin 10-15). *Why:* the schedules (lifetime of fights, tests/severs/duelCounts.report) hold 32 serious duelist fights for Obi-Wan, 36 for Luke and 174 for Anakin, and a duel costs the player a limb 29% of the time at gap 0 (10.6% at -20, 18.8% at -10, 38.0% at +10; D-244: a lost weapon arm or leg ends the duel), so every profile runs out of limbs (four limbs, two hands).
- **D-257 Proposal (not adopted; for Jared): lost limbs per lifetime.** Measured with morale on, 20 seeds, no deaths in any candidate (tests/severs/candidates, candidateD; the engine reads SEVERING.at and SEVERING.chance, both at today's values, 35 and 1, which draw no extra die). Candidates (limb lost per duel at gap 0; Obi-Wan / Luke / Anakin lost limbs per life; the rolls §8 table 1): now 29.0%, 4.30 / 4.25 / 4.30, passes. A, sever at W 50 (the ceiling: above it a limb hit becomes a mortal wound, dying): 27.4%, 4.35 / 4.15 / 4.20, passes (medians within 1). B, at 50 with the limb-hit share x0.5 (torso 35 -> 62.5): 13.0%, 3.25 / 3.25 / 4.35, passes (medians 2-3 shorter: gap 0 32, gap 20 27). C, at 50 with the limb share x0.25: 6.0% per duel; lifetimes and table not run. D, a sever chance of 0.1 on a qualifying limb hit (the rest stay critical limb wounds): 3.8%, 1.35 (0-4) / 1.30 (0-4) / 4.00 (3-5), **fails the length bands**: medians 47 / 45 / 45 / 40 / 32 / 26 / 18 at gaps 0, 5, 10, 20, 30, 40, 60 against 34 / 33 / 34 / 29 / 24 / 20 / 13 (wins within 2.3 points). D at 0.05: 1.9% per duel; fails the same (gap 0 median 48); lifetimes not run. The threshold cannot do it (nearly every saber hit is above 50), and the limb-hit share only halves the per-duel rate before it moves hits to the torso. Only a sever chance reaches Obi-Wan's and Luke's targets (medians 1 and 1), and it lengthens duels by about 13 exchanges because a sever ends a duel 17% of the time (D-244); adopting it needs D-206's duel-length tunables retuned again (as D-244 did), and the lifetime war shares re-measured. Anakin stays at 4 with any of them: 174 serious saber duels at about 2% a duel still take four limbs; his "about 2" needs fewer serious duelist fights in his schedule (his war shares are tuned by fight counts, D-159) or a per-duel rate near 1%. *Recommendation:* D at 0.1 with a duel-length retune (pressure or precognition, as in D-244), measured on the D-244 grid; then Anakin's schedule trimmed toward lore (his Legends duels are a handful a year, not 25 to 50) with the war-share gate re-measured.

- **D-258 Lifetime schedules: Legends-plausible duels against Force users (Jared, 2026-11-19; fight counts only, tests/lifetime/profiles.ts).** Serious duels against Force-using duelists over a life, old -> new (Jared's targets in brackets): Obi-Wan 32 -> 13 [12-15] (4 / 5 / 4 in the war years, were 10 / 11 / 11); Luke 36 -> 20 [about 20] (3.4 a year at 44-50, was 6); Anakin 174 -> 30 [25-30] (1 a year at 10-19, was 4; 4 a year in the war, was 8; 0.15 a year after it, was 2); the Average 0 -> 4 [a handful] (one every 15 years from 25 to 80, at 34, 49, 64 and 79). *Their XP from other serious fights* (minion groups; blasters, slugthrowers and amphistaffs never sever): Obi-Wan 12 Death Watch Mandalorian squads a year in the war (3 at gap 0); Luke 6 Yuuzhan Vong warrior groups a year at 44-50 (3 at gap 0); Anakin 3 elite squads a year as a Padawan (4 at -5) and 2 Mandalorian squads a year in the war and after it. *Tuning* (the gated seed for the milestones, then the 20 seeds for the war shares, D-159 amended): Obi-Wan's Mandalorians 14 gave a war-year mean of 31.1% on s7, 10 gave 20.5%, 12 gives 27.0% (20-seed median 27.9%, range 26.5-28.9%, none out of the 20-30% band). Anakin's war share rose with fewer duels (median 34.6%, 10 seeds above 35%): fewer lost limbs and tank days leave more war days fought. Neither his war Mandalorians (2 -> 0: 35.1%) nor his war sessions with his master (11 / 10 -> 5 / 5: 33.0%; -> 0 / 0: 33.1%; restored to 11 / 10) moved it; the war's B1 battle-droid fights did: 57 -> 40 a year gave 31.6%, **57 -> 25 gives 29.4%** (range 24.0-35.2%; 2 of 20 out of band, s13 at 24.0% and s15 at 35.2%; D-159's target is at most 2). The Average's duels at gap -5 ended the duel day over the tiredness gate on the gated seed (1.74 at 49); at -15 on 7 of 20 seeds, at **-25** on 3 (worst 1.62, at 34): a long duel alone can end a day over 1.1, so the Average's handful costs the tiredness gate a few seeds (the last spread had none). *The Average's skirmishes (Jared: counts only, K stays):* 1 -> **0.95** a year puts its highest form's 41 at 15.99 y on the gated seed (gate 16.0 +/- 0.1; 15.897 after the morale rule). 0.85 gave 15.93 and 0.9 15.95, but both drift a mission into the age-20 year, where the drill-bank release it opens lifts that year's XP to 601-685 against the table's 550; 1.05 gave 16.02 with 573. *Lifetime suite* (gated seed, old Y_avg table): 36 of 36 pass; the two Average failures of D-256 are gone (tier 41 by the skirmish count; Y_avg because the gated seed's path now takes no burn: its largest deviation from the table is +3.0% at 70). *Gated-seed milestones (years from the start):* Average 41 15.99, 101 82.27; Obi-Wan 81 38.80, 101 58.99, war mean 27.0%; Anakin 41 3.71, 81 14.74, 101 20.91, war mean 28.9%; Luke 101 33.83. *Spread (20 seeds; npm run spread, 2026-11-20):* Obi-Wan and Luke pass every gate on every seed; Anakin 2 seeds out of the war-share band, nothing else; the Average fails K on s5 (as before), Y_avg on s12, s15 and s16 (was s18 only), and day-end tiredness on the profile seed, s4 and s11 (new: duel days). Medians: Average 41 15.99, 101 82.20; Obi-Wan 101 59.23; Anakin 81 14.49, 101 20.59; Luke 101 33.59. The gated seed s7 passes every gate of all four profiles.
- **D-259 Y_avg re-measured after D-254 and D-258; not adopted (the D-166 safeguard).** *Method (as D-166 and D-207):* the Average with its yearly missions (on the D-258 schedule), training XP only (combat excluded; with combat the medians differ by under 2), the median of the gated seed (s7, the fixed seed since D-159 amended) and s1 to s5. *Old -> new (change; range of the 6 runs):* 4: 582 -> 580 (-0.3%; 572-591), 8: 607 -> 608 (+0.2%; 605-620), 12: 557 -> 562 (+0.9%; 547-569), 16: 531 -> 534 (+0.6%; 527-549), 20: 550 -> 536 (-2.5%; 526-550), 24: 450 -> 447 (-0.7%; 437-459), 30: 396 -> 392 (-1.0%; 388-400), 40: 471 -> 468 (-0.6%; 463-475), 50: 448 -> 453 (+1.1%; 438-454), 60: 487 -> 487 (0.0%; 475-491), 70: 440 -> 442 (+0.5%; 434-452), 80: 436 -> 425 (-2.5%; 413-442), 90: 289 -> 312 (+8.0%; 290-323), 98: 197 -> 202 (+2.5%; 194-207). *The 4.0x clamp check* (the lifetime suite with the new table against the old, same code): with the new table the Average's Y_avg test fails (the gated seed's 290.6 at age 90 is -6.9% from 312) and Anakin moves (41 3.71 -> 3.99, 81 15.02 -> 14.67, 101 21.11 -> 20.57; war-year shares 31.7 / 36.7 / 31.9% -> 35.9 / 30.9 / 35.5%); 35 of 36 pass. With the old table all 36 pass. A test outcome changes, so the old table stays (progression.config Y_AVG unchanged); D-254 stays, as Jared decided. Only age 90 moves by more than 2.5%, and the gated seed sits at the bottom of the six-seed range there. (Measured before the last Anakin and Average schedule steps of D-258, which do not touch the Average's training loop beyond its four duels' gap.)
- **D-257 amended: lost limbs per lifetime on the D-258 schedules (2026-11-20; report only, nothing adopted).** npm run severs, 20 seeds per profile, mean (median; min-max), morale on (morale off in brackets): Obi-Wan 3.05 (3; 1-5) [3.00], Luke 3.75 (4; 2-5) [3.15], Anakin 4.25 (4; 4-6) [4.25], the Average 0.30 (0; 0-1) [0.80 at gap -15]. Against the targets (Obi-Wan 0-1, Luke about 1, Anakin about 2, the Average 0-1) only the Average is in; Obi-Wan is three times his, Luke nearly four, Anakin twice. The counts fall less than the duels did (Obi-Wan 4.3 -> 3.05 for 32 -> 13 duels): at 29% a duel, 13 even duels still cost about 3.8 limbs before the limbs run out, and the cut duels were the cheap ones as much as the costly ones. *The sever chance again, on the D-258 schedules* (measured on Obi-Wan's, Luke's and Anakin's before Anakin's last B1 step, which does not change his duels; morale on): **D, a sever chance of 0.1 on a qualifying limb hit: Obi-Wan 0.65 (1; 0-2), Luke 1.05 (1; 0-4), Anakin 1.45 (1; 0-4)**, all at or inside their targets (Anakin a little under his 2). The rolls §8 duel table fails its length bands under D (medians 47 / 45 / 45 / 40 / 32 / 26 / 18 at gaps 0, 5, 10, 20, 30, 40, 60 against 34 / 33 / 34 / 29 / 24 / 20 / 13; wins within 2.3 points; D at 0.05 the same), because a sever ends a duel 17% of the time (D-244); adopting D needs D-206's duel-length tunables retuned (as D-244 did) and the lifetime gates re-run. For comparison: A (sever at W 50) 2.65 / 3.60 / 4.10 and B (at 50, limb-hit share x0.5) 1.60 / 1.90 / 2.70, both keeping the duel table. Per duel at gap 0: now 29.0%, A 27.4%, B 13.0%, C (share x0.25) 6.0%, D 3.8%, D at 0.05 1.9%. *For Jared:* D at 0.1 with a duel-length retune reaches every target on these schedules; B keeps the duel table and gets within one limb of each.
- **D-260 Strike intent, and severing by weapon (Jared, 2026-11-20; design defaults; the 10% sever chance of D-257 not adopted).** *Intent* (combat.config STRIKE_INTENTS, INTENT_WEIGHTS, strikeIntentFor; engine/duel.ts damageFighter, intentWeights): each NPC fighter strikes to kill, to disarm or to capture. Jedi disarm and kill only in lethal stakes; Sith, soldiers, mercenaries, Mandalorians, the Yuuzhan Vong and everyone else kill; a fighter the scene or a contract sets to take the player alive (combatStarted wantAliveRefs, PendingFight.wantAlive) captures. Minions take their kind's archetype's intent. Intent replaces the striker's location weights (no aim, no -2), oriented on the defender's weapon hand: kill torso 55, head 18, weapon arm 7, other arm 5, weapon hand 2, other hand 1, legs 6 + 6; disarm torso 15, head 2, weapon arm 25, other arm 10, weapon hand 18, other hand 4, legs 13 + 13; capture torso 12, head 2, weapon arm 20, other arm 12, weapon hand 14, other hand 4, legs 18 + 18, and its hits stop at critical (W 49 at most; no killing blow). Base, for the player and test fighters: torso 35, head 10, arms 12.5 + 12.5, legs 12.5 + 12.5, hands 2.5 + 2.5. The narration block has "Intent: X strikes to kill (torso and head); ..."; dev /intent <kill|disarm|capture> [all]. The lifetime harness gives its opponents intents the same way (a Sith duelist against a Jedi profile kills; a Jedi duelist against a Sith profile disarms; sparring partners of the profile's own order; minions by kind). *Severing* (engine/damage.ts severs): a lightsaber severs a limb at W 35+ as before; a non-saber blade (the melee kind: vibroblades, amphistaffs) severs only on a critical hit with W 20+ (a severe wound or worse; design default, since a blade's wound follows the margin and stops near W 30, D-161) to the limb of an unarmored target who is not a Force user (Force Sense or resistance 5+); nothing else severs (beskar blades and the like are M8). Specs: health §5, rolls §5. *Duel tables* (rolls §8): their fighters have no intent, so they do not move; the lifetime suite's duel-table test passes, no retune. *Tests:* tests/engine/strikeIntent.test.ts.
- **D-260 measured (2026-11-20).** *Lost limbs per lifetime* (npm run severs, 20 seeds, morale on; mean (median; min-max); targets in brackets): Obi-Wan 1.90 (2; 0-5) [0-1], Luke 1.65 (2; 0-3) [about 1], Anakin 3.95 (4; 3-5) [about 2], the Average 0.10 (0; 0-1) [0-1]. On the same code with every intent at the base weights (measured once, not kept): Obi-Wan 3.35, Luke 2.90, Anakin 4.20; intent roughly halves the lost limbs of the two whose duels are against killers, and barely moves Anakin's (his 30 duels and many serious fights against Force users still cost limbs; his spars use training sabers). *Mortal wounds per life, each a Dying state the medic stabilizes* (with intent; before intent): Anakin 48.6 (34.2), Luke 8.1 (6.7), Obi-Wan 7.9 (5.4), the Average 0.8: intent moves the hits from the limbs to the torso and head. *A sever chance of 0.1 on top (measured only, not adopted):* Obi-Wan 0.30 (0), Luke 0.20 (0), Anakin 0.80 (1), the Average 0.00, with mortal wounds per life 7.4, 11.8, 53.8 and 0.9; on top of intent it overshoots Obi-Wan's and Luke's targets (0-1 and about 1) to near none, brings Anakin under his 2, and fails the duel table's length bands as measured in D-257. *Lifetime suite (gated seed):* 35 of 36; the Average's Y_avg test fails at age 40 (401 against 471, -14.9%): on its path the gated seed takes burns or lung damage (kill intent puts more of the troopers' hits on the torso; a healed torso wound can leave burns, D-254), as 4 of 20 seeds do. Gated-seed milestones: Average 41 16.00, 101 85.17; Obi-Wan 101 59.30, war-year shares 28.0 / 30.0 / 23.8%; Anakin 41 3.79, 81 14.63, 101 20.54, war-year shares 33.2 / 40.3 / 30.1% (mean 34.5%); Luke 101 33.64. *Spread (20 seeds):* Obi-Wan and Luke pass every gate on every seed; Anakin 1 seed out of the war-share band (s4 24.0%; median 31.0%); the Average fails Y_avg on 4 seeds (s5, s6, s7, s12; median worst deviation 3.6%, range 1.6-34.9%) and rank minimums on s14, and passes the tiredness gate on all 20 (amended below). *No fight-count retune:* the failing gate is the D-254 burn on some seeds' paths; a fight count reshuffles which seeds take a burn, so a retune to clear s7 would fit one seed, not the 20; for Jared.

- **D-260 final runs (2026-11-20).** npm run test:all 138 s: 1020 of 1021 (the Average's Y_avg on the gated seed, as above). npm run sweep 33 s: 9840 fights, no broken behaviour; the D-158 rows within 0.5 points of the run before (the sweep's opponents strike to kill like before; only hit locations moved). npm run spread 937 s (80 lifetimes): the same gate results as above (Anakin s4 war share; the Average Y_avg s5, s6, s7, s12 and rank minimums s14; Obi-Wan and Luke clean).
- **D-262 Capture by subduing replaces D-260's capture weights (Jared, 2026-11-20; design defaults).** Legends: a Jedi is captured by being subdued, not out-fenced. *Means* (combat.config SUBDUE, subdueFor; set on every NPC fighter from its loadout, Fighter.subdue): Force Choke to unconsciousness (forceChoke 10+) or low-power Force lightning (forceLightning 10+) for a Force user; nets, whipcords and binders for Mandalorians and bounty hunters (thugs included); a stun baton, an electrostaff, a force pike or a shock weapon in melee (by the weapon's name); a blaster pistol or rifle on stun. *Fallback:* a fighter with none of these (a lightsaber without Choke or lightning, an amphistaff, a heavy repeater, a slugthrower) keeps its archetype's kill or disarm intent (strikeIntentFor takes the means; the fight notes say so). *Rule* (engine/duel.ts subdueFighter, capturedNow): a capturing fighter's landed strike is Vitality only, D = the means' base x hit quality - armor (a stun bolt its blaster's base 15 / 20, stun melee 12, a snare 14, a choke 18, low-power lightning 18; a snare and a choke go around armor): no location, no wound, no sever, no killing blow. The fight ends in capture when the player is Spent while a capturing opponent is engaged: end reason captured, the player out with no new wound, the fate captured with no roll. A stun bolt deflected back stuns its shooter. No capture in a spar. *In play:* combatStarted wantAliveRefs (named) and a minion group's wantAlive (new, for a squad sent to take the player alive); dev /fight ... alive; /intent capture only for an opponent with a means. The narration block reads "X fights to capture: subdues with blaster bolts on stun (no wounds; takes Y captive when Spent)"; the combat-narration prompt narrates a capture, never an injury, from those hits. INTENT_WEIGHTS.capture and the W 49 cap are gone. Specs: rolls §5, health §3, §4, §5. Tests: tests/engine/strikeIntent.test.ts.
- **D-263 Sith tactical withdrawal (Jared, 2026-11-20; design default 30%).** Separate from panic morale (D-256). A named Sith duelist (sithWarrior, sithInquisitor: combat.config TACTICAL_WITHDRAWAL, withdrawChanceFor; Fighter.withdraw) who is losing (a severe or worse wound or a sever this exchange, Spent this exchange, or the track swinging to 4 against it) withdraws with its archetype's chance: it breaks off and escapes, out of the fight (yielded withdrew), no fate to choose; the last one out ends the fight won, "the opponents broke". Legends: Dooku, Ventress and Grievous all did. Real fights only; the dice continue the duel's stream. The lifetime harness gives its Sith duelists the same chance. Two game tests that check morale and a fate choice against a dev Sith duelist set its chance to 0 (a withdrawn Sith leaves no fate by design).
- **D-264 The lifetime harness plays as a sensible Jedi: it disengages from a losing fight (Jared, 2026-11-20; harness input, design defaults).** tests/support/lifetime.ts PLAYER_WITHDRAWAL, wantsToWithdraw, fightWithWithdrawal: a real fight runs beat by beat; at a beat the profile tries the game's disengage contest (now shared: engine/duel.ts disengageContest, d20 + Agility / 10 (+ position) against d20 + the opponent's skill / 10) when it has a severe or worse wound or a sever from this fight, when it is near Spent (Vitality 20% of max or less) while losing on the track (advantage -2 or worse), or when the current opponent's effective level is 10 or more above its own. A win ends the fight as fled; a loss fights on to the next beat. With the rule off the fight is the one run to its end (tested). Legends: Obi-Wan pulling back from Grievous.
- **D-260 amended (Jared, 2026-11-20):** the 0.1 sever chance is not adopted (it overshoots to almost no limbs); blade severing at W 20 is accepted.
- **D-262 to D-264 measured (2026-11-20; npm run severs, 20 seeds, morale on, after the D-265 retune; Jared's targets in brackets).** *Lost limbs per lifetime, mean (median; min-max):* Obi-Wan 0.40 (0; 0-1) [0-1], Luke 0.90 (1; 0-2) [about 1], Anakin 1.15 (1; 0-3) [about 2], the Average 0.00 (0) [0-1]: all four in their targets (D-260: 1.90 / 1.65 / 3.95 / 0.10). *Mortal wounds per life, each a Dying state the medic stabilizes, mean (median):* Obi-Wan 2.35 (3) [1-3], in; Luke 5.75 (6) [2-5], just above; Anakin 14.05 (14.5) [2-5], well above; the Average 0.35 (0) [0-1], in (D-260: 7.9 / 8.1 / 48.6 / 0.8). Anakin's are his war and later fights against killers (kill intent puts hits on the torso and head) over about 2,000 real fights a life; not tuned, for Jared. *Disengages per life (D-264):* Obi-Wan 45.6, Luke 40.0, Anakin 437.2, the Average 2.05; on anakin-s9 most are after the war (about 230 of 330) against trooper squads and Mandalorians, mostly "clearly stronger": the rule compares effective levels, and after 22 he fights worn down by his 300 group spars a year, so his effective level falls 10+ under opponents scripted 20 below him. Reported, not changed. *Sith withdrawals per life (D-263):* Obi-Wan 2.85, Luke 7.05, Anakin 4.85, the Average 1.65. *Before the retune, with a 0.1 sever chance on top (the run before D-265; not adopted):* lost limbs Obi-Wan 0.15, Luke 0.15, Anakin 0.10, the Average 0.05. *D-158 rows* (npm run sweep, Knight at 50, flat floor, 300 fights each): unchanged with no capture (named and minion opponents still kill unless the scene wants the player alive). New capture rows, the same groups sent to take him alive (same seeds; win / wounded / exchanges / captured): 10 B1 100.0 / 0.0 / 17.6 / 0; 10 militia 100.0 / 0.0 / 14.8 / 0; 6 troopers 100.0 / 0.0 / 15.4 / 0; 6 elites 97.7 / 0.0 / 22.7 / 2.3%; 4 Mandalorians 96.7 / 0.0 / 40.0 / 3.3%; against kill 99.3 / 6.3, 96.7 / 22.3 and 97.3 / 17.0 for troopers, elites and Mandalorians: capture never wounds, and a lost fight is a capture. B2s and droidekas have no subduing means (heavy repeaters), so their capture rows equal the kill rows (99.7 / 1.7 and 99.0 / 4.7).

- **D-265 Retune after D-264: Anakin's war B1 groups 25 -> 80 a year (Jared, 2026-11-20; fight counts only, 20 seeds).** *Problem:* after the changes Anakin's war-year mean share fell to a median of 22.0% (19.4-24.0%), all 20 seeds under the 25-35% band. On anakin-s9 his own withdrawals spare him severe wounds and bacta days: training XP rose (1,160 -> 1,358 in the first war year) while combat XP fell (360 -> 288). *Search* (a throwaway 20-seed war-years probe, about 50 s a run): + 10 B2 groups a year 24.7% (14 seeds out); + 4 elite squads 21.8% (all out); B1 groups 50 25.4 / 26.4% (3 out); 65 28.0% (1 out); 80 30.5%, 28.5-34.2%, none out. More B1 groups past that saturate (the daily combat cap). 240 droid-army fights over the three war years fit Legends. *Final spread (20 seeds):* Anakin, Obi-Wan and Luke pass every gate on every seed; Anakin war-year mean median 30.5% (s9 29.1%), Obi-Wan 23.6% (22.1-24.9%; s9 23.6%). The Average fails Y_avg on 5 seeds (s2, s5, s7, s10, s12; median worst deviation 3.8%, max 23.0%; the D-254 burn paths, D-260) and rank minimums on s14; no fight-count change fixes a seed's burn path, so none was made. *Milestones, medians (s9):* Average 4.18 / 15.99 / 33.44 / 55.13 / 82.10 (4.21 / 16.05 / 33.57 / 55.29 / 82.27); Anakin 1.28 / 3.71 / 7.54 / 13.86 / 19.36 (1.28 / 3.73 / 7.55 / 13.79 / 19.16); Obi-Wan 3.25 / 12.46 / 23.60 / 38.97 / 59.14 (3.25 / 12.52 / 23.60 / 39.03 / 59.18); Luke 1.89 / 6.06 / 12.54 / 21.47 / 33.51 (1.92 / 6.00 / 12.50 / 21.48 / 33.61). Lifetime suite on s9: 36 of 36.

- **D-267 Mortal wounds diagnosed; four candidate rules measured, none adopted (Jared, 2026-11-20).** Lost limbs are accepted where D-262 to D-265 left them. *Diagnosis* (npm run mortals: 20 seeds per profile; each mortal hit is logged with the attacker's kind, weapon and Force use, the strike intent, the stakes, the defender's meters at the fight's start and at the hit, age and rank). Mortal wounds per life, mean (median), against the targets: Obi-Wan 2.35 (3), target 1-3; Luke 5.75 (6), 2-6; Anakin 14.05 (14.5), 3-6; the Average 0.35 (0), 0-1. *Top sources:* Anakin's are mostly blaster troopers in the skirmishes after age 22 (6.90 a life, 49%), then Force-user duelists (3.65), B2 heavies (1.85) and elite troopers (1.65). They come as a knight (72%), in his teens and 20s (4.0 and 3.25 a decade; 1 to 1.75 later), physically worn (at the hit 46% of the time at 1.0 to 1.5x capacity, 36% at 0.7 to 1.0; at the fight's start only 11% over capacity), as critical-quality hits (88%) to the torso (65%) or head (17%). The non-saber killing margins sit at 20 to 34, mostly 24 to 29. Obi-Wan's come mostly from duelists (66%), 91% in his 30s (the war). Luke's come from duelists (53%) and elite troopers (35%), fresh (84% at a physical meter of 0.4 or less), 88% in his 40s. Every mortal hit came from an opponent striking to kill. The Force and mental meters at the hit were low (mostly 0.4 or less) for every profile. *Candidates* (tests/support/candidates.ts, CANDIDATE=...; all off by default): (a) danger sense (precognitive defense, the same power) gets a second chance when it fails to avoid a mortal hit: min(0.6, Force Sense / 150), halved against a Force-user attacker, at PRECOG.load (combat.config PRECOG_SOFTEN); (b30) a non-saber hit is mortal only at a margin of 30 or more (health.config NONSABER_MORTAL.minMargin); (c) the harness starts no optional fight (missions, group spars) while a meter is past capacity, but war fights still happen (lifetime.ts READINESS); (d) the "clearly stronger" disengage rule compares the opponent's level with the profile's level without fatigue (PLAYER_WITHDRAWAL.strongerIgnoresFatigue).
- **D-267 measurements (20 seeds, 80 lifetimes a run).** Mortal wounds per life, mean, for Obi-Wan / Luke / Anakin / the Average; lost limbs in the same order; Anakin's disengages:
  - baseline 2.35 / 5.75 / 14.05 / 0.35; lost limbs 0.40 / 0.90 / 1.15 / 0; 437.2 disengages
  - a: 2.10 / 4.80 / 10.00 / 0.35; lost limbs 0.55 / 1.20 / 1.10 / 0.05; 425.8
  - b30: 1.60 / 3.60 / 5.45 / 0.20; lost limbs 0.35 / 1.40 / 1.05 / 0; 404.4
  - c: 2.35 / 5.75 / 12.70 / 0.35; lost limbs 0.40 / 0.90 / 0.85 / 0; 327.1 (81.8 optional fights skipped a life, all Anakin's)
  - d: 2.55 / 5.40 / 19.45 / 0.35; lost limbs 0.65 / 1.00 / 1.95 / 0; 353.7
  - a+b30: 1.45 / 3.05 / 5.30 / 0.15; lost limbs 0.55 / 1.15 / 0.90 / 0.05; 419.6
  - b30+c: 1.60 / 3.60 / 4.65 / 0.20; lost limbs 0.35 / 1.40 / 1.05 / 0; 345.7 (86.1 skipped)

  *Reading the results:* b30 alone puts every profile inside its target. It removes almost all of Anakin's trooper and B2 killing blows: under b30 his are duelists 3.55, troopers 1.75 and elites 0.15. (a) mostly overlaps with b, since its full chance applies only against non-Force attackers, whose hits b already caps; added to b30 it moves Anakin only from 5.45 to 5.30. (c) skips about 82 of Anakin's post-war fights, which lowers his disengages, but his mortals barely change because he starts few fights over capacity. (d) lowers disengages by 83 (437 -> 354): a worn Anakin no longer counts troopers as "clearly stronger", so he stays in those fights and takes more mortal wounds (14 -> 19.5) and lost limbs (1.15 -> 1.95). *D-158 rows* (the Knight at 50): every candidate is within 0.7 points of win and 1.0 point of mortal on every row; wounded is unchanged. *Rolls §8 tables:* the duel table and test 3 are unchanged under b, c and d; under (a) they move by at most 0.7 points, and every row stays on target. *Gates:* Obi-Wan, Luke and Anakin pass on every seed under every candidate (Anakin's war mean is 29.7 to 30.9%). The Average fails Y_avg on s2, s5, s7, s10 and s12 at baseline and under b, c and d. It fails on three more seeds (s3, s11, s14) under (a) and two more (s3, s11) under a+b30. Rank minimums fail on s14 at baseline, a, c and d; they pass under b30. The Average's Y_avg failures follow its wounds: s2, s7 and s12 are the seeds with a baseline mortal wound. A softened blow still means a critical wound with a long recovery, and the extra die sends those lives down a different path. *Recommendation:* adopt b30 (the margin is a design default that can be tuned), and optionally c as harness realism (it is harmless: Anakin 4.65). Do not adopt d (it raises mortal wounds). Leave (a) unadopted for now: on top of b it adds almost nothing and shakes the Average's gates. Y_avg (D-266): the old table is kept.
- **Recorded for the end-of-production traits list (not built):** Blink, a latent precognition for characters who are not Force-sensitive (Legends: Nova Stihl), as a small version of precognitive defense with no Force load (architecture.md, Character traits).
- **Runs (2026-11-20, D-267):** npm run mortals (80 lifetimes plus the D-158 and duel-table reports): baseline 961 s; a 999 s; b30 1045 s; c 973 s; d 945 s; a+b30 975 s; b30+c 1031 s.
  npm run test:all 175 s (1038 of 1038); npm test 13 s (1002 of 1002).
- **D-267 decided (Jared, 2026-11-20).** *Adopted:* (b30) the non-saber mortal band: a non-saber hit is mortal only at a rounded strike margin of 30 or more (health.config NONSABER_MORTAL.minMargin 30, tunable; health.md §4 amended). (c) Fight readiness, as harness realism: the lifetime harness starts no optional fight while a meter is past capacity, but war fights still happen (tests/support/lifetime.ts READINESS on). *Not adopted:* (a) and (d); their code is removed (kept in commit 7cf3a63). (a), precognition softening killing blows, is recorded for M6 with the precognition redesign, with these measurements (architecture §15). npm run mortals keeps two switches: b<margin> and noready ("now" is the adopted rules). *Recorded under architecture §15 (M8), not built:* prepared Jedi hunters (a prepared ambush, chosen ground, anti-Jedi gear; preparation costs time and resources in the story). With b30, preparation is how a non-Force fighter realistically reaches a killing blow on a Jedi. The lore file Docs/SWRPG lore/SW_Lightsaber_Combat_Marks_Zones_Techniques.md (Jared's, referenced by D-187) stays committed.
- **D-159 amended (Jared, 2026-11-20): the Average's Y_avg failures across the spread are expected.** Across the 20 seeds the Average fails Y_avg within +/-5% on about 5 seeds (s2, s5, s7, s10, s12 at D-267). Those are the spread of rare long recoveries: every failing seed whose Y_avg deviation is large (s2, s7, s12) has a mortal wound, and its recovery months shift training XP for the years around it. No gate change; the gate is still checked on the gated seed (s9).
- **Runs after D-267's adoption (2026-11-20):** npm run test:all 156 s (1038 of 1038). npm run sweep 38 s: 9840 fights, no broken behaviour; the D-158 rows identical to the b30 measurement (within 0.7 points of win and 1.0 point of mortal of the run before; wounded unchanged). npm run spread 1046 s (80 lifetimes): Obi-Wan, Luke and Anakin pass every gate on all 20 seeds (Anakin's war-share median 30.3%); the Average fails Y_avg on s2, s5, s7, s10 and s12 (the expected spread, D-159 amended; median worst deviation 3.8%) and now passes rank minimums on all 20. Gated seed s9: Obi-Wan 101 at 59.01 y, Luke 33.43, Anakin 19.02, the Average 41 at 16.05 and 101 at 82.27.
- **D-268 Sith tactical withdrawal narrowed (Jared, 2026-11-20; combat pass 5 issue 1).** D-263's triggers ended 40 of 58 of the player's won duels against dev Sith duelists in pass 5, most with the Sith unhurt, twice at exchange 2. *Rule now* (engine/duel.ts, combat.config TACTICAL_WITHDRAWAL): a named Sith duelist checks to withdraw when it takes a severe or worse wound or a sever, becomes Spent, or the track swings to 4 against it **while its Vitality is at or below 50%** (vitalityAtMost 0.5). One check per exchange with a trigger, **at most two a fight** (maxChecks 2; Fighter.withdrawChecks). An unwounded Sith above half its Vitality never withdraws. The chance stays 30% (sithWarrior, sithInquisitor). `/archetype` now replaces the fighter's withdrawal with the new archetype's, as it does morale (pass 5 issue 8). *Measured* (npm run sweep, the new report case "D-268 ...": a Knight test start at 30, form 53.6, 20 dev Sith duelists a gap, 15 serious duels each): won duels ending in withdrawal, gap -10 / 0 / +10: **19.2% / 24.5% / 17.1%, 20.6% overall** (target 20-30%); the old rule on the same fights: 58.2% / 69.1% / 71.3%, 65.4%. The Knight's wins over the 900 duels fall from 601 to 446 (Sith who stay can win), and his won duels last 31-41 exchanges instead of 19-23. *Tests:* tests/engine/strikeIntent.test.ts (only when wounded or at half Vitality; at most two checks), tests/engine/morale.test.ts (/archetype).
- **D-269 Natural-1 fumbles (Jared, 2026-11-20; combat pass 5 issue 2).** The block tells a natural 1 as that fighter's mistake: "X makes a mistake (fumble): an opening for Y; tell it as a misstep, a stumble or an overextension by X (no fall, no slip on the ground)" (turn/combatFacts.ts fumbleText). The slip rule (rule 9) lets a bare misstep, stumble or overextend word stand for a fighter whose natural 1 is in the passage (narrationCheck FUMBLE_WORD, SlipCauses.fumble); a slip on something ("stumbles over the uneven terrain", "slips on the scorched earth", "trips over a loose stone", "footwork falters") still fires, and the other fighter's misstep still fires. The check reads the passage, not the single exchange: the narration's sentences are not tied to exchanges. The prompt's slip sentence names the fumble. *Fixtures:* tests/turn/combatPass5.test.ts (the three pass-5 sentences pass; the pass-1 to pass-3 slips still fire with a natural 1 in the passage).
- **D-270 The Yuuzhan Vong warrior trains from youth (combat pass 5 issue 4; design default).** *Diagnosed through the whole path:* the narrator's npcIntroduced (no age: the soldier's default 28) and combatStarted, generation, the fight, the XP. Generation drew the training start age from the archetype's startRange [16, 30], the soldier's, capped at his age: a 28-year-old warrior often started the year he was met (melee 0.6 to 3.4 on four seeds), so he fought at "+0.4 level" and his gap to the Knight gave 0 combat XP. The fight read his generated melee correctly and the XP followed the gap: the start age was the one defect. *Rule:* data/archetypes.ts yuuzhanVongWarrior startAge 14, startRange [12, 17] (the caste trains for war from youth; the lore files give no age). *Effect:* the narrator's 28-year-old warrior has melee 36 to 47 and gives 4.4 to 6.9 combat XP to the Knight at 53.6 (seeds a to d); a 20-year-old 18 to 24, a 35-year-old 45 to 62. Minions are unchanged (their start age is the kind's, 17). *Test:* tests/turn/vongPipeline.test.ts, through the pipeline with the narrator's replies as fixtures (five campaigns): melee above 25, the fighter at its generated level with the amphistaff, combat XP on a win; it fails on 4 of 5 with the old range.
- **D-271 Long passages told in full (Jared, 2026-11-20; combat pass 5 issue 5).** From 10 exchanges (combat.config NARRATION_LENGTH), the block's "Key events" line becomes **Highlights**: the wounding hits and severs, the subduing hits (stun, nets, binders, Choke), every fighter leaving the fight (cut down, shot down, out, morale break, withdrawal, Spent and taken), the turning point (the first landed hit of the winner after the loser's last), and the deciding moment, in order, at most 12 (the deciding ones kept first). A **Length** line: about one paragraph per 15 exchanges, at least 1, at most 4, "Tell the fight itself first, as it happened, and only then its end and the aftermath". combat-narration.md asks for the fight's arc before the aftermath, most of all when it ends in capture, defeat or death. A stun hit is told with no place on the body (pass 5 issue 14: "squarely in the chest"). *Fixture:* the pass-5 capture fight (51 exchanges, 4 line troopers, Rell Tarn taken) in tests/turn/combatPass5.test.ts.
- **D-272 Pass-5 fixes (2026-11-20).** (issue 3) "Load an earlier save" reads the list again each time the campaign menu opens (ui/earlierSaves.ts; the last list shows while it loads); (6) the block sends "The fight goes on" only for a fight still going; (7) the code-written summary keeps morale breaks, withdrawals and a capture on its end line, and the narration check's "still contradicting" log line quotes the sentence; (9) the exchange a fighter breaks off on is headed by its margin, not the track reset; (10) a hit on a prosthetic reads "right arm (prosthetic): no wound" on the card and "the prosthetic limb takes it" in the block (Hit.prosthetic); (11) rule 10 repairs "his stamina taxed", "tests your own endurance" and "are forced outpaced"; (12) the narrator's own choice that starts a pending fight is the first beat, not "not understood", and a fighter outside the Force is named in the block with no Force choices against them; (13) a chosen fate is stated in RESOLVED as the act, here and now ("fate act" note); (15) the prompt: lightsabers do not clang like metal, and bulkheads and deckplates belong to ships; (16) `/intent capture` on a fighter with no means says only that nothing changed. Left as they are, with reasons, in docs-v2/testing/m5-balance-table.md's notes. *Tests:* tests/turn/combatPass5.test.ts, tests/ui/earlierSaves.test.ts.
- **D-268's effect on the lifetime gates (2026-11-20; reported, not retuned).** npm run test:all 187 s: 1066 of 1067; **the Average's Y_avg gate fails on the gated seed s9 at age 90: 305.1 against the table's 289 (+5.6%, band +/-5%)**. With the old withdrawal rule restored for one run (vitalityAtMost 1, maxChecks 999), tests/lifetime/average.test.ts passes 8 of 8 (67 s), so the narrowed rule is the cause: the Average's few serious duels against Sith a life (D-258) now run to the end more often. Options for Jared: (a) re-measure the Y_avg table on the new rule (a progression-number change); (b) accept it under D-159's amendment (the Average's Y_avg spread is expected) by moving the gated check to the 20-seed median; (c) leave the lifetime harness's Sith duelists on D-263's rule (the harness models the Average's opponents, not the game). npm run sweep 41 s: 9840 fights, no broken behaviour, every D-158 row identical (no Sith in them).
- **npm run spread after D-268 to D-272 (2026-11-20, 1,252 s, 80 lifetimes).** Obi-Wan, Luke and Anakin pass every gate on all 20 seeds. The Average fails Y_avg within +/-5% on 9 of 20 (average, s2, s3, s5, s7, s9, s10, s12, s15; at D-267 5 of 20), median worst deviation 4.4% (3.8% at D-267); every other gate of his passes on all 20. Gated seed s9 milestones: Obi-Wan 101 at 59.20 y, Luke 33.54, Anakin 18.43, the Average 41 at 16.05 and 101 at 82.42. The Y_avg options are in the entry above; waiting for Jared.
- **D-159 amended (Jared, 2026-11-20; option (b) of D-268's gate entry): the Y_avg calibration gate is judged on the 20-seed median.** npm run spread checks it: per Y_AVG age, the median over the Average's 20 seeds of (that year's main-form XP) / (the table's) must be within +/-5% (tests/spread/spread.ts yAvgMedianGate, Y_AVG_MEDIAN_BAND; the teardown prints "[spread-gate] ... PASS / FAIL" and a FAIL fails the run). The per-seed deviation stays a reported value (yAvgMaxDeviation), not a gate. In npm run test:all the gated seed keeps a sanity check at +/-10% (tests/lifetime/average.test.ts). Why: one seed's long recovery moves its own years past 5% (D-159 amended earlier the same day), and D-268's Sith who stay to fight made more of them; the table is a calibration of the typical life, which the median measures. *Test:* tests/engine/yAvgMedianGate.test.ts.
- **D-273 Cybernetic arm and hand adaptation (Jared, 2026-11-20; M5 balance table row 6).** *Cause:* while a severed arm or hand heals, the routine trains no forms (the injured skill), so only strength hours reached it: 1% at day 10, 3% at 30, 7% at 60 (Knight at 30, default routine), against the 30-40 day reference. *Rule:* health.config ARM_OTHER_SHARE: every other practice hour (Agility, Endurance, the Force skills, meditation) counts toward a lost arm or hand at this share; its forms and strength count in full as before; the leg is unchanged (LEG_FORM_SHARE). *Tuned (the share only, as in D-230):* 1: 21% at day 10, 48% at 20, 72% at 30, 98% at 40, 100% on day 41; 1.15 gives the same (99% at 40), since the 3%-a-day cap and the weekly rest day bind, so the share cannot bring it under 40 days. Measured with daily steps or a montage resumed after its story-event stops (a single /skip montage stops at "a mission is offered" or "the character is summoned"). *Row 13 checked* (Knight at 30, 200 duels each against dev duelists 10 below, lost): a whole Knight 28.0%; an arm alone at day 40 (98%) 27.5%; a leg and an arm at day 40 (leg 17%, arm 89%) 38.5%, before 68.5% (arm 2%); at day 60 (leg 27%, arm 100%) 41.0%, before 71.0%; a leg alone at day 40 (64%) 28.5%. What remains is the leg when both limbs are lost: the arm wound blocks form drills, and the leg adapts mostly from forms (D-230). Reported, not changed. *Test:* tests/engine/armAdaptation.test.ts.
- **Row 19 (2026-11-20): full Vitality after a stabilized mortal wound is the rule, not a bug.** A clean or critical saber hit sends all of D to the wound (BYPASS.lightsaber 1), so a mortal wound can leave Vitality full, and stabilizing only marks the mortal wound stabilized. health.md §3 amended (it was silent): wounds do not lower Vitality, and Dying, stabilizing and healing leave it as it is; a wound's weakness is its wound penalty. *Test:* tests/engine/stabilizedVitality.test.ts.
- **D-274 Cover on both sides on the roll card (M5 balance table row 4).** When both rolls of an exchange carry the cover bonus (D-243: both shooters on a build-up exchange), the card adds "cover on both sides: the two bonuses cancel" (ui/duelPanel.ts coverNote). *Test:* tests/engine/armAdaptation.test.ts.
- **Runs after D-273, D-274 and the D-159 amendment (2026-11-20).** npm run test:all 212 s: 1074 of 1074 (the gated seed's Y_avg sanity check at +/-10% passes). npm run sweep 46 s: 9840 fights, no broken behaviour; the D-158 rows as before; the new 3,653 BBY smuggler row 67.3% won (row 14); the D-268 withdrawal share 20.6%. npm run spread 1,503 s (80 lifetimes): **the Y_avg median gate passes, worst 2.8% at age 90**; every per-seed gate of every profile passes on all 20 seeds. Gated seed s9: Obi-Wan 101 at 59.20 y, Luke 33.48, Anakin 18.43, the Average 41 at 16.05 and 101 at 82.42 (its own Y_avg deviation 5.6%, reported).
- **Jared's calls on the last two open points (2026-11-20).** *Row 19 accepted:* a clean saber hit that drops the character to Dying without touching Vitality is a fair reading of how lightsabers work, and the status label (Dying) is what the player sees first (health §3 as amended). *A leg and an arm lost together adapting slowly accepted:* losing two limbs at once should mean a slower recovery (D-273's row-13 check: leg 17% at day 40 when the arm is lost too); recorded for M8, where the prosthetic rules for the other limbs can revisit it (architecture §15).
- **M5 closed (Jared, 2026-11-20).** Every gate passes on all three runs at 82c11b9 (test:all 1074/1074, sweep with no issues, spread with every gate on all 80 lifetimes and the median Y_avg gate at 2.8%).
- **D-268 amended: the Sith presses for the win (Jared, 2026-11-20; M5 final check issue 5).** A Sith withdrew from a Knight at 1 / 61 Vitality with two severe wounds; one more hit would have won it the duel. No withdrawal check now while the player is Spent, Dying, or at or below TACTICAL_WITHDRAWAL.pressWhenFoeAtMost (0.15) of max Vitality: the Sith fights on instead. The D-268 triggers and the two-check cap are unchanged. *Test:* tests/engine/strikeIntent.test.ts (200 seeds from full and from 10% Vitality: no withdrawal ever leaves the player at or below 15%, Spent or Dying, and none from the 10% start).
- **D-275 M5 cleanup from the final check (Jared, 2026-11-20; docs-v2/testing/m5-final-check.md issues 1 to 4, 6 to 8).** *Long-fight length (issue 1):* the response schema has no length cap (narrative is z.string().min(1)) and no reply was truncated (436 to 552 output tokens against a 2,024 budget), but two things kept the narration to one paragraph. The code reword (repairWording, which every long fight of the check went through) split on newlines and joined with spaces, so it erased the paragraph breaks; it now keeps paragraphs, as does the trimmed fallback. And the moment pacing's "Usually 1 to 3 short paragraphs" competed with the Length line. The Length line now asks for the paragraph count, the blank lines and 80 to 130 words each, says it overrides the pacing, and says "Not one paragraph"; pacing-moment.md defers to it; combat-narration.md calls it a requirement. A long passage's prose budget is its asked paragraphs at NARRATION_LENGTH.tokensPerParagraph (260), when above the pacing cap (four paragraphs: 1,040 instead of a moment's 700). *Engine words (issue 3):* the block says "no wound (winded)" instead of "no wound, stamina only"; "stamina" anywhere in prose is flagged and reworded in code ("strength", after the phrase swaps); "outpace" in any form is flagged and reworded ("outmatch") unless the passage's block gives "was outpaced" as a miss reason. *Reword repetition (issue 4):* each severity has three words (minor light / shallow / slight; moderate deep / painful / nasty; severe grievous / savage / brutal; critical terrible / devastating / ruinous; mortal deadly / fatal / killing), and the repair takes the first one not yet in the passage; a sentence that already says "unable to" drops the card's "out of the fight" clause instead of saying "unable" twice. *Force choices against opponents outside the Force (issue 2):* from the turn that starts the fight (a pending combatStarted, D-29, or an active fight), choices with a Force push, choke, lightning or mind trick against a Force-invisible opponent (a named Yuuzhan Vong, a Vong minion group) are dropped and logged; a Force jump, a thrown object and a saber throw stay; an emptied list gets "Meet the attack with your weapon" (fightChoices.withoutForceOnImmune). *Withdrawal card (issue 7):* an exchange on which a fighter withdraws, surrenders or flees reads "Duelist 1 withdraws · margin +13.3" with no track. *Wording (issue 8):* the Highlights line says "only Joren Tal lands anything" ("the opponents land" for several, "the opponent lands" for one); "steel" in a passage where both sides carry lightsabers is flagged and reworded ("energy"), except "steel yourself" and "of steel". *Dev loop (issue 6):* the pass-5 handoff's loop uses /heal all between duels. *Tests:* tests/turn/m5FinalCheck.test.ts; the earlier pass tests that asserted the old wording are updated.
- **Runs after the D-268 amendment and D-275 (2026-11-20).** npm run test:all 194 s wall (192.5 s of tests): 1089 of 1089, lifetime tests included. npm run sweep 45 s wall (9,840 fights in 40.5 s): no broken behaviour; the D-268 withdrawal share 20.0% of won duels (88 of 440; was 20.6%).
- **M5 complete (2026-11-20).** The final check's issues are fixed (D-268 amended, D-275); every gate passes.
- **D-276 Force lore renames resolve as aliases (2026-11-20).** The Force lore file update of 2026-11-20 (about 55 new entries) renamed nine entries: ForceCamouflage → Force Cloak, ForceStrength → Enhance Attribute, ForceEnhancedReflexes → Force Speed, PrecognitiveReflexes → Precognition, AingTiiFightingSight → Fighting-sight, ForceListening → Theran Force-listening, ForceRage → Force Enrage, TransferEssence → Essence Transfer, Endure → Crucitorn. *Change:* force-catalog.md's required checks now list each old key and old display name as an alias of its new entry. Unknown names stay rejected (D-07); no near spellings are added. *No code change:* the code has no Force-ability alias map yet (skills.ts has no aliases, D-07; the full catalog is M6). None of the old keys is a skill id or an intent value, and Force Speed was already `forceSpeed`. The old names that remain are spec text (05-force-abilities.md legacy audit, lore-checks.md, phase1-answers Q27) and the `unmapped` strings in src/data/canon/era09GreatGalacticWar.ts, which quote the character lore file and resolve through this table when M6 maps them. *Lore build:* scripts/build-lore.ts does not read the Force file (parse.ts fileKey accepts only character files and the timeline; the Force file is indexed in M6), so the new **Source:**, **Tradition:** and "> **Added from...**" lines do not reach it; the build runs and the index is unchanged. formMatchups.ts's line citations (:176-202) still point at the form section. *Flag:* Crucitorn's note calls Endure "the canon technique"; Endure is a Disney-era name. It is kept only as an input alias; Crucitorn (Legends) is the entry.
- **D-276 amended (2026-11-20): more legacy aliases and dropped names.** force-catalog.md's alias table adds TelekineticBarrage → Ballistakinesis, ForceEndurance → Remove Fatigue, EnvironmentalForceSense → Force Sense, ForceInsight → Force Sense, BattleForesight → Battle Precognition and ForceSubjugate → Mind Control (each target is an entry in the lore file). Dropped and rejected as unknown names, with no alias: ResistAging (now a passive effect of the age system, not an ability), ForceComprehension, AuraOfSerenity, KyberResonance and FieryEnergy. Exact matching only, no near spellings (D-07, D-13). Still no code change: none of these names is a skill id or intent value. The `unmapped` strings "Force Endurance" and "Battle Foresight" in src/data/canon/era09GreatGalacticWar.ts resolve through the table when M6 maps them.
- **D-277 Character lore and canon data use the current Force ability names (2026-11-20).** The old names in Docs/SWRPG lore/ (every file except the Force file) and src/data/canon/ are replaced by the current display names from the Force lore file, using only the D-276 alias table: Force Strength → Enhance Attribute, Force Endurance → Remove Fatigue, Precognitive Reflexes → Precognition, Battle Foresight → Battle Precognition, Force Rage → Force Enrage. 123 replacements in 17 character files and era09GreatGalacticWar.ts (`unmapped` strings), all in ability lists (game-stats lines, bench-table ability cells, canon `unmapped`). Matching is case-sensitive, so prose is untouched: "Force strength" meaning raw power (09B :76, :127; 16 :173) and "his will to endure" (08 :125) are not ability names. No dropped name (ResistAging, ForceComprehension, AuraOfSerenity, KyberResonance, FieryEnergy) appears in scope. *Held for Jared:* "Force Strength/Speed 92%" (12 :96, a combined entry); "Environmental Force Sense 82%" as a sub-entry of Force Sense 86 (03 :254; the mapping would make Force Sense its own sub-ability); "Force Body 88 (Strength 90%)" (14 :282). Names not in the catalog or the alias table, left unchanged: Sith Sorcery (02, 03, 09B, 14, 18), Mind Domination (14, 15, 18; legacy name of Dominate Mind), Battle-meld (15, 16, 17; the lore file's Force Meld lists it as an alias, the D-276 table does not), Multipresence and Galactic Force-psychosis (17), Beast-transformation and Beast-form (12), Force Healing / Resurrection (18 :191). *Checks:* scripts/build-lore.ts rebuilt the index; compared chunk by chunk with an index built from HEAD's lore files it is identical (0 chunks changed, entities and locations equal), because every renamed name sat on a game-stats line or a bench ability cell, which the parser strips (D-81). npm test 1053 of 1053.
- **D-277 amended (2026-11-20): Jared's rulings on the held and non-catalog names.** *Aliases* (force-catalog.md legacy table, exact matching): DominateMind, Dominate Mind and MindDomination → Mind Control; Battle-meld and BattleMeld → Force Meld. *New rule* (force-catalog.md): an ability that cannot be learned and belongs to one entity is written "Unique: <name>" in the character files; the alias and catalog check accepts the prefix and does not treat the name as a catalog ability (not looked up, aliased or offered for learning); an unprefixed unknown name is still rejected. *Character file edits* (18 replacements in 8 files): Mind Domination → Mind Control (14 :201; 15 :234, :363; 18 :79); Battle-meld → Force Meld (15 :127, 16 :96, 17 :257); 17 :83 "Unique: Multipresence 175" and "Unique: Inflict Force psychosis 170" (was Galactic Force-psychosis; description kept), 17 :84 "Unique: Multipresence \& near-immortality" (17 :46 is prose, unchanged); 12 :205 "Unique: Beast-transformation", 12 :228 "Unique: Beast-form"; 18 :191 Force Healing / Resurrection → Dark Transfer (description kept); 12 :96 "Enhance Attribute/Force Speed 92%"; 03 :254 "(environmental awareness 82%)"; 14 :282 "(Enhance Attribute 90%)"; 13 :113 and :139 "Enhance Attribute, suit-assisted 92%". Sith Sorcery needs no change: the Force lore file now has a Sith Sorcery entry (committed 279f81f). *Left as is:* lowercase prose such as "mind domination", "mind-domination" and "battle-melds" (03, 09, 10, 13, 14, 15), which describes rather than names the ability; 15 :329 "Force Body 84 (Strength 90%)", a bare Strength like 14 :282 but not in the ruling. *Checks:* the lore build changed one chunk against an index built from HEAD (c17:abeloth, the Unique techniques line now reads "Unique: Multipresence & near-immortality"); entities and locations unchanged; the other edits sit on game-stats lines or bench cells, which the parser strips (D-81). npm test 1053 of 1053.
- **D-277 tidy-up (2026-11-20).** 15 :329 "Force Body 84 (Strength 90%)" → "(Enhance Attribute 90%)", matching 14 :282. 17 :84 drops the "Unique: " prefix again ("Multipresence \& near-immortality"): the line already sits under "Unique techniques", and it was the one edit that reached the lore index. 17 :83 keeps both prefixes. *Checks:* the lore index is identical to the index built from 279f81f (before the amendment) and from the state before D-277 (0 chunks changed); npm test 1053 of 1053.
- **D-63 amended (2026-11-20): Shatterpoint read cost.** force-catalog.md's flat "0.3 Force load per read" is replaced by the rule in the lore file's Shatterpoint entry (agreed 2026-11-20, for M6): each read is an Advanced instant use, base 40 pool points divided by efficiency, multiplied by read depth (physical weaknesses or combat x1, person x1.5, relationship x2.25, situation x3.4, event x5), plus a fixed focus strain per read independent of skill (+0.05 for combat up to +0.25 for an event). The other D-63 rules (depth gates, misreads, once per fight) are unchanged. *Open:* the lore gives no focus-strain values for the person, relationship and situation bands, nor for physical weaknesses. *Not yet changed:* the code still charges 0.3 (SHATTERPOINT_COMBAT.forceLoad, src/data/combat.config.ts:82) and health.md §2 still lists "Shatterpoint read 0.3/use"; both move with the M6 pool work. *Also:* Postcognition, ForceMemory, Force Memory, ForceEcho and Force Echo are added to the legacy alias table as aliases of Psychometry (exact matching, D-276).
- **D-278 M6 Force catalog passes recorded (2026-11-20).** The Force lore file (Docs/SWRPG lore/SW_Force_Abilities_Forms_v3_complete.md) has been checked against Wookieepedia Legends across all 13 ability families and the lightsaber forms; design record: the "SWRPG M6 — Force Ability Catalog Spec" doc, sections 5 to 5k. *Aliases* (force-catalog.md, D-276 exact matching): DrainKnowledge → Mind Probe; ForceProjection → Dopplegänger; AccelerateHealing, HealingMeditation, CuratoSalva → Force Heal (Self); ControlDisease, ControlAnothersDisease → Cure Disease; ControlAnothersPain → Control Pain; RemoveAnothersFatigue → Remove Fatigue; PlaceInHibernationTrance → Hibernation Trance; ForceWhisper → Telepathy; ForceExhaustion → Force Slow; ForceStasisField → Stasis Field; ForceTeleportation → Fold Space (each target is a lore heading). The Psychometry aliases and the Shatterpoint read cost were already recorded (D-63 amended, 7b08f1e). *Rejected:* Shadowstrike (a Ta-Ree spell, not a Force power); it is no longer in the lore file, so the Q27 Sith-text question (00-index, phase1-answers, 05-force-abilities) no longer applies to it. *Form keys:* TKSaber, Lightwhip, SaberPike, StrongStyle, MediumStyle, FastStyle (the last three era-locked to after Luke founds the New Jedi Order). Zero-G is no longer a form; ZeroGravity (and the v2 id zeroG) becomes environment training, and saves holding it as a form keep the value as that training. *Architecture §15:* secondary-form growth (own E + half the main form's E, tuned with the lifetime harness), free switch at 61+ in both forms, cleave on Shii-Cho and Shien at 41+ against groups, Makashi precision (D-187) and the nine marks of contact are marked settled from the lore file's "Form Rules (M6)" section. *Record only:* nothing is built. The code still has zeroG as a form (skills.ts, activities.ts, formMatchups.ts, terrain.ts); its conversion and the save migration are M6 work.
- **D-279 Midi-chlorian counts as design anchors: a logged exception to the Legends-only rule (Jared, 2026-11-21).** The midi-chlorian counts from the 2016 canon book (Anakin Skywalker 27,700) are used as numeric design anchors for the Potential curve. *Scope:* numbers only, not lore. They calibrate the curve; they are never story facts, never quoted to the narrator and never used to settle a lore question. *Why an exception:* CLAUDE.md makes Disney-era canon (2014 onward) non-authoritative; this entry is the explicit, logged exception for these figures and nothing else. Legends story facts still win wherever they speak (for example the Legends "over 20,000" for Anakin in The Phantom Menace stays the story fact).
- **D-280 M6 unit conversion: the step 1 mapping is the integration mapping (Jared, 2026-11-21).** Approves `docs-v2/testing/m6-step1-report.md` §1.5 (checklist item 3).
  - *Capacity → pool max.* The M5 Force-meter capacity retires; its inputs move. Force Control goes into efficiency (reserve §3), Willpower into the overdraw check (§5), and pool max comes from Potential and growth (§2, §7).
  - *Load → pool drawn.* Each M5 load constant becomes a reserve draw (base × scale × effort × state ÷ efficiency). Precognition's 0.4 per trigger becomes a sustained per-exchange draw (D-282 once logged; the trial value is base 5). The Shatterpoint read's 0.3 becomes the D-63 amended formula (base 40 × depth ÷ efficiency + 0.05 focus strain).
  - *The 1.0 thresholds → strain 1.0.* This covers precognition's gate (replaced by "senses go dark" at running on will), the sequence-cap penalty, the D-194 warning, backlash and collapse.
  - *Backlash:* +0.5 × (strain above 1.0) × mental capacity, added to the mental meter. *Collapse:* 5%, +5 points per further use above 1.0, cap 50%. *Anger:* backlash threshold 1.3 (the same meaning as built).
  - *During integration,* the in-flight meter fraction is read as strain (strain = load ÷ capacity), so loads not yet converted keep working.
  - *Not changed:* M5 runs as built until the M6 integration.
- **D-281 Talent speeds learning, including the skills that drive pool growth; Potential alone sets the ceiling (Jared, 2026-11-21).** Keeps the path in step 1 report §2 rows 9–10: mind talent speeds Meditation and Force Knowledge XP, and those stats set pool refill and pool growth speed (reserve §6–7).
  - Talent never raises the pool ceiling, never changes the Potential/50 growth factor and never multiplies pool size. It speeds XP only, as in M5 (checklist item 4, two axes).
  - The Force-talent link to M5 capacity (through Force Control) retires with the old meter (D-280).
- **D-282 Precognition as a sustained per-exchange draw; pain strain once per new wound (Jared, 2026-11-21; adopted for M6, flag off until integration).** The rulings on precog-m6-trial.md, measured in `docs-v2/testing/precog-m6-trial-2.md`.
  - *Precognition:* while held, it draws base 5 per exchange (the catalog's Complex sustained cost) × state ÷ efficiency. Its trigger chance is unchanged (D-244). The senses go dark at running on will. No killing-blow softening (checklist item 5).
  - *Pain strain on sustained holds:* added once each time a fighter takes a new wound while holding any sustained ability: moderate +0.05, severe +0.15, critical or mortal +0.30. The starting values met every target, so they were not tuned. A wound already carried adds nothing per exchange. Fear and duration strain are unchanged. Reserve spec §4 amended to match.
  - *Measured* (base 5, 20 seeds per profile, all gates): every rolls §8 table row and every lifetime gate pass. In lifetime fights the player goes dark in 0 of 35,093 fights. Luke's Force-user opponents go dark in 6.1% of fights; these are pool-limited at count 10,000, and the rate is the same with pain off.
  - *Equal duels* (4,000 each): Masters 0–0.1% dark (target ≤2%), senior Knight 1.2% (≤3%), Knight 3.2%, Padawan 25 v 25 24.6% (20–30%).
  - *D-158 heavy-Force rows* at 1,000 fights: within 2.5 points of off (about 1.3 standard errors).
  - *Code:* `src/engine/precogTrial.ts` keeps `PRECOG_TRIAL.enabled = false` in the game. The trial-2 settings are `PRECOG_M6=base=5,wound=1` in the harness. The M5 rule (0.4 load per trigger, gate at meter 1.0) runs until the M6 integration.

## 2026-11-21 — Lore batch: equipment, Jedi Order, Sith orders (Jared; docs-v2/lore-batch-2026-11-21.md)
Nine research-bot lore files were reviewed in chat and added to Docs/SWRPG lore/. Lore files are named in short form below: *Ranks* = SW_Jedi_Order_Ranks_Council_OldRepublic.md, *Daily Life* = SW_Jedi_Daily_Life_Training_OldRepublic.md, *Jedi AE1/AE2* = SW_Jedi_Training_AllEras_Part1/Part2.md, *Sith Academies* = SW_Sith_Academies_Training_OldRepublic.md, *Sith AE1/AE2* = SW_Sith_Training_AllEras_Part1/Part2.md, *Sabers* = SW_Lightsabers_Crystals_Materials_AllEras.md, *Weapons* = SW_Weapons_Armor_AllEras.md. Where a file lists a [conflict] without picking, the version here is Jared's pick. None of these entries is built yet; each one is an input for the milestone it is filed under.

### Jedi Order ranks and the Council (lore: Ranks)
- **D-283 Satele Shan's career dates (Jared's picks among the Ranks file's conflicts).** Knight in 3,661 BBY and still a Knight at the 3,653 BBY peace talks, consistent with D-152 (the closed form makes her a 38-year-old Knight in 3,661). Master before 3,651 BBY (undated). Grand Master in 3,651 BBY, after she rediscovered Tython (amended: the earlier c. 3,648 date is removed; see "D-283 amended"). Her term ends c. 3,636 BBY with the Eternal Empire's conquest (Ranks Version A, footnoted to KotFE). Later narrative use of "Grand Master Shan" is the old title, not a term in office. *Lore:* Ranks, "Grand Master" and Timeline.
- **D-284 The Coruscant Temple is rebuilt c. 3,500 BBY (Jared's pick).** The Order works from Tython c. 3,653–3,636 BBY and is scattered after the conquest. The Coruscant Temple is rebuilt with major work from c. 3,500 BBY: the Ranks file's Versions B and C with Version A's date, dropping the "several decades" phrase as the outlier. *Lore:* Ranks, "Jedi Temple (Tython)", uncertainty notes.

### M8: equipment
- **D-285 Lightsaber technology by era (lore: Sabers, "Before the lightsaber" and "Hilt and blade variants"; Jedi AE1, Eras 1–2).**
  - Before c. 15,500 BBY: Force-imbued blades are the Jedi weapon, and they stay the main weapon on early Ossus.
  - The Forcesaber (Rakatan-derived; copied by the Je'daii in the Force Wars, c. 25,793 BBY) is powered through the dark side by lab-grown crystals and has no power source. It can only be used by drawing on the dark side, and every use counts as dark-side use (an M6 alignment deed).
  - c. 15,500 BBY: the first lightsabers. Unstable, they overheat after short use; rare and cumbersome.
  - c. 7,000 BBY (Hundred-Year Darkness) to the Great Hyperspace War: protosabers are common. The hilt is cabled to a belt power pack. No saber throw and no telekinetic saber combat.
  - After the Great Hyperspace War (complete by c. 4,800 BBY): modern self-contained lightsabers (the Sith moved the power cell into the hilt). From the Great Sith War (c. 4,000 BBY) on, every saber is modern.
  - The First Blade is a unique SWTOR prototype, not an era type.
- **D-286 Training sabers (lore: Sabers, "Training lightsaber"; Daily Life; Sith Academies).** A permanent low-power setting, matching M5's training-saber mode; a trained Jedi can overload one in an emergency (flavour only, no rule). Old Republic training sabers use bondar (stun) crystals; later eras use low-grade Adegan crystals. SWTOR-era Padawans (3,653–3,643 BBY) use electroblade practice swords until the Forge rite (D-303). All of them deal Vitality damage only.
- **D-287 Mass production (lore: Sabers, "Construction practice").** Lightsaber components can be mass-produced, but assembly is by hand, so stock sabers exist (the Brotherhood of Darkness, the Reborn, the Imperial Knights).
- **D-288 Crystal sources (lore: Sabers, "Construction practice and crystal sources").** Adega first; Ilum from c. 22,800 BBY.
- **D-289 Cortosis by purity (lore: Sabers, "Cortosis"; Weapons, "Anti-Jedi gear").** Refined cortosis (shields, gauntlets, cortosis rounds) shuts a lightsaber off for a short time. The exact duration is a design default set in the M8 equipment spec (sources range from "temporarily" to "several minutes"). Trace cortosis-weave in armour and vibroblades only resists a blade, like phrik.
- **D-290 Electrostaffs (lore: Weapons, "Electrostaff").** Phrik by default in the Clone Wars, though not every staff is (cheap staffs can be cut). Imperial riot-trooper staffs are cortosis. Electrostaffs bypass armour and neutralize personal shields.
- **D-291 Armour against blasters (lore: Weapons, "Armour: general classes", "Clone trooper armour", "Stormtrooper armour").** Good military armour reduces a blaster hit but never makes the wearer immune. Light armour and blast vests barely help against blasters but do help against slugs and shrapnel.
- **D-292 What a saber can do against each attack (lore: Weapons, "Slugthrowers", "Stun (as an effect)", "Sonic weapons", "Anti-Jedi gear"; Sabers, overview).** Blaster bolts: deflect and redirect. Slugs: block, never redirect. Stun: block (dissipated, as Ahsoka does in TCW "The Jedi Who Knew Too Much"), never redirect, at a slightly harder block. Flame and sonic attacks: no blade block; only a costly Force barrier.
- **D-293 Personal energy shields by era (lore: Weapons, "Personal energy shields", "Disruptors, the Mandalorian ripper and the Mandalorian disintegrator").** A by-era chance like beskarChance: prominent in the Mandalorian Wars and the Jedi Civil War, rare by the Clone Wars, nearly lost from the Galactic Civil War to the Vong War. Shield-piercing weapons (Mandalorian ripper, disintegrator, disruptors) and vibroweapons beat them.
- **D-294 Era kit (lore: Weapons, "History of blaster technology", "Pulse-wave blaster", "Vibroweapons", "Bowcaster").** Pulse-wave blasters only up to about the Great Sith War (they lost out to triple blasters; the self-contained blaster appeared before the Mandalorian Wars). Cortosis-weave vibroswords are common around 3,956 BBY and rare by the Galactic Civil War. Bowcaster: 6–10 quarrels standard.
- **D-295 Money (lore: Daily Life, "Padawan").** Padawans are not given funds; the Master manages them.

### M7: Jedi Order
- **D-296 Apprentices per Master, by era (lore: Jedi AE1 and AE2, cross-era tables; Daily Life).** Several before c. 3,996 BBY (praxeums up to about 20). After the Great Sith War, Masters are asked to take one, with exceptions: the default for the KOTOR and SWTOR eras, where in practice students also learn from several teachers. Lapsed in the New Sith Wars; strict from Ruusan (1,000 BBY); several again in the New Jedi Order; one at a time in the Legacy era.
- **D-297 Marriage, by era (lore: Jedi AE1 and AE2, cross-era tables and "Corellian Jedi").** Normal c. 4,000 BBY; discouraged after 3,996 BBY; forbidden by the late Republic. In the SWTOR era relationships are secret. Corellian Jedi are excepted. Allowed again in the New Jedi Order and Legacy eras.
- **D-298 The Jedi era table (lore: Jedi AE1 and AE2, cross-era tables and Game notes; Ranks; Daily Life).** The M7 Order rules read one row per era, with D-296 and D-297 for apprentices and marriage:

  | Era | Age of entry | Apprentices per Master | How knighting works | Family |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Je'daii (to 25,783 BBY) | Young child, on arrival on Tython | One, one-on-one | Survive the Great Journey and each Temple's displays → Ranger (D-299) | Mated couples allowed |
  | Early Order (to c. 5,000 BBY) | Any; adults taken | Several (2–5) | The Master's word, sometimes a personal test | Allowed |
  | Great Sith War to c. 3,950 BBY | Parent-chosen child or adult → younglings; adults by Council vote | Up to 20 in a praxeum → one nominal, several teachers | Master and peers judge, or a final trial and ceremony, or deeds in war | Normal → discouraged |
  | SWTOR (3,681–3,636 BBY) | Infant or young child (to Coruscant, then Tython) | One, with exceptions | High Council ruling; deeds count in wartime; field knighting (D-302) | Forbidden; secret relationships |
  | New Sith Wars (to 1,000 BBY) | Child recruited on campaign | Varies | Battle deeds; Jedi Lords | Hereditary titles |
  | Late Republic (1,000–22 BBY) | Infant; rare exceptions by Council vote | One | Five Trials; retakes allowed | Forbidden (Ki-Adi-Mundi excepted) |
  | Clone Wars (22–19 BBY) | As the late Republic | One; orphaned Padawans pooled (D-305) | The Council may knight for deeds | Forbidden |
  | Dark Times (19 BBY–4 ABY) | Any | 1–3 | The teacher's judgement | Hidden families |
  | New Jedi Order (4–c. 40 ABY) | Any at first, children later | Several (2–3) | Master and Grand Master approval after a proving mission | Allowed |
  | Legacy (c. 130 ABY) | Childhood | One at a time | Council or a senior Master; deeds | Allowed |

  The Game notes' training lengths are design defaults, not lore.
- **D-299 The Je'daii (lore: Jedi AE1, Era 1).** One-on-one Padawan training away from Padawan Kesh. Journeyers start the two-year Great Journey in their late teens. Ranks: initiate → Padawan → Journeyer → Ranger → Master → Temple Master.
- **D-300 Default Temple day (lore: Daily Life, timetable, general *The Jedi Path*; Jedi AE2, Era 6).** Morning Force study (Control, Sense, Alter), midday classes, afternoon physical and lightsaber training (Forms I–VI with training sabers), five meditations a day. Wartime and Tython eras run looser: field tasks, Council-approved solo Padawan missions, fewer formal classes. Padawans set their own schedule around missions. (Feeds the M7 Order schedule review, D-165.)
- **D-301 Progression (lore: Daily Life, "Initiate Trials", "Clans"; Sabers, Sevinte).** Initiate Trials can be retaken, but there is no extra year ("progress or leave"). The usual cutoff for being chosen is about 13; before Ruusan, adolescents and adults can be recruited. Initiates get real sabers only after showing strike control (the Sevinte cadence). Clan size: about ten.
- **D-302 Knighthood (lore: Ranks, "Jedi Knight"; Jedi AE2, Era 6).** The High Council rules on readiness. In wartime, deeds can count toward the Trials, and Masters can knight in the field. Which Trials the Old Republic used is still open (Ranks: not found).
- **D-303 Saber construction by era (lore: Daily Life; Jedi AE1; Sabers, "Construction practice").** An Initiate Trial before Ruusan in general; a late-Padawan rite at Tython's Forge (Vur Tepe) in 3,653–3,643 BBY.
- **D-304 Failing or not being chosen (lore: Daily Life, "Service Corps"; Jedi AE2, Era 6).** Leads to the Service Corps (AgriCorps, MedCorps, EduCorps, ExplorCorps) under the Council of Reassignment: a non-combat career branch. ExplorCorps sees the most fighting.
- **D-305 Clone Wars (lore: Jedi AE2, Era 6).** Padawans serve as Jedi Commanders (one per regiment), promotion is faster, fewer are sent to the Service Corps, and orphaned Padawans are pooled at the Temple. Anakin is knighted after Praesitlyn in a secret ceremony without Trials.
- **D-306 Grand Master date check (lore: Daily Life, Shigar Konshi; Ranks).** Shigar Konshi's selection by a "recently appointed" Grand Master is consistent with D-283's c. 3,648. No change. *(Superseded in part by "D-283 amended": Grand Master 3,651.)*
- **D-307 Not used: the *Jedi Academy* children's books' school-style Temple (lore: Jedi AE2, "Not used").**

### M7: Sith and other dark-side orders
- **D-308 Sith rank names by era (lore: Sith AE1 and AE2, cross-era rank tables).** The narrator uses the era's names:
  - Ancient Empire: castes (Kissai priest-teachers, Massassi warriors), Sith Lords, the ten-member Sith Council, the Dark Lord.
  - Kun's Brotherhood of the Sith (c. 3,996 BBY): disciples, Sith Lord, Dark Lord.
  - Imperial (Vitiate's Empire, to 3,636 BBY): acolyte → apprentice → Sith Lord → Darth → Dark Council (12 Dark Lords, one Sphere each) → Emperor's Wrath → Emperor; Overseer and Assistant Overseer are staff roles.
  - Revan and the Triumvirate (3,959–3,951 BBY): Dark Lord, apprentice, Shadow Hand, assassins and marauders; self-styled "Lord of" titles.
  - Brotherhood of Darkness (1,010–1,000 BBY): every Master is a "Dark Lord of the Sith"; Darth is discouraged.
  - JCW-era Korriban: "true Sith" status is earned through prestige.
  - Rule of Two (1,000 BBY–4 ABY): Master and apprentice only.
  - Lost Tribe: Tyro → apprentice → Saber → Master → Lord → High Lord → Grand Consort → Grand Lord.
  - One Sith: acolyte → apprentice → Lord/Lady → Darth (by merit only), plus the offices of Hand, Fist, Voice/Regent, Loremaster and Inquisitor.
- **D-309 "Darth" by era (lore: Sith AE1, Era 3 "Vitiate" and Era 6; Sith AE2, Eras 7, 9, 10).** Granted by the Emperor to high Lords (Vitiate did not use it as a young Lord); banned by Kaan; chosen by the Master at the oath under the Rule of Two (some kept birth names); self-styled (the Triumvirate, Caedus); merit only in the One Sith, so not every Sith is a Darth.
- **D-310 Shadow Hand (lore: Sith AE1, "The Shadow Hand title").** A single sworn subordinate who is both apprentice and second-in-command.
- **D-311 Non-Sith darksiders (lore: Sith AE2, Eras 7–9).** From 1,000 BBY to 4 ABY only the Rule of Two pair are Sith. Dark Acolytes, Dooku's assassins, Inquisitors, Emperor's Hands, Prophets of the Dark Side, the Royal Guard's Force-trained elite, the Dark Side Elite, Jerec's Dark Jedi, the Shadow Academy's Dark Jedi and the Reborn are not Sith. The narrator must not call them Sith. (Engine gap: D-320.)
- **D-312 Class size per instructor and the lesser academies (lore: Sith Academies; Sith AE1, "The lesser academies").** About 6 (Imperial Korriban), 3–4 (JCW Korriban), 20+ (Brotherhood Korriban). Brotherhood lesser academies train one role each: acolytes on Dathomir and Iridonia; assassins and spies on Ryloth, Umbara and Nar Shaddaa; warriors and marauders on Honoghr, Gentes and Gamorr. The acolyte stage normally lasts several years from a young age; SWTOR-style fast tracks are exceptional.
- **D-313 Imperial recruitment (lore: Sith Academies; Sith AE1, Era 3).** Academy training is mandatory by law for every Force-sensitive in Imperial territory, and parents who hide a child are punished. Slaves could be taken as apprentices during the Great Galactic War; slave and alien acolyte classes became policy in the Cold War and were still resented. Killing among Imperial acolytes is forbidden on pain of death, but common, disguised as tomb accidents.
- **D-314 The Sith Blademaster (lore: Sith Academies; Sith AE1, Era 6).** The counterpart of the Jedi Battlemaster. Brotherhood practice: sequences from all seven forms, one assigned key form per student, a critique after each bout, and end-of-class challenges that could be lethal.
- **D-315 Sith sparring and challenge mode (lore: Sith Academies; Sith AE1; Sith AE2, Eras 7–8).** Training bouts are non-lethal (Brotherhood pelko-barbed training sabers burn and briefly numb a limb), but challenges and trials use real weapons. M7 adds a challenge mode that uses the full M5 wound rules. Lethal trials by era: the Inquisitor audition (those not chosen are executed), the Royal Guard's final duel to the death, the Shadow Academy's knife drill, the Rule of Two survival tests (Maul on Hypori).
- **D-316 Killing the Master (lore: Sith AE2, Eras 7 and 10).** The promotion step under the Rule of Two (when the apprentice has surpassed the Master) and in the One Sith (on the Dark Lord's order, with the Master allowing it).
- **D-317 Weapons in training (lore: Sith Academies; Sabers, "Hilt and blade variants"; Sith AE2, Era 8).** A warblade or training saber during the trials; a lightsaber at apprenticeship, often the master's own as a gift. Apprentice Inquisitors use lightfoils.
- **D-318 Sith conflict picks (Jared; lore: Sith Academies, Sith AE1, Sith AE2).** Slaves and aliens were admitted gradually. Harkun's first class had 7. Gerr was badly injured, not killed. Vindican was a Sith Lord in 3,681 BBY (keep the name Darth Vindican; whether he is alive in 3,661 stays the D-152 question). JCW admission is by Sith Medallion. The Korriban Academy was founded by Exar Kun's disciples in 3,996 BBY. Darth Rivan was betrayed by his apprentice. Emperor's Hands start training after their powers develop (Mara Jade the exception). Inquisitor recruitment is staged, with Byss as the founding cohort.

### M6 checks
- **D-319 Three M6 checks from the lore batch (lore: Sith Academies; Daily Life, Orgus Din; D-262).** Added to docs-v2/spec/m6-integration-checklist.md:
  - *Re-measure:* a simulated Sith-academy run (interrogation, judging prisoners, trial killings) to check how fast Dark climbs under the per-turn and per-scene deed caps.
  - *Conversion by torture:* a captured Jedi (captured by subduing, D-262) may face a conversion arc. Torture works only through temptation moments and corruption pressure and never sets alignment directly, so a fall still comes from the character's own deeds.
  - *Step 1 review:* Orgus Din (weak Force aptitude, Council-level skill "through endless practice"). Low Potential and talent plus heavy drilling must still reach high skill (force-xp-rework.md §6 has the proposed lifetime gate).

### Engine check (report only; no code changed)
- **D-320 "Sith" opponents between 1,000 BBY and c. 25 ABY: the engine allows them (proposal, waiting for Jared).** *Finding:* D-213's era check covers minion kinds only.
  1. The narrator's npcIntroduced accepts the roles sithLord and sithAcolyte and the faction sith in any year (data/entities.config.ts NPC_ROLES; features/npcs), so a generic "Sith Lord" can be generated in 500 BBY or 10 ABY.
  2. The sithAcolyte minion kind (data/npcGen.config.ts MINION_KINDS) has no eras, so "four Sith acolytes" can be fielded in any year.
  3. The dev /fight command (turn/devCommands.ts) spawns a role sithLord in any year; sithFactionAt only switches the faction between sithEmpire and sith.
  4. No Dark Jedi, Dark Acolyte or Inquisitor role or minion kind exists, so there is nothing era-correct to fall back to.

  *Proposed fix (not built):*
  - Add the roles darkJedi, darkAcolyte and inquisitor, using the existing sithWarrior / sithInquisitor archetype numbers, so balance does not change.
  - Give sithLord and sithAcolyte a role era list that excludes [-1000, 25). The end year is a design default: Sith AE2 has the One Sith founded on Korriban "before 27 ABY" (era table c. 25 ABY), and the Lost Tribe and Lumiya come later still. Generic Sith after 25 ABY are then One Sith, Lost Tribe or Lumiya's line, and the narrator still uses the era's rank names (D-308).
  - Out of era, a generic Sith role becomes darkJedi (from sithLord) or darkAcolyte (from sithAcolyte), with a D-213-style note. A name that matches a canon figure keeps its Sith role (the Rule of Two Masters and apprentices in the canon rosters).
  - Give the sithAcolyte minion kind the same eras, with a darkAcolyte fallback kind.
  - Add one narrator line from D-311 to the era block.
