  - Tests: role and minion cases at 500 BBY, 4 ABY and 3,661 BBY, and a canon match (Darth Maul at 32 BBY stays Sith).
- **D-320 amended (Jared, 2026-11-21): approved with era gating on the new roles; built.**
  - *Roles* (data/entities.config.ts NPC_ROLES; data/npcGen.config.ts): darkJedi, darkAcolyte and inquisitor. They use the Sith numbers (the sithLord archetype weights: sithWarrior 0.6, sithInquisitor 0.4; Order talent rolls; combatant war years). Morale (the late group: break chance 0.06, the batch's "6%"; 0.2 of breaks are surrenders) and the D-263 tactical withdrawal (0.3) are keyed by archetype, so the new roles inherit them unchanged.
  - *Era gating* (ROLE_ERAS, ROLE_ERA_FALLBACK, roleForYear; [from, to) years, negative = BBY):
    - Sith Lord and Sith acolyte: every year except 1,000 BBY to 25 ABY ([-100000, -999) and [25, 100000)). Out of era, a Sith Lord becomes a Dark Jedi and a Sith acolyte becomes a Dark Acolyte.
    - Dark Acolyte: 22–19 BBY only ([-22, -18)); otherwise Dark Jedi.
    - Inquisitor: 19 BBY to c. 11 ABY ([-19, 12)); otherwise Dark Jedi.
    - Dark Jedi: any era, the default fallback.
  - *Rank kept:* an era-corrected NPC stores the narrator's role as rankRole (features/npcs npcSchema), and generation reads its rank and default age from it (engine/npcGen.ts rollNpcInputs; features/npcs selectors). A Sith acolyte corrected to a Dark Jedi keeps the acolyte rank, so the correction never changes balance (tested: identical generation inputs).
  - *Where it runs:* the pipeline's follow-ups (turn/pipeline.ts canonEvents) check each new NPC. A name that matches an in-era canon figure (D-81) is linked and keeps its role (Darth Maul in 32 BBY stays a Sith Lord). Otherwise an out-of-era role gets the engine event npcRoleEraCorrected (features/npcs, engine source only), which sets the era's role and notes the change ("no Sith Lord in 500 BBY: a Dark Jedi instead (D-320; only the Rule of Two Master and apprentice are Sith in these years)"). Features cannot read the lore index (architecture §1), so the canon check stays in the turn layer.
  - *Minions:* the sithAcolyte kind is gated with the Sith roles; new kinds darkAcolyte (22–19 BBY) and darkJedi (any era) with the Sith acolyte's numbers. minionKindForYear now follows a fallback chain (Sith acolyte → Dark Acolyte → Dark Jedi). /fight's duelist takes the era's role.
  - *Narrator:* core-gm.md gains the D-311 line (from 1,000 BBY to 25 ABY only the Rule of Two pair are Sith; others are never called Sith); contract.md tells it to use darkJedi, darkAcolyte or inquisitor for them.
  - *Tests:* tests/turn/sithEraRoles.test.ts (the role table, notes, minion chain, balance equality, the engine event and its refusals, and the pipeline at 32 BBY, 4 ABY and 3,661 BBY). tests/engine/eraMinions.test.ts: "every fallback is era-neutral" becomes "every fallback chain ends at an era-neutral kind". npm run test:all: 1,132 of 1,132 pass (157 s).
  - *Known limit (D-81 alias matching, not changed):* the canon match uses the lore index's aliases and era windows. The reborn Emperor (4–25 ABY) is indexed only as "palpatine reborn", so a narrator's "Emperor Palpatine" in 10 ABY is not matched and would become a Dark Jedi. Fix in the lore index aliases when the era 14 roster is built.

## 2026-11-21 — Lore batch 2: Force potential and the dark side (Jared; docs-v2/lore-batch-2-2026-11-21.md)
Lore files: *MIDI* = SW_Midichlorians_Force_Potential.md (revision 2); *Lucas* = SW_Lucas_Statements_Force_Potential.md (behind-the-scenes statements, not in-universe lore: it informs design numbers only and never overrides Legends); *DS1/DS2/DS3* = SW_DarkSide_Fall_Corruption_Redemption_Part1/Part2/Part3.md. The batch's part 2 rulings (D-283, the 25 ABY end year, the queue priorities, the narrator note, D-320 and the §15 M7 rank-names task) were built and logged in 2d347b0 (D-320 amended); the alignment audit (part 5) is docs-v2/testing/alignment-lore-audit.md from the same commit.

### M6: Force potential
- **D-321 Legends midi-chlorian counts (lore: MIDI §2 "Named counts", §3 "Thresholds and averages").** Anakin "over 20,000" per cell, the highest known (27,700 is the accepted anchor, D-279). Average Jedi 10,000 (anchor). Yoda is second only to Anakin, with no number. No Legends count exists for Luke, Obi-Wan, Palpatine or anyone else. An average non-Force-sensitive Human has under 2,500 (MIDI §3: "less than 2,500" in the body, "normal Human levels of 2,500" in the lead; best fit "up to about 2,500"). A mildly Force-sensitive person has about 5,000 (Nova Stihl, "in excess of 5,000"). The Legends scale is kept; Lucas's "like a thousand" per cell (Lucas, Topic 5c) is not used.
- **D-322 Design counts (design values, not lore; lore: Lucas Topics 1, 2 and 4; MIDI §2).**
  - Luke 27,700: Lucas says Luke had his father's potential ("But the son could become that", Topic 1).
  - Obi-Wan 13,000 (the reserve spec's "strong Master" row).
  - Palpatine about 17,400: derived so that unhurt Anakin (27,700) has twice Palpatine's pool ceiling, per Lucas's "twice as good as the Emperor" (Topic 2a). With the reserve §11 ceiling 500 × (count ÷ 10,000)^1.5, exactly 2.0× needs 27,700 ÷ 2^(2/3) = 17,450; the reserve spec's table has 17,300 (2.03×). Both round to "about 17,400"; the reserve spec is an exported doc, so the value is updated there before its next re-export, not in the repo copy.
  - Orgus Din 7,000 (the reserve spec's Jedi minimum; force-xp-rework.md §6).
- **D-323 Cybernetics and the Force ceiling (checklist item 15, replacing "cybernetic ceiling loss"; lore: MIDI §6 "Losing or changing potential"; Lucas Topics 3 and 4).** Supersedes the reserve spec §7 table (5 / 10 / 20% plus 15 points per replacement) and the architecture §15 note that copied it.
  - A single lost or replaced limb never lowers the pool ceiling. Legends: losing limbs does not reduce potential (*Darth Plagueis*; MIDI §6); Luke's hand, Anakin's arm.
  - The ceiling falls only when a large share of the living body is lost or replaced (Lucas, *Vanity Fair* 2005: "as a living form, there's not that much of him left", Topic 3).
  - *Proposed curve (design default, for review before adoption):* permanent loss = max(0, s − 0.20), where s is the share of the living body lost or replaced, from a body-share table: hand 1%, forearm 2%, whole arm 5%, whole leg 17%, lungs replaced by a respirator 5%, extensive burns needing artificial skin 15%. Vader after Mustafar (both legs 34%, left arm 5%, the earlier right forearm 2%, burns 15%, lungs 5%) has s = 61%, so a permanent loss of 41% (target about 40%). One hand: 0. Both arms: 0. One leg and one arm: 2%. The hinge is continuous; a smooth (softplus) version can replace it if the build prefers.
  - *Psychological part:* about 20% for Vader, recoverable through story events. *Dark Lord*: Sidious judges Vader's limits psychological and says he could regain his full power by facing his choices (MIDI §6).
  - *Total:* the two parts add: Vader loses about 60% of his ceiling, so his ceiling is 0.40 × 2,305 = 922 against Palpatine's 1,152 at 17,450: 80% (Lucas: "maybe 20 percent less than the Emperor", Topics 3a and 4b; Legends: "eight-tenths of the strength of the Emperor", MIDI §6).
  - *Lightning:* cybernetic hands or arms block lightning-type powers (Legends: Vader rarely used Force lightning because it risked shorting his cybernetics and life support, *The Jedi Path*; MIDI §6). Separate from the ceiling.
  - *The suit's immobility* is a combat mobility effect (M5/M8 armour weight, architecture §15), not a Force effect.

### M6: Alignment
- **D-324 Fall-speed targets (lore: DS1, DS2 and DS3, Section 1 and the falls tables).** Harness targets for the M6 re-measure, not formulas:
  - Self-driven falls take years: Anakin about 3, Cade about 7, Dooku about 12, Jacen about 13.
  - Outside pressure compresses them to days or months (Kyp, Luke on Byss, Ulic), or about a week under torture (Bastila).
  - A wound in the Force can make a fall immediate (Nihilus).
  - A final tipping chain can complete in under a day (Anakin).
  This answers audit finding 3; the audit's 3-scene Knight fall is checked against it.
- **D-325 Physical corruption follows immersion and heavy dark-power use, not Dark alone (lore: DS1 §4 "General description"; DS2 §4; DS3 §4).** Eyes turn yellow only in anger at moderate immersion (Dooku, Starkiller); permanently yellow at deep immersion; pallor, decay and ageing come only after long, heavy immersion (Bane, Sion, Nihilus); speed and severity vary by person (DS3 §4: Teneniel Djo at once, Caedus eyes only after a year, Ulic, Kun and Revan unmarked). At redemption the eyes recover and the skin does not (Vader, DS2 §3–4). This settles the canon eye-state check (checklist re-measure line): Dooku flickers in anger rather than staying "Normal". It answers audit findings 8, 9, 15 and 16.
- **D-326 Redemption follows Shim's stages (lore: DS1 §1 "Tolaris Shim's Four Stages", Stage 4; DS1–DS3 §3).** Minor taint is atoned for by reflection (meditation and the hold table). A true fall needs a selfless act done without the dark side. Redemption never zeroes Corruption, and a lasting hold remains that can flare under stress (DS3 §3: every later-era redemption keeps one). It answers audit findings 5 and 6; finding 1 (the spec's redemption effect leaves deep-Dark characters still fallen) still needs a spec change.
- **D-327 Madness amplifies existing trauma; Force psychosis is Abeloth's (lore: DS2 §6; DS3 §6 "Force psychosis").** The dark side amplifies existing trauma; it does not cause madness by itself (the DS page's best fit, both parts). "Force psychosis" is the Legends term for an affliction caused by the entity Abeloth (c. 43 ABY, *Fate of the Jedi*), eased when the victim is cut off from the Force and lifted when she is defeated; it is not a result of a character's own dark-side use. *Reframed:* force-catalog-questions.md's ForcePsychosis row (not a power; "a candidate for the illness or world-events systems") becomes a world event for the Fate of the Jedi era only, and madness from a character's own dark-side use is a separate rule (audit finding 10) that needs an existing trauma.
- **D-328 Shim's "a killer of innocents can't be redeemed" is an in-universe opinion, not a rule (lore: DS1 §1 [conflict]; DS2 §3; DS3 §3).** Anakin and Kyp contradict it. No alignment rule bars redemption after killing innocents.

## 2026-11-21 — Rulings on 84ec410 (Jared): cybernetics curve, Palpatine's count, pressure, gray use, redemption
Spec and decision changes only; no code. The changed specs are exported docs, so each header lists these repo-only changes to copy into the doc before its next re-export.
- **D-323 amended: the cybernetics curve (Jared).** Approved in shape with threshold 30% and slope 1.3: permanent loss = max(0, share of living body lost − 0.30) × 1.3, with the D-323 body-share table (hand 1%, forearm 2%, whole arm 5%, whole leg 17%, lungs replaced 5%, extensive burns 15%). Checks: Vader after Mustafar (61%) 40.3%; both legs (34%) 5.2%; all four limbs (44%) 18.2%; one hand, one arm, one leg or both arms 0. With the recoverable 20% psychological part, Vader's ceiling is 0.397 × 2,305 = 915 against Palpatine's 1,138 (17,300, D-322 amended): 0.80. The reserve spec §7 cybernetics table is replaced in the repo copy to match (header note).
- **D-322 amended: Palpatine's count is 17,300 (Jared),** the reserve spec's existing value (ceiling about 1,138; unhurt Anakin 2.03× his ceiling), not the derived 17,450.
- **D-329 Pressure multipliers (Jared; approved as proposed; lore: SW_DarkSide_Fall_Corruption_Redemption_Part1-3.md, Sections 1, 2 and 7; checklist item 24).** A pressure factor P, the product of the sources present, capped at 4. P multiplies Dark from deeds and from dark-tagged Force use and the per-scene deed cap, and divides the temptation cool-down. Sources: a wound in the Force (Malachor V, 3,960–3,951 BBY) 3; Byss (10–11 ABY) 2; a dark spirit or entity working on the character 2; Korriban by era (table in alignment-m6.md §9); Yavin 4's temples while Kun's spirit is there (to 11 ABY) 1.5; the Dagobah cave while inside 1.5; a carried Sith artifact 1.5; a Sith mentor turning the character 1.25; torture 1.5 plus one temptation moment each scene of captivity. Korriban's pull: strong in the KOTOR era and around 23 BBY, faded by about 1,000 BBY (lore); the 3,636 BBY and post-23 BBY values are design defaults. Re-measure: a Kyp-style case (a dark spirit ×2 and Yavin 4's temples ×1.5, P = 3) falls in days to weeks (D-324).
- **D-330 The intent discount for dark-tagged powers (Jared; lore: Part1 §8 Jolee Bindo, Part3 §8 Kyle Katarn; answers audit finding 13; checklist item 25).** A dark-tagged power used calmly with light intent gives a small Dark gain (design default +0.2), no immersion and no Corruption, and at most one such gain per scene. "Calmly with light intent" (engine test): no fear or rage state, no anger fuel that scene, and the use carries no dark deed tag. Anything else counts in full, and anger deeds count in full. Re-measure: a Kyle Katarn–style career (frequent calm tool use, no anger deeds) ends below the Dark band. The Vaapad and Juyo rules in item 25 stay drafts, pending Jared's review.
- **D-331 Redemption moves a character from fallen to redeemed at any depth (Jared; lore: Part1 §1 Shim's Stage 4, Part1-3 §3; resolves audit finding 1).**
  - *Trigger:* a redemption act, Shim's Stage 4: a selfless act done without the dark side (no dark-side draw that scene), at real cost. The engine reads it as a major or extreme `sacrifice`, `mercy` or `forgiveness` deed while fallen. The cost is a story matter, not a mechanic.
  - *Effect:* the fallen flag clears and the redeemed flag is set, however deep Dark is. Dark drops to just below the fall line (49, if higher); Light +15 and immersion to 0, so the eyes clear (D-325). Corruption does not reset, and the skin's physical corruption stays (D-325).
  - *After:* Dark falls only through atonement over time (meditation and Light gains, the hold table). The lasting hold stays: while redeemed, a stress trigger (a fear or rage state, high strain, a loved one threatened) opens a temptation moment even inside the cool-down, still at most one per scene.
  - *Re-measure:* a Vader-like deep-Dark character (Dark 90, Light 0, high Corruption) redeemed by one act lands just below the fall line (Dark 49) with Corruption unchanged.

## 2026-11-21 — Rulings on cb3f0d1 (Jared): redemption event path, severance, the calm-use gate
Spec and decision changes only; no code. Each changed spec's export header lists these as repo-only changes.
- **D-331 amended (Jared): Light +15 on redemption kept,** and an event path added (answers audit finding 4; lore: SW_DarkSide_Fall_Corruption_Redemption_Part1.md §3, Revan's mind wipe and Ulic cut off from the Force). The event path: an explicit engine event of a mind wipe or severance from the Force clears the fallen flag. Dark drops to 49 if higher; Corruption and the skin's corruption are unchanged; immersion drops to 0 (design default: the character cannot draw on the Force, or no longer remembers how). There is **no Light +15**, and the redeemed flag is not set: the character must still choose the light afterwards. The redeemed flag (and its stress-flare rule) is set at the character's first light choice at a temptation moment (`resisted_temptation`, or a light deed of moderate or higher severity) (design default). Until then a single dark deed can push Dark back over the fall line. The event needs a visible cause, like a narrator's interrupted fight end (D-217): the narrator cannot declare it. The cause is an AI event in the same reply naming who does it, which the engine checks before it emits the engine event. Mind wipe: the target is captured or subdued and the actors are a council or Masters able to do it (Revan, the Jedi Council, 3956 BBY). Severance: the actor holds a Force-severing technique (Nomi Sunrider: a unique technique, Characters_04-05), or the target is at a wound in the Force at the moment of a catastrophe (Meetra Surik at Malachor V, Characters_06). A refused cause leaves alignment untouched and is logged.
- **D-332 "Severed from the Force" status (Jared; proposal, not built; lore: Characters_04-05 Nomi Sunrider and Ulic Qel-Droma, Characters_06 Meetra Surik, SW_DarkSide_Fall_Corruption_Redemption_Part1.md §3; M6 checklist item 26).** Proposed rules, all design defaults for review:
  - *Cause:* the D-331 severance event (by another, as Nomi severed Ulic) or self-severance at a wound in the Force (Meetra at Malachor V).
  - *While severed:* no Force use at all: no draws, no Force abilities, no Force Sense, no precognitive defense, no dark surge. Force skills earn no XP and do not rust (frozen). Lightsaber forms work as physical skill only (muscle memory); Force-dependent techniques in them (Force-assisted leaps, saber throw) are off. Others sense the character as non-Force-sensitive. Deeds still move Light and Dark; dark-tagged use is impossible; immersion decays as usual.
  - *Return:* a return value R from 0 to 1, rising smoothly over time: R = smoothstep(t ÷ T), with T = 10 years (Ulic about ten years cut off; Meetra about nine before she reawakened). While R < 1 the usable pool is R × pool max and Force skills work at R × their level. Story catalysts (a Force bond, a teacher, a light nexus; Kreia for Meetra) advance t; design default: each catalyst event adds a year.
  - *Lore limits:* the lore gives the cases and the durations, not mechanics; Ulic is not recorded regaining his full power. A permanent severance is a story choice (T unset), not the default.
- **D-330 amended (Jared): the Kyle Katarn sim gates the +0.2 value.** If a calm-use career drifts into the Dark band (lean "Leaning dark" or worse), report it and propose either a lower value or letting meditation and atonement wear down calm-use Dark (for example, calm-use Dark kept as its own pool that the hold table and meditation remove first). The value is not adopted until the sim passes.
- **Jared approves D-331 amended, D-332 and D-330 amended (2026-11-21),** including the defaults: immersion to 0 on a mind wipe or severance, so the eyes clear (Revan showed no marks after the wipe); redeemed only at the first light choice.
- **D-332 amended (Jared, 2026-11-21; applies when the severed status is built; lore: Characters_06 Meetra Surik).**
  - *Sensing:* a severed character is an absence in the Force, not an ordinary person (Kreia sensed Meetra as a void). Ordinary sensing reads nothing. A strong senser (a clear win on the sensing contest, alignment-m6.md §8) notices the void itself and knows something is wrong.
  - *Return:* the slow curve (R = smoothstep(t ÷ T), T = 10 years) stays as the fallback (Ulic). A reconnection event (a Force bond, a teacher, returning to the place of the wound) starts a faster recovery instead of adding a year: R rises from its current value to 1 along a smoothstep over months (design default 6 months; Meetra reconnected over months once Kreia and her companions bonded with her, after about nine years cut off). Like the other events, a reconnection needs a visible cause; the narrator cannot declare it. Permanent severance stays a story choice.
- **D-333 Force XP from exercise difficulty (Jared, 2026-11-21; force-xp-rework.md; measured in force-xp-trial.md, force-xp-trial-2.md and force-xp-trial-3.md).** Adopted as the M6 Force XP model. The flag (`FORCE_XP_TRIAL.enabled`) stays off in the game until M6 integration (checklist item 21).
  - Force-discipline sessions train on an exercise whose rating (demand + finesse, proposal §1–2) replaces the 10–90 drill ladder for Force skills. The M4 XP formula and K are unchanged.
  - Free exercises earn the challenge factor × 0.1 (the trickle).
  - Neutral start: each exercise aims at today's challenge mean, as [level, factor] anchors at 10-level band midpoints for each rank, effort and kind, interpolated between anchors. At level 80 and above the last anchor holds, so there is no ceiling.
  - Below a tier's normal demand rating, effort is continuous, floored at light (Jared accepts, ruling 3 on force-xp-trial-2.md).
  - Luke's first two milestones run about 9% slower than the old model, inside the ±20% gate. Jared accepts this; no step table (ruling 2 on force-xp-trial-2.md; Golden Rule 7).
- **D-334 Exercises fit the pool (Jared, ruling 2 on force-xp-trial.md).**
  - Each day's Force block is fitted to the pool: one demand factor, at which the projected strain peaks at or under the standard limit of 0.75. Finesse makes up the rating, so XP is unchanged.
  - The floor is Basic at light effort (Force Sight at light effort for sense).
  - Each session also fits its own block from the reserve at the time.
  - Pool max gates only the scale classes, via Output (reserve §3).
  - The strain trim (proposal §4) stays as the safety net, in hours, for days when even the floors overrun.
  - Backlash and collapse above 1.0 strain (reserve §5) are not modelled in training, which stays under 0.75. They are built and gated in scene at M6 integration (checklist item 27; ruling 4 on force-xp-trial-2.md).
  - Gate: Orgus Din (count 7,000, a design default per D-322) on a heavy Control routine reaches Force Control 84 within ±2 years of count 10,000 on all 20 seeds.
- **D-335 Physical Force boost saturates at Force Control 115 (Jared, ruling 1 on force-xp-trial.md and ruling 1 on force-xp-trial-2.md).**
  - Boost = min(45, 0.3 × (min(FC, 115) − 40) + 0.2 × body-ability level). This amends progression-numbers §11 when the D-333 model is switched on.
  - The ceiling removal stays for Force abilities. Luke's 58–68 peak gate is unchanged.
  - Chosen from runs at 115 and 120 (20 seeds each) as the cap whose Luke physical peak median is nearest 63–65 with every gate passing: 115 gives a median of 63 (range 62–63); 120 gives 65.5 (65–66). The 110 trial value gave 59–60 (force-xp-trial-3.md).
- **D-336 Overnight strain (Jared, ruling 3 on force-xp-trial.md; finding).**
  - Reserve §6 recovery is applied as specified. Carried strain came from same-day overdraw, which D-334 removes.
  - No change to recovery.
- **D-337 Padawan binding limit (Jared, ruling 4 on force-xp-trial.md).**
  - The old meters binding for Padawans is accepted. The proposal's prediction that strain binds is withdrawn: its block assumed 2 h of Control, where the real default block is 1 h Control plus 1 h sense.
  - No tuning of R or the multipliers.
- **D-338 Force powers research replaces the v3 powers sections (Jared, 2026-11-21; docs-v2/lore/batches/force-catalog-revision.md).** `SW_Force_Powers_Part1a.md` to `_Part3b.md` (added to `Docs/SWRPG lore/` in this commit) are the source of truth for the Force catalog. They replace the Force powers sections of `SW_Force_Abilities_Forms_v3_complete.md`. That file's lightsaber forms sections stay current until lore batch 10 applies the forms catalog check (batches 3 and 10 handle them). Part3b's catalog check is applied to `force-catalog-m6.md`; findings with no ruling below are applied as Part3b states them (listed in the spec's revision notes). Sources: citations of *The Clone Wars* (2008) on Legends pages are accepted; only the 2014 *Lost Missions* material is excluded (Part3b developer decision 12).
- **D-339 The Force created Anakin (Jared, ruling on point 1; Part3b d1; force-catalog-m6.md §5j).** In the game world it is fact that the Force created Anakin: his conception was the Force's own act, and Shmi had no father for him. Whether Plagueis's (and Sidious's) experiments provoked it stays their belief, unresolved. The narrator may present it as what they believed, never as confirmed. Midi-chlorian manipulation keeps its tier and availability. Replaces the 2026-11-20 wording ("the Force itself created Anakin in response, to end the Sith", written as fact). Logged against Wookieepedia, which gives the whole account as Plagueis's belief (*Midi-chlorian manipulation* §intro; *Chosen One/Legends*).
- **D-340 Flow-walking cannot change the past (Jared; Part3b d2, d3; §5i).** Caedus admits this in *Legacy of the Force*; a walker can watch and leave an imprint, nothing more. Time-drifting stays dropped. Conflict recorded: the Wookieepedia *Flow-walking* intro says flow-walkers "could alter the past"; the game follows the novels.
- **D-341 Tutaminis and Force Absorb are one power (Jared; Part3b b1, c15; §5c).** Tutaminis, base tier Basic, with tiers by level: absorb ordinary energy, then lightning (design default level 41), then catching a lightsaber blade (61). "Force absorb" redirects to Tutaminis on Wookieepedia, and it was taught in the first academy classes. The `ForceAbsorb` key resolves to Tutaminis. Supersedes the 2026-11-20 tiers (Tutaminis Complex, Force Absorb Advanced).
- **D-342 Sides (Jared; Part3b c1–c11).** Neutral: battle meditation (Sith battle meditation's domination is a dark deed), hibernation trance, Force resistance, Oneness, Force healing (self and others), Transfer Force, the Voss healing ritual and Force stun. Dark: Force Sphere, Force Wind, memory rub (Sith sorcery). Dark healing stays dark: Force drain, Dark Transfer, the death field (and Fulminiss's corrupted Voss ritual). Kinetite becomes an application of Electric Judgment (light, forbidden class), not a standalone telekinesis power; the `Kinetite` key resolves there.
- **D-343 Drops, and Purification kept (Jared; Part3b b2, b6, b7; §5e, §5h, §5i).** Dropped: Create Force Wraith (unlicensed *Polyhedron* source) and Force Travel (Wookieepedia's non-canon banner); both become rejected names. **Purification (Crystal) is kept** as a crafting technique, not a combat power: light side (a light deed); Advanced, Knight and up; a Jedi Order technique, rarely used, available whenever the Order exists; only on synthetic crystals made by dark-side users, removing the dark taint so the crystal can go into a Jedi lightsaber, with a colour change; a long meditation in downtime or crafting, never in a scene; linked to the crystal lore. Lore: the Lightsaber crystal/Legends page (an advanced Jedi technique for cleansing dark-side synthetic crystals) and *Riptide* (41 ABY: Jaden Korr turns a Dark Jedi's red crystal into a yellow-bladed Jedi crystal through meditation). The bot's "Canon only" tag was wrong; the tagging error is noted in the research queue, and the bot's rule 3 now checks Legends pages first.
- **D-344 Lore-locks (Jared; Part3b f1–f5; §5, §5e, §5f, §5i).** Fold space: taught by the Aing-Tii; outsiders from 43.5 ABY (Luke). Theran Force-listening: learnable from the Therans. Darksight: mainly the Sorcerers of Rhand and the Nightsisters, rarely others. Telekinetic Barrage renamed Ballistakinesis and made a Jensaarai tradition (`TelekineticBarrage` key resolves there). Phase: rare, no Killik lock (supersedes the 2026-11-20 lock).
- **D-345 Force-meld by era (Jared; Part3b f6; §5c).** Rare in the Old Order (Jorus C'baoth), unavailable 19 BBY to 25 ABY, revived from 25 ABY (Anakin Solo redeveloped it in the Yuuzhan Vong War). Supersedes "available in every era as rare" (§4).
- **D-346 Forbidden availability class (Jared; Part3b f7, f8, f10; §3).** New learning class. Knights can't learn a forbidden power without a Master's permission; Masters may study it under control. Electric Judgment: forbidden to Knights, controlled study from 990 BBY, used openly by the New Jedi Order. Deadly Sight: forbidden. Morichro: forbidden by 1000 BBY (Yaddle the exception). Covert learning risks Order reputation (M7 hook).
- **D-347 Scale (Jared; Part3b d4, e1–e5; §5c, §5e, §5f, §5i, §5j).** Supernova tops out at a binary star (needs Sith crystals; backfire risk: Naga Sadow's Denarii suns; Aleema Keto's nine stars). Force storm (lightning) is a radius around the user; fleet scale belongs to Force storm (wormhole). Force illusion is interstellar only at the top tier and only amplified (meditation sphere, group work: Naga Sadow, the Fallanassi at N'zoth). Force-flash has two uses (holocam fuzz, blinding flash); system scale is Abeloth's only. Scale check against the reserve spec: docs-v2/testing/force-scale-check.md (report only).
- **D-348 New entries (Jared; Part3b a1–a3, a8, a14–a16, a18; §5b, §5d, §5e, §5h, §5j).** Life Detection (*prima vitae*), Sense Force (*tactus otium*), *altus sopor*, Summon Storm, Vongsense (from 25 ABY), and the named lightning variants as tiers of Force lightning (Force Tempest, Sith barrage, Lightning Shield, Lightning Grenade, Sith Seeker, Lightning Burst). Sith necromancy and Dark Plague are added as NPC and story powers, not learnable by the player. Costs follow §3's tier defaults; the remaining Part3b gaps are listed in §5j, not added.
- **D-349 "ShatterSense" renamed shatterpoint perception (Jared, ordered earlier; Part3b b9).** Renamed in docs-v2 specs, architecture.md and code (talent key `shatterpointPerception`, `STUB_PLAYER_SHATTERPOINT_PERCEPTION`). Not renamed: earlier entries in this log and progress.md (history), and `legacy/` and `Docs/` (read-only and canon source); 06-shatterpoint.md notes the legacy key.
- **D-350 Lightsaber forms (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 3; lore: SW_Lightsaber_Forms_AllEras_Part1.md and _Part2.md, decisions [1]–[12] in SW_Lightsaber_Index_AllEras.md).**
  - *[1] Standard weapon:* protosabers become the standard Jedi weapon c. 7000 BBY. The 5000 BBY date (Force-imbued sword page) is consistent, since protosabers are standard by then too. No change to D-285's technology timeline.
  - *[2] Niman origin:* the Kashi Mer Royale Macheteros dual-blade style, adopted by the Legions of Lettow, then by the Jedi. "From Jar'Kai" is recorded as a conflict and not used.
  - *[3] "Vaapad" before Windu:* the *Insider* 92 reconciliation. "Vaapad" was an old alternative name for Juyo (the creature has both names), so NPCs c. 1000 BBY may call Juyo "Vaapad". The Vaapad variant, with its own mechanics, is Windu's and Sora Bulq's late-Republic creation. Karpyshyn's typo statement is recorded as [BTS].
  - *[4] Juyo-Kos:* a Juyo variant (an SWTOR-era Jedi refinement) with Juyo's profile and no separate mechanics.
  - *[5] Sith Shien:* an alias of Shien, flavour only.
  - *[6] Niman mastery:* Drallig's "ten years or more" is a calibration target for the XP system (true mastery of a form takes about a decade of dedicated study), not a hard rule. Noted for the Force and form XP review.
  - *[7] NJO Strong/Medium/Fast:* forms, era-gated to the New Jedi Order (from c. 13 ABY), labelled game-derived (*Jedi Outcast*, *Jedi Academy*). Confirms the 2026-11-20 catalog ruling (force-catalog-m6.md §5k).
  - *[8] Dun Möch:* a dark-leaning technique. Used by a Jedi it is a minor dark deed, with the intent discount (D-330) where used calmly as a tactic rather than to humiliate.
  - *[9] Tràkata:* allowed for Jedi with no alignment cost. Its reliance on deception is a conduct and reputation note with traditionalist Masters (M7), not a deed.
  - *[10] Trispzest:* only for fighters who can fly or levitate (S'kytri, Maw).
  - *[11] Thin entries:* Su ma is folded into Ataru as its rotation moves. Lus-ma is NPC-only (Dooku's students: Grievous, MagnaGuards) with a Form III-like profile. Juyo-Kos follows [4].
  - *[12] Untrained dual-wielding:* its own penalty profile, separate from trained Jar'Kai (the second blade hinders without training).
  - *Form VII access (with D-352 [19]):* Juyo and Vaapad use the forbidden (restricted) availability class of the Force catalog revision (D-346). Before Ruusan, Masters won't teach it to the reckless. After Ruusan, Juyo goes to hand-picked students only, and Vaapad needs Windu's permission (only Depa Billaba received it). Juyo needs mastery of several other forms first.
- **D-351 Sub-group styles (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 3; lore: SW_Lightsaber_SubgroupStyles_AllEras.md, Index [13]–[17]).**
  - *[13] Mandalorian anti-Jedi methods:* gear (beskar) plus the Jedi-hunter doctrine already logged. No invented named style.
  - *[14] Matukai, Zeison Sha, Jensaarai:* Force traditions for NPCs and backgrounds, with their weapons (wan-shen, discblade, cortosis armour). Whether they're playable is decided in M7.
  - *[15] Sith sword:* its dark-side amplification is mechanical, a modest boost to a dark-side wielder's dark powers. The size is set in the M8 equipment spec.
  - *[16] Zeison Sha origin:* the New Sith Wars. The KOTOR II armour item is a game-item appearance and isn't used.
  - *[17] Echani:* learnable by anyone with a teacher (special forces, the Royal Guard and Imperial Intelligence trained in it).
- **D-352 Lightsaber training (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 3; lore: SW_Lightsaber_Training_AllEras.md, Index [18]–[21]; M7).**
  - *[18] First lightsaber, late Republic:* Initiates (younglings) build their first saber on Ilum in the Gathering, as in *The Clone Wars* (2008) "The Gathering" arc (Legends under D-338's sources rule). The Padawan page's forging ritual is the deeper crafting done during the apprenticeship. Extends D-303 (an Initiate Trial before Ruusan; a late-Padawan rite at Tython's Forge in 3,653–3,643 BBY).
  - *[19] Form access by rank (post-Ruusan):* Initiates learn the basics of Forms I–VI with training sabers; Padawans spend a year or two on each of Forms II–V; Knights specialize; Form VII per D-350's access rule.
  - *[20] Ataru c. 1000 BBY:* discouraged for Padawans (fewer teachers offer it); no lock and no penalty.
  - *[21] NJO form access:* from c. 11 ABY the academy teaches the NJO styles and the three rings; the classic forms return gradually as the Order rediscovers them (*The Jedi Path*, holocrons, the Drallig recording). Luke knows Ataru and Form V from the start.
- **D-353 Weapon variants (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 3; lore: SW_Lightsaber_WeaponVariants_AllEras.md, Index [22]–[26]; M8).**
  - *[22] Training sabers (amends D-286):* the low-power setting is standard, and a fully trained Jedi can readjust one for real use in an emergency. This is now an actual option, no longer flavour only.
  - *[23] Saga Edition and other RPG claims:* usable as game data only where no narrative source gives a figure (rule 8, last resort), and always labelled as game stats. A shoto can be thrown like any saber, with no special throwing bonus.
  - *[24] Saberstaff:* no alignment cost. The Order frowns on it, a reputation note with traditionalist Masters (M7). Combat use is practically unheard of among Jedi by 32 BBY.
  - *[25] Lightwhip:* a breakage rule. A heavy power blow (for example a Djem So power strike) can short it out.
  - *[26] Electrostaff bisection setting:* dropped. Lethal shock and blunt trauma are enough.
- **D-354 Combat techniques (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 3; lore: SW_Lightsaber_CombatTechniques_AllEras.md, Index [27]–[31]; M5/M8).**
  - *[27] Cortosis (keeps D-289's split by purity):* refined cortosis shorts a blade out; cortosis weave only resists. That reconciles KOTOR's block-only portrayal with later sources. Design default for the short: the blade is out for the rest of the exchange and can re-ignite the next exchange; purified Cortosis-Shield shorts it longer (set in M8). Mestare crystals are immune. Fast striking against cortosis only sparks.
  - *[28] Marks of contact:* the alignment weight comes from the situation, not the cut. Killing a disarmed, yielding or helpless opponent is a dark deed (as with Dooku's execution). Sai cha against droids, or against a great danger that resists non-lethal subdual, is acceptable. Sai tok used by a Jedi is a minor dark deed ("Sith-like"). Tied to strike intent (D-260).
  - *[29] Tutaminis absorbing a blade:* the top Tutaminis tier of D-341 ("catching a blade"), gated by skill tier, not rank.
  - *[30] Beskar:* stops the cut, but some blunt force carries through: a saber hit on beskar becomes a Vitality hit or a minor wound instead of a cut.
  - *[31] Ysalamiri:* inside the bubble, Force powers and Force-assisted techniques stop working: precognition, Force-boosted leaps and speed, and the Force parts of Ataru, Niman and Vaapad. Plain blade skill still works.
- **D-355 Duels and rankings (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 3; lore: SW_Lightsaber_Duels_AllEras.md, Index [32]–[34]).**
  - *[32] Exar Kun's record:* both statements hold. As a student he sometimes lost to Baas in sparring; he was never defeated in a real duel.
  - *[33] Rankings:* soft calibration targets for the duel harness, not hard per-era tiers.
  - *[34] Comparisons across eras:* there are no lore rankings across eras. Results come from the characters' stats alone.
- **D-356 Jedi councils and leadership (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 4; lore: SW_Jedi_Ranks_Councils_Roles_AllEras_Part1.md (P1) and _Part2.md (P2); extends SW_Jedi_Order_Ranks_Council_OldRepublic.md; M7).**
  - *[P1-1] High Council origin:* the founders' councils existed from the Order's start. An inner circle of 12 Masters existed by the Great Hyperspace War (5000 BBY) with no fixed home, and it was formally constituted by 3997 BBY. The "after the Great Sith War" version is the move to a Coruscant spire, not the founding.
  - *[P1-3] Grand Master and Master of the Order:* two offices, often held by one person. Grand Master heads the whole Order, for life or until given up. Master of the Order is the elected leader of the High Council. The engine stores them as two flags an NPC can hold together.
  - *[P1-4] Nomi Sunrider:* recognized as overall leader at Exis Station (3986 BBY); Grand Master in effect, on the Grand Master list.
  - *[P1-5] Fae Coven:* Grand Master from c. 990 BBY for at least a century; end date unset (after 896 BBY).
  - *[P1-6] Yoda:* the office of Grand Master ends with the Order in 19 BBY. Survivors and the narrator may still call him Grand Master until his death (4 ABY), as a courtesy.
  - *[P1-9] Awdrysta Pina:* kept as Grand Master (by 24,500 BBY), [gray].
  - *[P1-11] Legacy era:* Kol Skywalker is "head of the Council". The formal titles (Grand Master, Master of the Order) stay unset; the Master of the Order reading is Wookieepedia's own inference.
  - *[P2-1] Service Corps:* overseen by the Council of Reassignment (confirms D-304).
  - *[P2-2] NJO Masters' Council:* formed during the Yuuzhan Vong War, continued to 35 ABY, formalized as the 12-seat High Council in 36 ABY.
  - *[P2-4] Gray items kept:* the High Council appoints members by unanimous vote (backed by *The Jedi Path*'s rule for all Coruscant Councils), and the Jedi Artisan role.
  - *[P2-10] Anakin on the Council:* the first Knight appointed by a Chancellor. Knights had sat before during the transition to Master (Ki-Adi-Mundi).
  - *Late Republic structure (design default):* the High Council has 12 seats (5 for life, 4 long-term, 3 limited-term), plus the Councils of First Knowledge, Reconciliation and Reassignment (5 seats each). All four are dissolved in 19 BBY.
  - *SWTOR era (default start, c. 3661 BBY):* Grand Master Zym until 3653 BBY, then Satele Shan. The logged Satele dates stand (D-283: Master 3651, Grand Master c. 3648, term ends c. 3636; *amended: Master before 3651, Grand Master 3651*); the new file's 3651–3636 range is consistent with them. Council members are rarely together, for security. Wartime succession: Jedi summoned to replace fallen Councilors often serve only until stability returns. Barsen'thor is a ceremonial title, given only twice before the Cold War-era Consular received it.
- **D-357 Jedi rank and membership (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 4; lore: SW_Jedi_Ranks_Councils_Roles_AllEras_Part1.md and _Part2.md; M7).**
  - *[P1-2] Becoming a Master:* the High Council promotes at its discretion. Usual grounds: training a Padawan to Knighthood, modified Trials, or noted service; some Knights are never promoted. Where Masters may train several apprentices (before c. 3996 BBY, and in the NJO), training several to Knighthood is the common path. Self-declared Masters are accepted in the Old and New Sith Wars and in times of disbandment, with a reputation cost among orthodox NPCs. In the NJO, promotion is by Luke or the Council, for mastery or deeds.
  - *[P1-7] Jedi Lords:* feudal Jedi Lords existed for centuries in the New Sith Wars; the Grand Council period (c. 1100–1000 BBY) is the best documented. The title is renounced at Ruusan.
  - *[P1-8] Order size after the Jedi Civil War:* fewer than 100. Most gathered at Katarr (the "last known hundred") and died there, leaving scattered survivors (Surik and the Lost Jedi).
  - *[P2-3] Order size before the Purge (design default derived from lore):* about 10,000 Knights and Masters in 19 BBY, from "fewer than 100 survivors, about one percent" (*Star Wars Insider* 87). Initiates, Padawans and the Service Corps come on top. Lucas's 1977 "several hundred thousand" predates the prequels and stays [BTS], not used.
  - *[P2-5] Right of Denial:* a Clone Wars Padawan option to refuse a military rank (handing the lightsaber in to the Temple), [last resort].
  - *[P2-6] Sentinel title:* available in every Republic era (it appears in the Jedi Civil War). In the NJO the role exists without the title until it returns by c. 137 ABY.
  - *[P2-7] SWTOR advanced classes:* Sage, Shadow and the rest are flavour names for that era only, with no mechanics of their own.
  - *[P2-9] The Lost Twenty:* only Masters who resigned voluntarily (Dooku the 20th, 32 BBY). Knights who left (Bardan Jusik) aren't counted; exiles and expulsions are separate.
  - *[P2-11] Ossus survivors:* the reconciliation is accepted. About half the Order survived, mostly the half that hadn't withdrawn to Ossus.
  - *Expulsion (design default):* in Republic eras, a hearing before the Council of Reconciliation with testimony. Outcomes: exile, stripped rank, or reassignment to the Service Corps.
- **D-358 Force traditions: catalog mapping and M6 rulings (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 5; lore: SW_Force_Traditions_AllEras_Part1.md and _Part2.md; force-catalog-m6.md §5l).**
  - *[1] Power names:* every power the traditions files name is mapped to its catalog entry (§5l table). Powers with no entry are added as tradition-gated "review" entries at the §3 defaults: Field Detection, *ayna-seff*, Projectile Guidance, Force Imbuing (gear: Jal Shey, Nightsister and Ember of Vahl items), Control Web, Spirit Ichor (NPC/story), Shadow Armor, Elemental Shaping, Parasite Expulsion (unique, NPC), Force Immersion (Fallanassi) and the Ta-Ree powers (Kathol, NPC). Mapped instead of added, because an entry already covers them: Fighting-sight (§5d), the Wyrd's Drain Knowledge (Mind Probe's alias), Cloak of Shadow (a section of the Force cloak page).
  - *Lore-lock amendments:* Aing-Tii flow-walking was taught to an outsider by c. 30 ABY (Jacen Solo, after the Yuuzhan Vong War); fold space taught to outsiders stays from 43.5 ABY (Luke and Ben). Theran Force-listening's page credits Qui-Gon, Obi-Wan and Ahsoka, confirming D-344's loosened lock. Darksight: Rhand plus the Nightsisters ("heartshadow"), matching D-344.
  - *[3] Gand findsmen:* mechanically their divination is Force use (a sensing power), but they believe it isn't the Force; the narrator frames it as religious ritual, like the untrained Voss Mystics.
  - *[4] Kel Dor telepathy:* a lie the Baran Do spread (the latest source, *Outcast*); not a technique.
  - *[5] Aing-Tii fold space:* a Force-sensitive player character may learn it from the Aing-Tii, inside the catalog's era gate. Teaching it to non-Force-sensitives stays NPC-only (the Car'das precedent).
  - *[9] Species abilities:* species traits, not tradition-taught powers: Miraluka Force sight, Neti hibernation, Qom Jha telepathic speech.
  - *[14] Dathomiri spells:* the same catalog powers, but Allyan magic needs a chant, song or ritual: a visible and audible tell and a short casting time, and the witch can't cast while silenced or bound, except prodigies. Nightsister Shadow Magic (Gethzerion's line) needs no chant. Many witches are gifted in only some families of powers.
  - *[17] Potentium:* dark-side techniques still taint, whatever the user believes. The intent discount (D-330) applies only under its own rules, with no extra exemption. Holding Potentium beliefs is heresy for reputation with the Jedi (M7).
  - *[19] Gray Paladins:* a minimal-Force fighter archetype. The Force only augments physical skills; they use blasters, Teräs Käsi, Stava, throwing whorls and spinsticks, treating lightsabers as unreliable. Jedi NPCs see it as heterodox.
- **D-359 Force traditions: history, NPCs and era spawning (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 5; lore: SW_Force_Traditions_AllEras_Part1.md and _Part2.md; M7).**
  - *[2] Legions of Lettow:* disputed in-universe. Jedi sources say Xendor attacked; Arden Lyn's account (via Inquisitor Torbin) says the Jedi raised an army first. The narrator presents both as disputed.
  - *[6] Sorcerers of Tund:* founded c. 4250 BBY (in-universe date). Peña's 10,000 BBY intent stays [BTS], not used.
  - *[7] Mecrosa vs Talzin:* c. 230 BBY accepted. Talzin is several centuries old, consistent with long-lived Nightsister matriarchs.
  - *[8] Kashi Mer:* the dynasty ends in the 25,000 BBY supernova. The Guardians of the Breath survive scattered (still recorded in 990 BBY).
  - *[10] Teepo's Paladins:* still active into the New Republic (*Essential Guide to Warfare*, the more specific source). The Gray Paladins' fate after the Purge is unknown.
  - *[11] Corellian Jedi:* a sect of the Jedi Order with a Corellia-first loyalty flag, not a separate tradition.
  - *[12] Nightsisters:* a recurring name for dark-side exile clans on Dathomir (known by 340 BBY; Talzin's covens in the late Republic). Gethzerion and Baritha founded their own clan in the late Republic.
  - *[13] Zalem's Infinity Gate plot:* 32 BBY.
  - *[15] Antarian Rangers:* a non-Force background. A rare Ranger may have limited sensitivity (the founder Kaskutal did), but there are no Ranger Force techniques.
  - *[16] Iron Knights:* the *Complete Star Wars Encyclopedia* account (the Aqinos page) for all three conflicts. Aqinos was excommunicated before c. 60 BBY and then taught aboard the Chu'unthor; the Iron Knights fought for the Republic as High Marshals in the Clone Wars; Luke approached them in 13 ABY.
  - *[18] Tyia:* an ancient tradition (Thuwisten seen across the galaxy for much of its history) that reached Revyia recently, c. 30 BBY.
  - *Who exists in each era:* Part 2's narrator table is the spawn table for tradition NPCs. "Y" means present, "y" presumed present (optional), "?" or "–" absent unless a story event places them there.
  - *Playable backgrounds and teachers:* the Game notes lists from both parts are recorded under architecture §15 as M7 candidates. Nothing is decided.
- **D-360 SWTOR era: dates and office holders (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 6; lore: SW_SWTOR_Era_Power_Structures_Part1.md (Sith Empire) and _Part2.md (Republic, full timeline); M7 setting data).**
  - *[1] Galactic War start:* 3641 BBY. Taris is overrun in 3642 BBY, as a late Cold War event.
  - *[2] Janarus's term ends:* 3640 BBY (killed). Saresh is elected in an emergency election in 3640 BBY.
  - *[3] Supreme Commanders:* Rans serves through the Great Galactic War; Stantorrs holds the post in the late Cold War (by 3643 BBY); Rans returns at the outbreak of the Galactic War, commands at Ilum, then retires; Jace Malcom takes over in 3640 BBY. This reading fits all four pages.
  - *[12] Player-choice outcomes in SWTOR:* Wookieepedia's implied canon is the default timeline: Janarus killed; Acina dies at Iokath and Vowrawn becomes Emperor (3629 BBY); Galena Rans elected. Player actions in the sandbox can change these (legacy rules).
  - *[13] First Emperor's Voice:* by 3954 BBY (shown in *Revan*). The *TOR Encyclopedia*'s c. 3950 BBY is recorded as a conflict.
  - *[15] Garza and Trant on Bomodon:* 3674 BBY.
- **D-361 How the Sith Empire works, SWTOR era (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 6; lore: SW_SWTOR_Era_Power_Structures_Part1.md; M7).**
  - *[4] Minister of War:* answers to the whole Dark Council and works most closely with the three military Councilors (Offense, Strategy, Defense).
  - *[5] Imperial Reclamation Service:* controlled by the Sphere of Ancient Knowledge.
  - *[6] Minister of Intelligence:* answers to the Councilor of the Sphere of Imperial Intelligence. The post is abolished in 3640 BBY, when Sith Intelligence starts reporting directly to the Council.
  - *[7] Watchers:* analysts who also handle field agents, reporting to the Keeper.
  - *[8] Sith Intelligence:* founded by Darth Marr acting for the Dark Council.
  - *[9] The Emperor's Hand:* the Council knew it existed; only a few Councilors knew its members or purpose.
  - *[10] Grand Moff:* an honorary title with real privileges, including direct access to the Minister of War.
  - *[11] Survivors of the Zakuul conquest:* Acina and Vowrawn. Mortis and Ravage survived in hiding and reappear later.
  - *[14] Imperial Navy ranks:* include Commander. In the Republic Army, "Commander" stays an honorific.
  - *Dark Council structure:* twelve Spheres, with the corrected names from Part 1's table (including Military Offense and Sith Philosophy); Acina's five Spheres after the conquest. A seat passes to the fallen Councilor's apprentice, by the Emperor's appointment, to whoever kills the Councilor, or by unanimous vote of the others.
  - *Kaggath (lore rules, for M7 Sith rivalry):* only a Sith the Council recognizes can declare one. The challenger chooses the arena, each side uses only its own forces, and rule-breakers are executed by uninvolved Sith. Declining costs the decliner their rank. The winner takes or destroys the loser's assets.
  - *Below the Council:* Lords hold power bases inside one Sphere's pyramid: Sith followers, military allies (Moffs) and agency staff. A power base passes to the apprentice when its Lord dies.
  - *Non-Sith ceilings:* the Imperial Guard and the Minister of Intelligence are the highest posts open to non-Sith. A Cipher ranks on a par with Sith below Lord. Non-Force-sensitives are conscripted for life (25–30 years' service). Aliens are admitted to the military from 3640 BBY.
- **D-362 How the Republic works, SWTOR era (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 6; lore: SW_SWTOR_Era_Power_Structures_Part2.md; M7).**
  - *[16] "Planetary Security Council":* dropped; it doesn't exist in Legends.
  - *Republic structure (design default):* the Senate elects the Chancellor (four-year terms, removable by a vote of no confidence) and has final authority over the military. The Supreme Commander advises on and oversees military plans. General Garza commands the Special Forces, and treaty-sensitive missions need the Senate's consent. The SIS (Director Trant from 3650 BBY) works alongside the Jedi. Saresh's emergency rule after c. 3636 BBY cut the Senate out of military matters.
  - *Jedi and the government:* Chancellors ask the High Council directly for help. Jedi may hold military or political posts (Fleet Admiral Oteg, Jun Seros as Janarus's aide). A Jedi delegation stays in the Senate after the move to Tython.
  - *Overlap check (bot decision 17; report only, older decisions unchanged):* no contradictions. Sith side: Part 1's "Darth granted by the Emperor", "Lords above Overseers and below Darths" and "Overseers as instructors, often but not always Lords" match SW_Sith_Academies_Training_OldRepublic.md's rank table and D-308. One nuance: in 3678 BBY the Dark Council itself granted Teneb Kel the rank of Sith Lord and the title Darth Thanaton (Part 1, Dark Council), which reads as the Council acting under the Emperor's allowance. Jedi side: Part 2 is consistent with SW_Jedi_Order_Ranks_Council_OldRepublic.md and D-283 (Satele Master and Council seat 3651, Grand Master c. 3648, term ends c. 3636). One nuance: Part 2 has the interim Chancellor Am-Ris telling "Master Zym" after the treaty that the Temple can't be rebuilt, while the ranks files say Zym was killed "after the fall of the Temple" in 3653 BBY. Both hold if Zym died later in 3653, after that conversation.
- **D-363 The Great Galactic War, setting data (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 7; lore: SW_Great_Galactic_War_Part1.md (3681–3667 BBY), _Part2.md (3666–3660) and _Part3.md (3659–3653)).**
  - *[1] Peace process:* the Dark Council's offer comes in 3654 BBY; the Alderaan talks, the Sacking and the treaty in 3653 BBY.
  - *[2] Grand Master Zym:* survives the Sacking and is killed afterwards (by Braden), consistent with the logged "killed after the fall of the Temple" (and with D-362's nuance).
  - *[3] Bomodon:* 3674 BBY, already logged as D-360 [15]; no new entry.
  - *[4] Hydian Way blockade:* 3661–3660 BBY, broken in 3660.
  - *[5] Mandalore the Lesser:* dies c. 3660 BBY, after the Great Hunt he called; Artus Lok becomes Mandalore the Vindicated.
  - *[6] Mandalorians:* Imperial Intelligence installs Mandalore the Lesser in 3667 BBY; the Mandalorians enter open war in 3661 BBY (the blockade).
  - *[7] Outer Rim:* the Empire holds nearly half of it by c. 3671 BBY. "Finalized" refers to the end of the Seswenna campaign.
  - *[8] Galaxy at the treaty:* roughly half under Sith rule (the main war page). The treaty's territorial terms in the power-structures file stand.
  - *[9] Jace Malcom:* Corporal at Korriban (3681), Captain commanding Havoc Squad at Alderaan (3667). "General" in 3666 is an error; he reaches General later.
  - *[10] Theron Shan:* conceived after Alderaan (3667 BBY), born c. 3666 BBY (*Annihilation*). The author's 3670 BBY tweet stays [BTS], not used.
  - *[11] Battle of Rhen Var:* c. 3653 BBY. Syo Bakarn (born c. 3678) fights there as a Knight, which rules out a date soon after Alderaan.
  - *[12] Havoc Squad:* a Great War unit (at Alderaan in 3667). The "formed after the move to Tython" line is a page error.
  - *[13] Ord Mantell:* no Great War battle there.
  - *[14] Balmorra:* fighting from early in the war (by 3670 BBY at the latest), still contested at the treaty, with the resistance continuing into the Cold War.
  - *[15] Admiral Greik's victory at Bothawui:* 3671 BBY.
  - *[16] First Republic counter-offensive:* the Battle over Korriban (3678 BBY) is the first planned strike into Sith space. The 3680 BBY Seswenna and Minos fighting was defensive.
  - *[17] "Brentaal campaign":* dropped (an error in the brief).
  - *The default start (c. 3661 BBY), M7 input:* the Hydian Way blockade is starving Coruscant's lower levels; riots against the Senate and talk of surrender; a Jedi strike force has been wiped out near Devaron; Satele Shan (a Knight and war hero) has been recalled to Coruscant; the Rim Campaign has begun. The Jedi are still at the Coruscant Temple under Grand Master Zym, and Berooken is in the latter stages of his chancellorship. No source covers Initiate life in wartime, so Temple routine uses the logged *Jedi Path* defaults (D-300).
  - *Cross-check (report only, data unchanged; awaiting Jared's ruling):* eight mismatches between the lore and src/data/canon/era09GreatGalacticWar.ts, listed in docs-v2/testing/ggw-canon-crosscheck.md. Satele a Master from 3653 in the data vs 3651 (D-283); Zym "killed in the Sacking" vs after it; Malgus an acolyte in 3681 vs a Lord, and "leads the Sacking" vs Angral leading it; Syo Bakarn born 3725 vs c. 3678; Gnost-Dural with no Knight period; Mandalore the Lesser missing.
- **D-364 The Coruscant Jedi Temple, c. 3700–3653 BBY (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 8; lore: SW_Coruscant_Jedi_Temple_OldRepublic_Part1.md (the Temple and other Jedi sites); setting data).**
  - *[1] Founding:* a meditative enclave on the sacred spire from c. 5000 BBY; the Four Masters' Temple Ziggurat from c. 3996 BBY (after Ossus), growing for about a thousand years.
  - *[2] Layout in this era: attested core plus tagged inventions.*
    - **Attested (fact):** Temple Court with bronzium statues; Processional Way; main gate and entrance hall with upper mezzanines; Great Hall with hooded statues; four Council spires (High Council Tower, Tower of First Knowledge and two others); First Knowledge quarter (academy wing); small Archives a few storeys above the main entrance; Halls of Healing (a wing; details unattested); Room of a Thousand Fountains (exists "in all incarnations"); detention center; defence-grid mainframe; security recorders; lower levels reachable from The Works.
    - **Functional rooms, invented layout:** Initiate clan quarters, dormitories, a refectory, training salles and meditation rooms existed in function, because the logged *Jedi Path* defaults (clans of about ten, the daily schedule; D-300) require them. Their layout, names and details are campaign inventions, tagged `invented` in the data so the narrator never presents them as canon.
    - **Not available in this era:** the later Temple's named features: the five-spire layout with the Tranquility Spire and Hall of Knighthood, the large Archives (c. 2500 BBY), the Clone Wars Temple Security Force of 500, sublevel hangars and the spaceport tower, the Agricultural Corps lab. The narrator must not describe these before they exist.
  - *[3] Spires in 3653 BBY:* four.
  - *[4] The 3653 BBY destruction:* the exterior and all four towers are ruined, and entire floors of archives, holocrons and manuals are lost. Parts of the interior and the lower levels survive (security recordings among them).
  - *[5] High Council Chamber:* the tower falls, but the chamber's remains survive deep in the ruins (visited in 3643 BBY).
  - *[6] Rebuilding:* c. 3519–3500 BBY; the academy returns from Tython (consistent with D-284). The spires and High Council Tower are rebuilt in 1019 BBY.
  - *[7] Tower of First Knowledge:* northwest; rebuilt 1019 BBY.
  - *[8] Tython:* rediscovered by Satele Shan after the Sacking, consistent with D-283 (Master and Council seat 3651).
  - *[9] Genarius:* the Jedi Enclave purged by the Bane Brigade in the war is kept. The 232 BBY "discovery" line is a source contradiction, not used.
  - *[11] Temple Guard:* a minor presence in this era, tagged [inferred]. Gate security in wartime is Republic soldiers and the Temple Security Force (non-Force-sensitive).
  - *Jedi at the Temple:* no figure is sourced. Most Jedi are deployed in wartime. At the Sacking "dozens" fight in the entrance hall, six Councilors die, and most Knights and Padawans inside are killed. Design default: a reduced wartime population of Masters, instructors, healers, archivists, Initiates and Padawans between assignments; the number is set in M7.
- **D-365 Coruscant, the city, c. 3700–3653 BBY (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 8; lore: SW_Coruscant_Jedi_Temple_OldRepublic_Part2.md (city, law, crime, NPCs, location table); setting data).**
  - *[10] Republic Executive Building:* doesn't exist yet. The 3653 BBY "blackened facade" line refers to another government building.
  - *[12] Yinta Lake spaceport:* on Vulta, not Coruscant (an error in the brief). Coruscant's ports in this era are the Coruscant Spaceport (main) and Liston Spaceport.
  - *Before-and-after gate for the narrator:* Black Sun, the Justicars' Brigade, the Guild-run Old Galactic Market, Black Sun territory and Justicar territory all come after the Sacking (from 3653 BBY). Before 3653, the Galactic Market is a bustling commercial district, and the Migrant Merchants' Guild is a respectable trade body that quietly smuggles and launders money.
  - *Coruscant Security Force (Great War era):* off-white or tan shirt and trousers, grey blast vest and helmet. It runs Coruscant Control and the orbital security stations, and shares Senate security with the military's Senate Security Officers.
  - *No named NPCs* (instructors, healers, merchants, cantinas, gang leaders) are sourced for Coruscant before 3653 BBY apart from the file's NPC table. The narrator may generate them as non-canon NPCs.
- **D-366 Where Imperial Force-sensitives train, 3681–3653 BBY (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 9; lore: SW_Dromund_Kaas_KaasCity_OldRepublic_Part1.md and _Part2.md; setting data).**
  - *[1] Academies:* Korriban is the main acolyte academy from its recapture in 3681 BBY. Force-sensitives found anywhere in the Empire are sent there "willing or not", including Force-sensitive slaves during the war (a sourced route for a slave-born start). The Dromund Kaas Sith Academy (attested before the war for Scourge and Malgus) continues as a lesser academy, tagged [inferred] for the window. The Sith Sanctum in the Citadel is the Sith Order's headquarters.
  - *Imperial-born childhood (design default, consistent with the logged recruitment rule):* wealthy families may hire private Force instructors before the academy (as Malgus had). Families who hide a child's Force-sensitivity are punished (Thana Vesh's family is the sourced example). The age of testing and of departure is not sourced; set in M7.
- **D-367 Kaas City in the window (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 9; lore: SW_Dromund_Kaas_KaasCity_OldRepublic_Part1.md; consistent with D-364's attested/invented rule).**
  - *[2] Layout:* attested places are fact (the Citadel, Sith Sanctum, Dark Council chamber, the Emperor's chambers, parade streets, air taxis, the spaceport with customs). Kaas City wasn't sacked, so long-standing places attested both before and after the window (the Nexus Room, Larvit's store) may be used in the window, tagged "unconfirmed in window". SWTOR Cold War places with no date (the Galactic Trade Center, the opera, Kaas Falls) may be used under the same tag. Invented places get the `invented` flag.
  - *Not available before 3653 BBY:* the Spires of Victory (commissioned after the treaty), Vaiken Spacedock ([13], built in the Cold War), the Kaas City Expansion district and Outpost, Outpost Warden, the six named hubs as a district system, and the reopened Dark Temple.
  - *Dark Temple:* sealed for the whole window (c. 4600 to 3643 BBY). For children it's a forbidden place and a legend.
  - *[3] Hub 3's office:* Ministry of Logistics (post-window detail).
  - *[4] Ministry of Logistics headquarters in the window:* the Imperial Citadel. It moves to Ziost in the Cold War.
  - *[9] Passes and curfew:* none attested. Design default: a citizens-only public transit system with Ministry of Logistics passes, extended back from the Cold War as a campaign invention (tagged `invented`), because it fits the Ministry's long-standing control of transit. No standing curfew; martial law and curfews are emergency measures only (as after the c. 3950 BBY Purge riots).
  - *Weather:* near-constant rain and lightning under permanent cloud, caused by the Emperor's dark-side rituals. Outdoor scenes default to storm.
- **D-368 Dromund Kaas: history, NPCs and society (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 9; lore: SW_Dromund_Kaas_KaasCity_OldRepublic_Part1.md and _Part2.md; setting data).**
  - *[5] Calypho:* arrested on Korriban, held and judged in the Dark Council chamber on Dromund Kaas (c. 3678 BBY).
  - *[6] Malgus:* born on Dromund Kaas in 3700 BBY (matches the canon roster's birth year), raised on his adoptive father's off-world estate, then sent to the Dromund Kaas academy.
  - *[7] Vaiken's terentatek kill:* 4904 BBY.
  - *[8] The 4980 BBY exodus:* a small founding population (eight transports, hundreds of people). The "whole remaining Empire" phrasing is rhetoric.
  - *[10] Location:* the Dromund system (Esstran sector, Stygian Caldera).
  - *[11] Elara Dorne's enlistment:* 3648 BBY.
  - *[12] Ord Radama:* a Republic-held world in the Esstran sector, contested in 3660 BBY (the Harrower Disaster, then the Reconquest). Not an Imperial home world.
  - *Wartime Kaas City:* victory parades and ceremonies (3678 BBY), the Red Legion branded by Darth Ekkage, and the Dark Council headquartered in the Dromund Kaas chamber during the war. The capital is never reached by Republic attack. Late in the war, whole worlds are stripped for the war effort.
  - *Society defaults (general, undated; nothing contradicts them for the window):* Sith purebloods and Humans are treated as master races. Slavery is hereditary and permanent (mostly Twi'leks, Duros and Zabraks). Non-Force-sensitive purebloods are killed or enslaved unless their family buys them a post. Every non-Force-sensitive adult is conscripted, with live-fire jungle training. The Emperor is revered as god and king, and the news is propaganda from the Outreach Bureau.
- **D-369 Lightsaber forms catalog check adopted (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 10; lore: SW_Lightsaber_Forms_Catalog_Check_Part1.md and _Part2.md; report docs-v2/reports/lore-batch-10.md).** The v3 lightsaber content (`SW_Force_Abilities_Forms_v3_complete.md`, LIGHTSABER FORMS and the lightsaber lines elsewhere) is corrected and now matches the SW_Lightsaber_* research set; the 53 matches are unchanged and the design-only items (Part 1 §E) stay as they are. Rulings on the check's 16 decisions:
  - *1. Vaapad before Windu:* in the New Sith Wars "Vaapad" was an old name for Form VII (*Book of Sith*, *Path of Destruction*, reconciled by *Star Wars Insider* 92; consistent with D-350 [3]). Windu's technique (channelling one's own darkness through the blade) is late Republic. Pre-Windu "Vaapad" is an alias of Juyo. Karpyshyn's typo comment stays [BTS].
  - *2. Niman origin:* Kashi Mer Royale Macheteros, then the Legions of Lettow, then the Jedi; "developed from Jar'Kai" noted as the minority version.
  - *3. Niman weaknesses:* relaxed bladework; nearly hopeless against a Makashi user. "No single weakness or strength" deleted.
  - *4. Niman difficulty:* easy to learn, at least 10 years to master (Drallig). Difficulty Advanced → Beginner; the long mastery curve is the design expression. No harness gate is affected: form difficulty is not used in code.
  - *5. Ventress:* restored as a skilled Makashi user, not a master, often with two blades (reverses the 2026-11-20 removal).
  - *6. Makashi:* "footwork-centred, minimal energy expenditure"; "minimal footwork" and "minimal movement" removed.
  - *7. Vaapad mastery:* Billaba became a master; Vos completed his mastery at Saleucami; Windu was the last master after Bulq and Billaba died. The Yoda quote "One student alone, to mastery has risen" is deleted everywhere (no source found, Canon or Legends).
  - *8. Clone Wars Vaapad teachers:* Windu, only with his personal permission; Bulq, unsanctioned. Billaba is not a teacher.
  - *9. Vaapad gate:* D-370.
  - *10. Trispzest:* open to characters who fly or levitate (S'kytri, Maw).
  - *11. Saberstaff:* "does more damage" and "less precise" removed. Legends: wide coverage and surprise against foes with no experience of it; power and penetration actions are hard to apply; elaborate flourishes leave the user open. A damage bonus applies only to thrown saberstaffs. Invented by Sith Lords; Exar Kun is the first recorded user.
  - *12. Revan as a Niman user:* [unclear] in the lore text; nothing mechanical built on it.
  - *13. Telekinetic combat peak:* Darth Traya, with at least three sabers against Meetra Surik. Luke removed.
  - *14. Dun Möch:* "a Sith doctrine; Jedi use is rare and debated". D-350 [8]'s design (minor dark deed with the D-330 intent discount) is unchanged.
  - *15. Tradition column:* Soresu, Ataru, Niman and Sokan become "Jedi origin; also Sith". The learning code blocks no path from them (tested: tests/features/formAccess.test.ts).
  - *16. Missing items:* Juyo-Kos (Juyo variant, Great Galactic War / SWTOR era, Jedi only, Jaric Kaedan; restricted like Juyo; playable, review); reverse Shien grip (a Form V variant; also the Jensaarai's style); personal Form V variants (Bane, Zannah, Vader) and Kas'im's two-saber Ataru (NPC lore notes); Praetoria Vonil and Ishu (Imperial Knight team styles, Legacy era, gated to that tradition, learned on top of the classical forms); the three rings of defence (a technique layer under the NJO styles, flavour only); mounted lightsaber combat (technique note); form alternative names and named moves (D-371); saber variants and sub-group styles cross-referenced to batch 3's files.
  - *Other corrections (Part 1):* Juyo in the Old Republic only for students judged steady, status between frowned-upon and taboo, with era access (Vaunk's hand-picked students; Drallig refusing Anakin) (A16); Galen Marek a Dark Jedi, not Sith (A18); Makashi's "high mental and willpower requirements" removed as unsourced; no stat gate depended on it (A4); the double-bladed Temple Guard pike fights like a saberstaff, the polearm type has no stated weaknesses (A40); free form switch cites "Sidious was a master of all seven forms", the mechanic stays (C1); Saber Barrier needs multiple sabers, Windu with one the exception (D3); protosabers can't be thrown (D1); Form Zero named by Skarch Vaunk, supported by Yoda, Kyle Katarn taught a version (B2); Force Valor "necessary" (D6); Tutaminis "a rare few masters could catch or dispel a blade or bolt" (D8); Je'daii Force-imbued swords kept, [gray] with pre-2014 backing (D10).
- **D-370 Vaapad gate: a teacher or records (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 10, ruling 9; lore: Catalog Check Part1 A19–A26, D11–D13; Forms Part1 Form VII).** Replaces `maceLiving` ("Vaapad teachable"), `maceTrustEstablished` ("Vaapad learning begins"), the Vaapad half of `jediCouncilPermission` and the "Verify Mace Windu alive + Prequel era" trigger with one gate. *Teacher:* Windu's personal permission; Bulq (unsanctioned; a corruption risk on learning); or any living Vaapad master (Billaba, Vos while alive, or a character who has mastered it). *Records:* from the New Jedi Order onward, the recovered records (*The Jedi Path*, the Great Holocron), preserved into the Legacy era. Before Windu creates it, no Vaapad technique exists. Design gates kept: light side only, Juyo 61+. `jediCouncilPermission` stays for Juyo only. *Built:* `src/features/progression/formAccess.ts` (`vaapadAccess`, pure, tested) and the Juyo/Vaapad drill's new `formGate` unlock (Vaapad only; Juyo stays known-only); a new archive record `record:njoFormVII` (Jedi path, from 11 ABY); Vaapad removed from the Sith archive record. *Design defaults:* Vaapad created c. 40 BBY (Legends says only "the late Republic"); the NJO records from c. 11 ABY (D-352 [21]); a teacher counts as a Vaapad master at Vaapad 61+; until alignment exists, the Sith path stands in for "not light side". The sims use neither records nor the Juyo/Vaapad drill, so no harness number moves.
- **D-371 Named maneuvers are flavour in M6 (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 10, ruling 16; lore: Catalog Check Part2 "Missing from v3").** A Named maneuvers table (form alternative names such as Way of the Sarlacc, about 25 named moves, the Tràkata, Sokan and Jar'Kai moves, basic moves and ready stances) is added to the v3 forms section, each tied to its form, for the narrator. They are names on existing actions with no new mechanics; giving signature moves effects is an M7 question (architecture §15).
- **D-372 Yuuzhan Vong personal gear (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 11; lore: SW_YuuzhanVong_Gear_Index.md, _Part1.md to _Part3.md; rulings on Part 3's 14 decisions; built at M8).**
  - *1. Lightsaber vs vonduun armour:* the armour stops slashes and blaster bolts. Thrusts are resisted but can get through with a strong hit. Called shots at the armpit gills, a bare face or the throat bypass it (Mara at Belkadan, Jaina at Ebaq 9). Force lightning destroys it. Crushgaunts crack it through continuous pressure. Bafforr pollen kills armour and wearer, but is unobtainable after Ithor. The Ando shellcrusher carbine is the one blaster that works against it.
  - *2. Amphistaff vs lightsaber:* it can't be severed; a saber can wound it but not cut through it (Luke; the Scepter of Power). It dies from repeated blunt blows to the head or one blow to the centre of the head crest.
  - *3. Jacen on Helska IV:* cloaker plus gnullith.
  - *4. Vacuum:* the cloaker is the vacuum suit. The *Trickster* masquer case is a one-off exception.
  - *5. Jacen's slave seed:* per his own page (Vergere stabbed it in and it tied him to a dhuryam). The hook-of-bone failure was an earlier, separate attempt [inferred].
  - *6. Embrace of Pain after 40 ABY:* Embraces exist in the Legacy era (Krayt, the One Sith, Maladi); "last known" meant known to the Jedi.
  - *7. Yammosks:* they sense the Force and recognise Jedi. Voxyn are the Jedi-hunting creation. A PC hiding from a yammosk needs a Vergere-style "make yourself small" check.
  - *8. Krayt's armour:* an exoskeleton grown from his implants, not vonduun crab armour.
  - *9. Dark Transfer:* works on Vong. Maladi's bioform was a specially engineered exception (One Sith, Legacy era only).
  - *10. Surge coral:* both are true. Slaves normally vanish in the Force. A trained Force user with a Vong-biotech link can, as a rare narrative event, turn it into Vongsense; never a menu choice.
  - *11. Whipsting and finger-spear:* two variants of one shaper-hand weapon; shaper caste only.
  - *12. Fighting claw:* kept, tagged [last resort]; warrior implant, NPC gear.
  - *13. Tokhlor tallow 1.6 kg:* figure dropped; the item stays, any weight design-assigned.
  - *14. Mind tricks:* the general rule applies (D-373).
  - *Vongsense (catalog, review, built at M8):* needs a link to Vong biotech (lambent, slave seed, shaping); works only on Vong and their biots; some users must set aside their Force connection to use it; era 26 ABY onward (amends D-348's "from 25 ABY").
  - *Lambent crystal:* added to the M8 crystal ideas (architecture §15): harvested under planetary or dovin-basal gravity; gives an attuned user a vague sense of Vong. Users: Anakin Solo (26 ABY), Cade Skywalker (137 ABY).
- **D-373 The Force against the Yuuzhan Vong (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 11, ruling 14; lore: SW_YuuzhanVong_Gear_Part3.md; for M6's Force code, applied at M8).** Powers aimed at a Vong's body or mind fail: mind tricks, sensing, choke, a push on the body. What works: telekinesis on objects and the surroundings, including air pressure; telekinesis on gear (masquer, senalak), case by case; Force lightning and Emerald lightning, the dark-side Force net, Dark Transfer; Shatterpoint, partly. Jedi notice Vong as "holes" in the Force. Mara sensing masquered infiltrators stays [unclear]; the narrator may allow it only as noticing a hole. Vongsense (D-372) is the exception for those who have it.
- **D-374 Living-weapon bonding (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 11; design rule, not lore).** A living weapon (amphistaff, tsaisi) is bonded to its captor. A stranger needs a bonding event: fluency in the Vong language (Jariah Syn), Vongsense (Jacen), or capture at the polyp. Without one the weapon is inert or hostile. Villips, cloakers, gnulliths and healing swatches work for anyone. A qahsa needs its genetic key.
- **D-375 The Sith Academy on Korriban (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 12; lore: SW_Korriban_Academy_Part1.md and _Part2.md; rulings on Part 2's 13 decisions).**
  - *1. Main academy:* Korriban is the destination for new acolytes in game from 3681 BBY (as D-366). It is sourced as the main academy from 3653 BBY; for 3681–3653 it is tagged [inferred]. The default start (3661) falls in the inferred stretch, which is acceptable.
  - *2. Dromund Kaas academy, 3681–3640:* a lesser academy, tagged unconfirmed (matches D-366).
  - *3. Founding:* an ancient site (the Ritual Chambers are older); the academy itself was founded in 3996 BBY by Exar Kun's disciples.
  - *4. Admission:* Force-sensitive slaves from the Great Galactic War (the Emperor's decree); aliens in small numbers early (the Rattataki rumour), then widely from the Cold War; every Force-sensitive in Imperial territory by law from the Cold War. In 3661 an alien acolyte is rare and faces open prejudice. The Dromund Kaas files are narrowed accordingly: in wartime only the slave decree is dated, and the all-Force-sensitives law is attested from the Cold War. In game an Imperial-born Force-sensitive in 3661 still goes to Korriban by default, by custom and family ambition rather than law [inferred], with the lesser Dromund Kaas academy as the alternative (amends D-366's wording).
  - *5. Killing rivals:* an "unauthorized murder" is punishable by death. Killings ordered or sanctioned by staff, or protected by a powerful patron, go unpunished. Most acolyte killings are staged as tomb accidents. The killer still takes the dark deed under the M6 rules.
  - *6. Dreshdae:* built over in this era; the three-days'-walk figure is from another era.
  - *7. Library keeper:* whoever heads the Sphere of Ancient Knowledge at the time; Thanaton only from 3640 BBY.
  - *8. Sphere responsible:* the Dark Council owns the academy and the Sphere of Ancient Knowledge supervises it [inferred].
  - *9. Tremel and Harkun:* both killed (c. 3643 and 3640 BBY).
  - *10. Kallig's class:* six.
  - *11. SWTOR protagonists:* the Warrior/Wrath and Inquisitor/Nox are title-only canon NPCs (D-377). Their trials are background events; player characters are other acolytes in the same years.
  - *12. Developer files:* cross-checked (docs-v2/reports/lore-batch-12.md). Where the old files contradicted the new ones, the new files won: Gerr was killed, not "badly injured"; the library keeper is the Sphere's head of the day; the founding conflict is resolved by ruling 3; the all-Force-sensitives law is dated to the Cold War. Old Korriban items the new files don't cover are tagged `unverified` and queued for a check.
  - *13. Tomb of Ludo Kressh:* in the Valley, tagged as attested only in 3951 BBY.
- **D-376 Korriban in 3661 BBY: roster and gap defaults (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 12; lore: SW_Korriban_Academy_Part1.md and _Part2.md).**
  - *On record as active then:* Tremel (an Overseer for decades), Ragate (reopened the Ritual Chambers during the war), Spindrall (hermit in Ajunta Pall's tomb), the Dark Honor Guard, and the Children machinery in the depths. The Republic attack of 3678 BBY is recent history.
  - *Not in post yet:* Harkun (trained there himself; still an acolyte or junior then [inferred]) and Zash. Neither is placed as staff in 3661.
  - *Only in the Cold War:* Rance, Cestus, Sathel, Ardran, Markan, Loun, Samus, Knash, Arzanon. None is placed in 3661.
  - Other staff the start needs are invented, tagged `invented`.
  - *Gap defaults (all `invented`):* age on arrival usually 10 to 18 (Teneb Kel at about 14 is the one anchor); a stay of one to four years, prodigies much faster (as the Warrior and Kallig); an Overseer runs a cohort of 6 or 7, and groups compete for a visiting Lord's notice; the narrator invents meals, wake times and lessons within the attested rooms (dormitories, lecture, meditation and training chambers, library, jail); acolytes wear plain dark robes (invented) while staff wear black and red (on record); the acolyte's weapon is a warblade and the lightsaber comes with apprenticeship (on record). Unchosen acolytes take the sourced outcomes (executed, killed in trials or by rivals, sent home, retrained by Spindrall for re-entry, kept as never-Sith hangers-on, deserting into the tombs); military or Intelligence service and re-enslavement are invented outcomes, used sparingly. Mortality is high: only one of many may survive a trial. The M6 Sith academy run sim takes these as anchors (checklist).
- **D-377 SWTOR protagonists stay NPCs (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 12; closes the open question from lore batch 6).** Players can't take over a SWTOR class-story protagonist. All eight stay title-only canon NPCs whose paths a player character can cross, as allies, rivals, patrons or enemies, consistent with their recorded deeds and dates. *Design backlog (not now):* letting the player become the leader figure in the KOTFE/KOTET era (the Outlander / Alliance Commander role), possibly in place of the canon Outlander; undecided, needs its own design pass (architecture §15).
