- **D-378 Jedi life at the Coruscant Temple, Great Galactic War (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 13; lore: SW_Jedi_Temple_Life_GGW_Part1.md; rulings on its 11 decisions).**
  - *1. Daily-life defaults:* the post-Ruusan *Jedi Path* details are adopted as defaults, tagged "later-era default": Initiate clans (about ten, with fosterers), refectories, the daily schedule (Force studies, politics, history, physical training, meditation), and plain tunics. Not adopted: the Padawan braid and severing ceremony, the annual selection tournament and the age limit for selection, which contradict this era's attested practice (Padawans served several Masters, Knighthood came in the field, older recruits were taken). A narrator description of a Padawan in 3661 has no braid. Wartime colour: many Initiates and Padawans are away with Masters at the front, so the Temple feels emptier than in peacetime [inferred].
  - *2. Satele's Knighthood:* some Trials under Darach in 3681 BBY; a Knight under Dar'Nala, before 3667 BBY (Alderaan). (Relevant to docs-v2/testing/ggw-canon-crosscheck.md #2: the canon data's Knight-from-3677 fits "before 3667".)
  - *3. Invented Temple rooms:* confirmed; any room not on record stays `invented` (D-364).
  - *4. Devaron strike:* heavy losses; survivors, Gnost-Dural among them, retreated to Devaron. The "five squadrons" figure in the GGW file stays, tagged unconfirmed.
  - *5. Council deaths in 3653 BBY:* six in the Sacking; Zym dies afterwards in the ruins (matches D-363 [2]).
  - *6. Recruitment age:* normally young children; older recruits are allowed as exceptions (Kaedan at twelve, Bestros as a youth). This is the rule for a Jedi PC who starts late.
  - *7. Corellian Jedi academy:* included, existing by 3653 BBY, founding date [unclear]. Its distinct customs (green robes, marriage allowed, Jedi Credits) make it a possible alternative Jedi start, noted for M7 (architecture §15).
  - *8. Malachor III:* mid-war, no fixed date. In a 3661 start it may be recent history or a rumour; never placed after 3661.
  - *9. SWTOR protagonists:* the Hero of Tython and the Barsen'thor are excluded from the 3681–3653 setting (they belong to the Cold War), per D-377.
  - *10. Developer files:* cross-checked (docs-v2/reports/lore-batch-13.md). The old daily-life file already separated "general" from "era" facts; its post-Ruusan items now carry the ruling 1 tags; its Padawan-training refocus is corrected per ruling 11; one uncovered item (the Kiffar infant of c. 3661) is tagged `unverified` and queued. The ranks file and Jedi Training Part 1 have nothing in the window that contradicts the new file.
  - *11. Lightsaber Training §2:* rewritten to follow the Padawan page: Core World politics before the war; the shift to military readiness and diplomatic service during wartime liaison with the Republic military; completed in the Cold War after the move to Tython.
  - *Edits to earlier files (the new file's "Changes from the previous file"):* SW_Great_Galactic_War_Part1 "The Jedi at war" gains the Hydian Way strike (command, the Council's reluctance and motives, outcome per ruling 4, the Jedi starfighter fleet in the 3660 relief); SW_Great_Galactic_War_Part3 and SW_Coruscant_Jedi_Temple_OldRepublic_Part1 record the six-vs-half Council deaths conflict, resolved per ruling 5; the Coruscant file marks the Tython decision confirmed (briefly homeless, then Tython) and adds Shol Bestros and the Battlemaster post to the Temple staff.
- **D-379 The Jedi in 3661 BBY: roster (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 13; lore: SW_Jedi_Temple_Life_GGW_Part1.md §D, §G).**
  - *On Coruscant:* Satele Shan (Knight, recalled this year, witnessing the riots); Shol Bestros (mentor); Grand Master Zym [inferred].
  - *At the front:* Ven Zallow (Rim Campaign); Gnost-Dural (the Devaron strike, then the fleet); Orgus Din; Jaric Kaedan; Bela Kiwiiks; Aryn Leneer; Syo Bakarn (a young Padawan, about 17).
  - *Hidden:* Ngani Zho, raising Theron (about 5) away from the Order's eyes.
  - *Location unknown in 3661:* Dar'Nala (on Coruscant in 3671) and Tol Braga; placed where a story needs them, consistent with their recorded deeds.
  - *Dead by 3661:* Darach (3681), Belth Allusis (3671), Wyellett (lost 3665).
  - *Game hooks (Game notes; M7 candidates, architecture §15):* apprenticeship as a sequence of Masters with field postings to specialists; the 3661 Hydian Way crisis as an event chain (Council debate, the Devaron strike, food riots, surrender talk, smuggler relief in 3660); a Padawan PC as an unknowing Child of the Emperor (the Bakarn pattern), a heavy hook flagged for M7 design, not a default.
- **D-380 Coruscant the city, Great Galactic War (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 14; lore: SW_Coruscant_City_GGW_Part1.md; rulings on its 12 decisions).**
  - *1. 3680 BBY riots:* "the Coruscant riots of 3680 BBY" is used as a named event; the name is Wookieepedia's own title, the events are on record.
  - *2. Surrender talk in 3661:* it came from individual senators, many of them, including war hawks. The Senate as a body never formally debated surrender. (SW_Great_Galactic_War_Part2 reworded to "many senators, even the most ardent war hawks, began to suggest surrender", citing the Blockade of the Hydian Way page.)
  - *3. Peace offer:* the offer came in 3654 BBY; the conference and the Sacking in 3653 BBY (D-363 [1]). The Power Structures and GGW files now all read this way.
  - *4. Blockade relief (3660):* both accounts fit: the SIS sent Republic Navy elements and the Jedi starfighter fleet, and starfighters and corvettes from nearby systems joined.
  - *5. Visz's medal:* the Senate organised a ceremony; she didn't attend.
  - *6. Blockade dates:* 3661–3660 BBY. The riots begin in 3661 and end when the blockade breaks in 3660.
  - *7. Senate security:* the CSF plus the military's Senate Security Officers. The narrator may say "Senate guards" informally; the formal Senate Guard (blue robes, Senate Commandos) is later-era and stays out.
  - *8. Underworld Police:* allowed in this window, tagged undated.
  - *9. Garza at Bomodon:* a ten-month campaign spanning 3674–3673 BBY (refines D-360 [15]'s 3674).
  - *10. Pre-Sacking underworld (departs from the bot's best fit):* the named cast stays the Migrant Merchants' Guild (laundering, smuggling), the Exchange (offworld engspice) and the Hutts (money behind blockade runs). Small local gangs are allowed, tagged `invented`, because a lower-level start needs street crime. No invented organisation may be galaxy-wide or fill Black Sun's later role; that space stays empty until after the Sacking (D-365).
  - *11. Rationing, curfews and conscription:* show shortages, soaring prices and volunteer enlistment, as on record. No formal rationing (the riots demanded food precisely because the Republic wasn't supplying it). No conscription (the record says volunteers). No standing curfew (as Kaas City, D-367); short district lockdowns during riots may be narrated, tagged `invented`.
  - *12. Market kiosks and the market air-taxi station:* both named places stay out of 3661. Generic public transport (air taxis, turbolifts, airspeeders) may be narrated as era-neutral city function, with no specific station named (D-364's function rule).
- **D-381 Coruscant in 3661 BBY: non-Jedi roster and hooks (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 14; lore: SW_Coruscant_City_GGW_Part1.md).**
  - *On record:* Senator Dorian Janarus (Coruscant), long-serving in the war; Marcus Trant (SIS field agent; the SIS is now a full espionage bureau, headquartered at the Heorem Complex, start date unclear); the CSF.
  - *Chancellor Berooken:* Chancellor in 3661, per D-363, tagged [inferred]; the sources say only that he was elected late in the war. No source found dates his election after 3661.
  - *Arriving in 3660:* Hylo Visz and her crew, Tyresius Lokai, Zale Barrows and Quan, with the relief convoy.
  - *Elsewhere in 3661:* Elin Garza (Army, at the front), Jace Malcom (front), Nico Okarr.
  - *Game hooks (M7 candidates, architecture §15):* a food-price index by level for 3661–3660 BBY (numbers `invented`; the record gives only "dramatic" rises); the 3661 riots from the street (starving lower-level residents, protests at the Senate, the news of the failed Jedi strike); the 3660 relief (cheering crowds at the freighter landings, an empty podium at the medal ceremony); security tiers (SSOs and CSF in the Senate District, the CSF in the mid-levels, the Underworld Police below).
- **D-382 The Mandalorians in the Great Galactic War (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 15; lore: SW_Mandalorians_GGW_Part1.md; rulings on its 10 decisions).**
  - *1. The Lesser's title:* 3667 BBY. The Empire courted the Mandalorians that year and he claimed the title; the clans rallied on Geonosis over 3667–3665, and the army left a year after that. (The 3665 alternative is recorded as a resolved [conflict] in the GGW files.)
  - *2. Gap before the Lesser:* centuries; no Mand'alor between the Preserver (c. 3951 BBY) and the Lesser.
  - *3. Battle on the Hydian Way:* 3660 BBY; the blockade runs 3661–3660 (D-380 [6]).
  - *4. The Vindicated's accession:* c. 3660 BBY, right after the blockade's defeat and the Great Hunt. The *Encyclopedia*'s "after the Treaty" stays [BTS].
  - *5. Crusader's schism:* under the Vindicated, c. 3660–3652 BBY [inferred from Cadera's "nearly a decade" in hiding before c. 3642]. In 3661 it hasn't happened: Clans Ordo, Itera, Kelborn and Cadera are dissenters in waiting, still loyal in form.
  - *6. Great Hunt:* both kept. The Defenestrator won a Hunt between 3678 and 3668; the Lesser's c. 3660 Hunt "revived" the contest as a Mandalorian rallying event ("revived" read loosely).
  - *7. Culture from other eras:* allowed as flavour, tagged `[other era]`: Resol'nare, Mando'a vocabulary, adoption, nomad life, food, verd'goten. The old Neo-Crusader zealotry (outsiders inducted under threat of death) had faded by this era.
  - *8. Beskar:* rare in this window (design default, tagged `invented`). Most warriors wear ordinary Mandalorian armour; full beskar is a mark of wealth or standing. The state of Mandalore and its mines is unrecorded and isn't described beyond the file.
  - *9. Republic-side Mandalorian PCs:* allowed as exceptions. Most clans refused the Sith in 3667 and stayed independent nomads, so a nomad mercenary on Republic contracts is plausible. No institutional backing; the Lesser's followers see them as traitors. After 3660 the schism clans are the natural route, at the cost of the stigma of arue'tal.
  - *10. Kyramud and Davaab craft:* excluded from this war; blockade ships are Mandalorian cruisers and support ships.
  - *Edits to earlier files:* the Lesser's 3665 alternative recorded and resolved in GGW Part1 and Part3; the Crusader's schism row in GGW Part2 marked and resolved; GGW Part2's dead pointer to the Power Structures file now points to SW_Mandalorians_GGW_Part1; the Defenestrator vs "revived" Hunt conflict flagged and resolved in GGW Part1; the Jedi Temple file's skirmish years widened to c. 3664–3661 BBY; the sub-group styles file gains the Mandalorian Jedi hunter who attacked Syo Bakarn's Master as this era's example. Batch 14's surrender re-cite was already in place.
- **D-383 Mandalorians in 3661 BBY: roster and M7 start options (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 15; lore: SW_Mandalorians_GGW_Part1.md).**
  - *Mandalore the Lesser:* commanding the blockade (the stretch between Devaron and the Minos Cluster). His real name is unknown; the narrator never gives one.
  - *Artus Lok:* rising under the Lesser; exact whereabouts unknown.
  - *Shae Vizla:* an Imperial contractor for heavy-risk work, hating Jedi since her brother's death (c. 3664); placed where a story needs her, consistent with her recorded deeds.
  - *Jicoln Cadera:* Clan Cadera chief, not yet in revolt; whereabouts unknown.
  - *The warship* Beskad: lost this year.
  - *Mandalorian Jedi hunters:* active (the Bakarn pattern).
  - *M7 start options (architecture §15):* a nomad mercenary who refused the Sith in 3667; a blockade warrior under the Lesser; a member of one of the future schism clans; a Vizla-style Imperial contractor; a Republic-side exception (ruling 9). Design hooks: blockade picket duty (hail, warn, intercept; sourced), a stigma track (arue'tal), and the rare death duel (Geroya be Haran, a Cold War custom).
- **D-384 Nar Shaddaa and Hutt Space in the Great Galactic War (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 16; lore: SW_NarShaddaa_HuttSpace_GGW_Part1.md; rulings on its 10 decisions).**
  - *1. Supreme Mogul:* Karagga the Unyielding, ruling from Nal Hutta, tagged [inferred] for the window (from "nearly two centuries by the Cold War").
  - *2. Barrga's Rendili deal:* during the war, before the blockade run, following the order of Visz's career (the *Crimson Fleece* lost on Corellia, then the *Vanguard*, then the blockade).
  - *3. The Exchange's defeat by the Shadow Syndicate:* c. 3643 BBY, after the window.
  - *4. The Lesser's Great Hunt:* stays c. 3660 BBY (D-382 [6]). The sources for c. 3660 (the Lesser, Great Hunt c. 3660 and Vindicated biography pages, plus the game, the Holonet site and *The Journal of Master Gnost-Dural*) outnumber those for "after the Treaty" (the Great Hunt competition page, the Vindicated intro and the *Encyclopedia*), recorded as the minority version. The three wartime Hunts on the competition page (the Defenestrator, Bloodworthy, a third) stand. Either way the Lesser is Mandalore in 3661.
  - *5. Quesh breach:* c. 3643 BBY, after the window; the Hutt–Imperial treaty is unbroken throughout the war.
  - *6. Operation: Undertow:* c. 3664 BBY. Watcher X's move to Shadow Town is later and undated; Shadow Town doesn't appear in 3661.
  - *7. Nar Shaddaa districts:* never sacked, so D-367's looser Kaas City rule applies, not Coruscant's. Districts attested in the Cold War may appear in 3661, tagged "unconfirmed in window", without their Cold War occupants and events: the Corellian, Nikto and Duros Sectors, the Promenade, the Red Light Sector, Network Access. Out, because they depend on post-Treaty developments: Shadow Town (Imperial prison slum), the Bounty Brokers Association, the Republic Embassy (the treaty forbids the Hutts to further Republic interests), the Shadow Syndicate. The Refugee Sector and the Jekk'Jekk Tarr belong to the 3951 BBY refugee wave; out unless a story needs an old ruin, tagged `invented`. Always available: the Undercity, the Cartel docks and shipyards, an unnamed cantina.
  - *8. Tatooine:* not Hutt-ruled in this window (Hutt control comes in 65 BBY). The Exchange holds Mos Ila, founded during the war, so in 3661 it may be newly founded or not yet taken [unclear].
  - *9. Hutt currency:* allowed in Hutt Space as flavour (peggats, truguts, wupiupi), tagged `[other era]`; credits remain the main money.
  - *10. Visz's hiding:* c. 3659 BBY [inferred].
  - *Edits to earlier files:* a Hutt Cartel pointer entry in SW_SWTOR_Era_Power_Structures_Part1; Operation: Undertow and Watcher X tagged per ruling 6 in GGW Part2; "no direct Hutt combat role" kept in GGW Part1–3 with a cross-reference for the Hutt funding of the blockade run, Barrga's drive sale and the Xizor hire; Bloodworthy and the minority "after the Treaty" dating added to the Mandalorian file's Great Hunt notes; the Coruscant city file gains the Hutts collecting a year after the run and the Xizor hire, and Visz's refugee transport now "stopped at Nar Shaddaa, bound for more peaceful regions".
- **D-385 The underworld in 3661 BBY: roster and M7 start options (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 16; lore: SW_NarShaddaa_HuttSpace_GGW_Part1.md).**
  - *Nal Hutta:* Karagga [inferred]; Suudaa Nem'ro (Jiguuna).
  - *Nar Shaddaa:* Barrga, based there, employing Visz before the Corellia debacle; Hylo Visz, a young Cartel smuggler working toward independence, on the moon or around Ord Mantell; Tyresius Lokai, Zale Barrows and Quan freelancing, before the 3660 run.
  - *Rising:* Nok Drayen, from small-time smuggler to syndicate lord; his massacre of half the Cartel is undated, so it isn't placed before 3661 without a reason.
  - *Bounty hunters:* Braden is active.
  - *On Coruscant:* Jewl'a Nightbringer, a child in the lower levels during the food riots (a future Hunt champion).
  - *Not yet:* the SIS bureau under Marcus Trant (from 3654); the Shadow Syndicate; the Bounty Brokers Association.
  - *M7 start options (architecture §15):* three starting homes (Undercity orphan, Cartel dock mechanic, freelance smuggler; all sourced to Visz's life); faction hooks (a Hutt patron, neutral and paying both ways; the Exchange, debt-driven; Drayen's rising syndicate; Imperial Diplomatic Service courting from 3667); a debt track (Hutt investment expects a return; Exchange debt escalates; figures `invented`); bounty work (low Republic payouts and heavy taxes push hunters to underworld contracts).
- **D-386 The Republic Military in the Great Galactic War (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 17; lore: SW_Republic_Military_GGW_Part1.md; rulings on its 11 decisions).**
  - *1. Supreme Commander in 3661:* Rans, tagged [inferred] (the only holder named for the war). Agrees with D-360 [3] (Rans, then Stantorrs late in the Cold War, Rans again briefly, Malcom from 3640).
  - *2. Havoc Squad:* a Great War unit (Infantry Squad 326, named "Havoc" under Malcom); the post-Tython origin on the Havoc page's History section is the minority version. Agrees with D-363 [12]. Its commander in 3661 is Jek Kardan [inferred], with Tavus a member since 3667.
  - *3. Special Forces:* founded during the Great War (Garza a founder; Omas, Kaedan's team, Tavus); the trooper page's post-Treaty origin is the minority version.
  - *4. Malcom's rank:* both pages fit as a sequence: a colonel after Alderaan (3667) and a general by Hoth (3665) [inferred]; in 3661 he is General Malcom. **Differs from D-363 [9]**, which called "General" in 3666 an error and said he reached General later. This ruling applies; D-363 [9]'s "error" reading is superseded and **flagged for review** (docs-v2/reports/lore-batch-17.md). Captain at Alderaan in 3667 still stands from D-363 [9].
  - *5. Training:* 18 weeks of basic training, then up to 12 months of specialist training, used for this window, tagged [conflict] (attested for the Cold War).
  - *6. Garza in 3661:* not placed by the sources. She commanded Special Forces during the war; placed where a story needs her, within Special Forces or the Minos Cluster, tagged.
  - *7. Korr's discharge:* after the deaths and injury in his family, sometime before 3653. Default for 3661: still serving [inferred].
  - *8. Numbered fleets:* Republic fleets stay unnumbered. If a story needs a number, invent one tagged `invented` (e.g. "Third Fleet"); never "Seventh" (the war's Seventh Fleet is Imperial; the Republic's is a Clone Wars fleet).
  - *9. Officer schools:* Navy officers train at the Coruscant Naval Academy. The Army officer school is unnamed; if a name is needed, it is invented and tagged. Carida isn't used in this window.
  - *10. First Battle of Bothawui:* 3671 BBY (Allusis killed 3671). Agrees with D-363 [15]; Greik's c. 3670 is the minority version.
  - *11. Blockade route:* the Hydian Way. The SIS page's "Rimma Trade Route" is recorded as an error or variant, not a second route.
  - *Edits to earlier files:* Power Structures Part2 tags the training figures [conflict] (ruling 5), records the Great War Havoc evidence and the Special Forces origin as resolved (rulings 2, 3), and adds the SIS's 3661 Shange abduction and the Outer Rim listening posts behind the 3668 Dread Masters capture; GGW Part3's who-was-where gains Garza's Medal of Valor (Minos Cluster, c. 3667–3665) and the Hoth page's pre-3665 "General" for Malcom, read per ruling 4; the Bomodon notes in the GGW and Coruscant files cite the 45th Infantry page for 3673 (the D-380 [9] span stands); the GGW and Nar Shaddaa files note the "Rimma Trade Route" variant. No file called the *Star of Coruscant* a medal (it is a superdreadnaught lost at Hoth, 3665), and none gave the Republic a Seventh Fleet; no correction was needed. The war's medals are the Medal of Valor, the Silver Crescent, the Mark of Dedication and the Chancellor's Service Medal.
- **D-387 Republic Military: gap defaults and the 3661 roster (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 17; lore: SW_Republic_Military_GGW_Part1.md).**
  - *Gap defaults (all `invented` unless noted):* ranks from Power Structures Part2's Cold War list, tagged "later-attested", insignia described plainly with no invented detail; no ground vehicles are attested, so generic transports, speeders and armour are narrated unnamed; the Navy pays better than the ground forces (sourced), other pay, leave and discipline details are the narrator's; rations are the Cold War's four days of field rations, "later-attested"; medal order Mark of Dedication, Silver Crescent, Medal of Valor, Chancellor's Service Medal (only the Medal of Valor and the Chancellor's medal are called prestigious in the sources); soldiers' views of the enemy have no general statement on record, so the narrator draws on events (Hoth, the Dread Masters, the blockade) without stating a doctrine.
  - *Command:* Supreme Commander Rans [inferred]; General Jace Malcom [inferred].
  - *Havoc Squad:* Jek Kardan commanding [inferred]; Harron Tavus a member. As the war nears its third decade, Tavus and Orgus Din lead forces in the Minos Cluster (the Karideph win).
  - *Special Forces:* Elin Garza (placement open, ruling 6).
  - *Jedi with the military:* Ven Zallow (Rim Campaign, c. 3660); Jaric Kaedan.
  - *SIS:* Marcus Trant, a field agent. This year the SIS abducts the Cathar prince Shange and holds him in stasis on Belsavis.
  - *Possibly still serving:* Zeerid Korr [inferred].
  - *Dead:* Laren Omas (Hoth, 3665).
  - *The Navy:* still reeling from Hoth; the *Intercessor* is one of the few ships that escaped intact.
  - *M7 start options (architecture §15):* a trooper (a post-Alderaan recruit of c. 3667, a veteran of four to six years in 3661, among Hoth survivors' stories); a pilot (Liberators off a Valor-class cruiser; the *Intercessor* carried 110 fighters); a spy (an SIS start inside the 3661 Shange operation, a morally gray abduction).
- **D-388 The Imperial Military and Imperial Intelligence in the Great Galactic War (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 18; lore: SW_Imperial_Military_Intelligence_GGW_Part1.md; rulings on its 10 decisions).**
  - *1. Commanders in 3661:* no Moff, admiral or general is named for that year; none is invented as "the" commander. Grand Moff Zellos and Admiral Layek Davos can appear as serving officers, tagged [inferred]. Ministers: Shareis, Minister of War, in post [inferred from 3660]; Shullis Khamarr, Minister of Logistics, whose page says she held the post during the war, so possibly in post in 3661, tagged [unclear].
  - *2. Navy ranks:* the Imperial Navy page's list (the longest, Grand Admiral to Private), with its sub-grades such as "Captain First-Class". Refines D-361 [14] (which kept Commander from one list).
  - *3. Grand Moff:* a real wartime rank (Zellos, 3671), which could also be conferred as an honour (Kilran after the war); both uses stand. Refines D-361 [10] ("an honorary title with real privileges").
  - *4. Armour colour:* gray is the standard. The sourced variants are unit markings: black and white for commandos, green for grenadiers, black and red also attested. The Imperial Guard wear white with red.
  - *5. Rim Campaign and Ord Radama:* c. 3660 BBY, tagged [gray] (the date comes from the FFG adventure *Onslaught at Arda I*). The *Book of Sith* author's 3655 BBY intent stays [BTS]. The blockade (3661–3660) and the Rim Campaign overlap at the start of 3660.
  - *6. Operation: Undertow and Fixer Seven:* c. 3664 BBY. Watcher X's forced retirement to Shadow Town comes later, undated, and Shadow Town is out of 3661 (D-384 [6]), so in 3661 Watcher X is in post or under review [unclear].
  - *7. Soldiers' training:* the sourced route is conscription at adulthood, live-fire exercises in the Dromund Kaas jungles within weeks, then 25–30 years of service. A naval officer's first command is often a Gage-class transport, and demotion back to one is a known punishment. An officer academy may be invented if a story needs one, tagged `invented`; never the Canon or Galactic Empire "Imperial Academy".
  - *8. Terminus-class destroyer:* allowed as the Harrower's escort, tagged [unclear] (undated on the general pages; its own page says "by the Cold War"). The Harrower and the Gage remain the attested core.
  - *9. Keeper and Minister of Intelligence in 3661:* unnamed; the narrator uses the titles only.
  - *10. Aliens in the military:* none in the regular forces in 3661; formal admission comes after Ilum in 3640 (consistent with D-361). Routes for an alien Imperial PC: a Chiss agent or liaison (the Ascendancy is the Empire's only true ally; Chiss Imperial Agents are attested); a Force-sensitive slave sent to Korriban (D-375 [4]); an informer or asset outside the formal ranks. Field hospitals treated only favoured species (Humans, Chiss, Duros, Kaleesh, Twi'leks, Aqualish, Zabraks, Purebloods), a detail the narrator can use.
  - *Edits to earlier files:* Power Structures Part1 gains the third Navy rank list (ruling 2), Zellos against Kilran's honorary title (ruling 3), the armour-colour conflict and officers' code cylinders (ruling 4), and Broysc and Zellos in its Moffs "Not found" line; GGW Part3's who-was-where adds Shareis, Khamarr, Zellos, Watcher X and the Eagle (Druckenwell, 3653); the Ord Radama date is tagged [gray] in GGW Part2 and both Dromund Kaas files (ruling 5); the Republic Military file gains the Sacking page's "by order of Supreme Commander Rans"; the Nar Shaddaa file's Undertow row is resolved (ruling 6), noting that Fixer Seven's page also calls him a Cold War Fixer. No file treated the Mk VI interceptor (the Mark VI Supremacy-class "ISF interceptor", the standard fighter) and the Fury-class (a separate transport-style interceptor used by Sith as a mobile base) as one ship; no correction was needed.
- **D-389 Non-Sith Imperials in 3661 BBY: roster and M7 start options (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 18; lore: SW_Imperial_Military_Intelligence_GGW_Part1.md).**
  - *Ministers:* Shareis (War) [inferred]; Khamarr (Logistics) [unclear].
  - *Officers:* Zellos and Layek Davos [inferred, serving]; Broysc (between Talay and his later Moff rank); Rycus Kilran, a rising Navy officer [inferred].
  - *Garrison:* Vasil Dorne, garrison commander since 3665.
  - *Young:* Malavai Quinn, about 19, newly serving.
  - *Army sniper:* Vrath Xizor.
  - *Imperial Intelligence:* Watcher X (after Undertow; status unclear); Fixer Seven (recovering from his Undertow injuries [unclear]); Eckard Lokin (Fixer Fifteen). The Ministry of Intelligence planned the blockade now running and handles Mandalore the Lesser.
  - *Imperial Diplomatic Service:* still courting the underworld (since 3667), with little success.
  - *M7 start options (architecture §15):* a soldier (a conscript aged about 18–40 in the 25–30-year term; a Rim Campaign posting gives Ord Radama's rise and fall as an arc); a junior naval officer (captain of a Gage-class transport, with demotion back to one as a setback); an agent (a Minder or Fixer under a Watcher, handling Mandalorians during the blockade). Design hook (a mechanic, not lore): a "Sith displeasure" track for officers serving under a Sith Lord.
- **D-363 [9] amended (Jared, 2026-11-21; ruling on the lore batches 3–18 run; supersedes D-363 [9]'s reading of "General" in 3666 BBY as an error).** Jace Malcom was a Captain at Alderaan (3667 BBY), a colonel after Alderaan and a general by Hoth (3665 BBY), and General Malcom in 3661 BBY, all [inferred] (D-386 ruling 4 stands).
- **D-370 amended (Jared, 2026-11-21; ruling on the lore batches 3–18 run).** Accepted as design defaults: Vaapad created c. 40 BBY, tagged as a design default because Legends says only "the late Republic"; the Sith path as the stand-in for "not light side" until alignment exists. The switch to the real light-side check is M6 integration checklist item 31. Also confirmed: batch 3's "restricted availability class" is the forbidden class (D-346); M7 and M8 items stay in architecture §15 for now; the ShatterSense rename (D-349) doesn't rewrite history (old decisions, progress.md, legacy/ and Docs/ stay as they were); the code's ability sides lagging the catalog is M6 checklist item 32.
- **D-390 Era 09 characters: rulings on the 20 decisions (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 19; lore: SW_Characters_09_GGW_ColdWar_Index.md and Part1–3b, which replace the pre-bot SW_Characters_09_GreatGalacticWar_ColdWar.md, retired as OLD_).**
  - *1. Zym's death:* killed by the bounty hunter Braden on Coruscant after the Treaty, 3653 BBY; fits "Zym dies afterwards, in the ruins" (D-378 [5]; "killed by Braden" added to the Jedi Temple file and to the 3661 roster, D-379). The Kiwiiks page's "in the Sacking" is the minority version.
  - *2. Syo Bakarn:* born c. 3678 BBY.
  - *3. Bela Kiwiiks:* a Master from 3663 BBY, including at Rhen Var.
  - *4. Warren Sedoru:* an acclaimed Knight in the war, a Master by c. 3642 BBY.
  - *5. Satele's knighting:* under Dar'Nala, before 3667 BBY (D-378 [2]).
  - *6. Vitiate's defeat on Dromund Kaas:* 3640 BBY, matching the Power Structures timeline; the old 3637 is dropped as unsourced.
  - *7. Darth Jadus:* born 3688 BBY, tagged "speculated" (as the infobox marks it).
  - *8. Darth Angral:* a Sith Lord, not a Dark Councilor; oversaw the Rim Campaign with the Council and led the Sacking fleet.
  - *9. Darth Thanaton:* on the Dark Council from c. 3643 BBY until his death in 3640 BBY; before that a Darth in the Sphere of Ancient Knowledge (D-375 [7]'s library-keeper ruling stands).
  - *10. Darth Nyriss:* Era 08 (died 3950 BBY); her old entry moved to SW_Characters_08_DarkWars_SithTriumvirate.md, with a note that its forms (Makashi, Shii-Cho) contradict her page, which names Juyo.
  - *11. Supreme Commanders:* D-360 [3]'s order stands (Rans; Stantorrs late in the Cold War; Rans again briefly; Malcom from 3640). Rans in 3661 is [inferred] (D-386 [1]).
  - *12. Elara Dorne:* born 3669 BBY (her own infobox); the Dromund Kaas file's "3665 BBY" row fixed.
  - *13. Theron's conception:* after Alderaan (3667 BBY), fitting his 3666 birth; "after Korriban" is the minority version (D-363 [10]).
  - *14. Bomodon:* D-380 [9] stands, a ten-month campaign spanning 3674–3673 BBY.
  - *15. Janarus:* Wookieepedia's default: killed by the Hunter in 3640 BBY aboard the *Founder*; the game's survival branch isn't used.
  - *16. Mandalore the Lesser:* died c. 3660 BBY; Artus Lok is Mandalore from then (D-382), so the Vindicated is Mandalore at the Sacking.
  - *17. Vasil Dorne:* born c. 3703 BBY (page text); c. 3706 is the infobox variant.
  - *18. Suudaa Nem'ro:* died c. 3641 BBY (his own infobox).
  - *19. Hunter (Star Cabal):* died 3640 BBY aboard the *Star Chamber*; who killed her isn't stated until confirmed (Cipher Nine is title-only).
  - *20. Section order:* the bot's template order is fine.
  - *Batch 7 cross-check (docs-v2/testing/ggw-canon-crosscheck.md; Jared's ruling: the new files win):* the seven open mismatches are settled in D-391 (each listed in docs-v2/reports/lore-batch-19.md). One difference with D-283 is flagged for review: the new file has Satele made Grand Master in 3651 BBY, where D-283 has Master and a Council seat in 3651 and Grand Master c. 3648.
- **D-391 The Era 09 canon roster rebuilt from the new files (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 19; src/data/canon/era09GreatGalacticWar.ts, era09Additions.ts, era09Status.ts; types.ts).**
  - *Corrected facts on the 45 existing sheets:* species (Vindican and Vowrawn Purebloods; Exal Kressh a Sith hybrid, species 'other'; Dar'Nala a Togruta; Eleena Daru a Rutian Twi'lek; the seven title-only protagonists have no species); dates (Baras born 3697, died 3640; Jadus born 3688 "speculated", faked his death 3643, no death recorded; Marr died c. 3636; Revan born c. 3994, died c. 3642 temporarily and 3637, the Emperor's prisoner to c. 3643; Vindican died 3681; Vowrawn born 3716; Thanaton born 3695, died 3640; Kilran born c. 3692, died c. 3641; Orgus Din and Angral died 3643; Mekhis died 3643 in the Vesla system; Dar'Nala died 3653 on Dantooine; Tol Braga and Kaedan died 3640; Acina died 3629; Shae Vizla died c. 3622; Aric Jorgan born 3673; Kira Carsen born 3663; Vitiate's defeat 3640); fates (Malgus survives Ilum; Ngani Zho killed c. 3643 in the Vesla system, not by Malgus; the Dread Masters prisoners on Belsavis 3668 to c. 3641); roles (Angral a Sith Lord; Gnost-Dural never "Keeper of the Archives"; Kaedan never Battlemaster; Kilran a captain at the Sacking, Grand Moff only after; Saresh Chancellor only from 3640; the Lesser, not the Vindicated, reunited the clans). Darth Nyriss removed (Era 08).
  - *Canon IDs follow the new headings*, with the old IDs kept as `formerCanonIds` so links made under the old file still resolve: lore:zym (was lore:grand-master-zym), lore:orgus-din, lore:darnala, lore:ngani-zho, lore:gnost-dural, lore:scourge (was lore:lord-scourge), lore:dread-masters, lore:rycus-kilran (was lore:grand-moff-kilran), lore:hero-of-tython, lore:emperor-wrath, lore:the-grand-champion, lore:voidhound.
  - *The five changed estimates:* Kao Cen Darach's main form Jar'Kai (not Ataru); Jaric Kaedan's Juyo-Kos (entered as juyo, a Juyo variant per D-369) with battle meditation; Darth Vowrawn's combat ratings lowered (age had probably weakened him), politics and charisma kept; Mandalore the Vindicated's "reunified the clans" dropped; Darth Nyriss removed. Forms and powers added where the sources name them: Gnost-Dural (Soresu, Djem So, Juyo), Scourge (Soresu; Juyo and Ataru already there), Baras (Soresu, Shien), Satele (telekinesis and tutaminis already there; foresight and telepathy recorded). Unsourced old forms stay as estimates.
  - *138 new figures* (era09Additions.ts, generated from the master index and the entries): sourced facts (species, sex, homeworld, birth and death, ranks, teachers, forms and powers, alignment) in a `facts` field; role, archetype and missing birth years are design (birth estimate: an adult of 25, or 45 for a Master or high office, at the earliest year the files date them; eight hand overrides listed in the report). No levels are hand set: 100 active adults in 3661 BBY are `provisional` and take the npc-generation closed form for their archetype and age (src/features/npcs/selectors.ts), for Jared's review; 38 children, unborn or dead in 3661 BBY are `factsOnly`, aging in the same way when they appear.
  - *Status snapshots:* every one of the 183 figures carries `status3661` (active, child with age, unborn, dead, imprisoned, or not stated, with the lore wording) from the master index, and `status3653` from the entries: the "who is on the board" filter for the default start.
  - *M6 hand-set alignment (checklist item 14):* the files' alignment and corruption notes are recorded in `facts.alignment`: Dar'Nala's red eyes from 3653; Zash's yellow eyes and decaying body behind a Force mask; Mekhis visibly warped; Revan's yellow eyes in his Sith past; Sajar and Praven redeemed; Tol Braga's fall in 3640.
  - *Harness:* no lifetime, spread, mortals, severs, sweep or Orgus suite reads the canon roster, so no gate can move and none was re-run.
- **D-392 The lore parser reads the research bot's character layout (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 19; src/lore/parse.ts, ac27899).** Era character files in the bot's layout (`###` entries with "**Type:** character" bullets, the era in the H1 title, minor figures in tables found by header) are indexed alongside the old layout: citations stripped from story text; ";"-separated parenthetical names, quoted epithets and "X and Y" pairs become aliases; an alias two figures of one set share is dropped; each part of a split set gets its own file key (c09p1, c09p2, c09p3a, c09p3b; the Index c09i adds no chunks); master index and "old estimate" tables add nothing. The old layout is unchanged (the other eras' files). Index after the switch: 47 old Era 09 chunks removed, 183 added (70 entries, 113 table figures), 1 added in Era 08 (Darth Nyriss), none changed; no Era 09 figure appears twice.
- **D-283 amended (Jared, 2026-11-21; settles the flag in D-390).** Satele Shan was made Grand Master in 3,651 BBY, after she rediscovered Tython, matching SW_Characters_09_GGW_ColdWar (lore batch 19) and the 3700–3600 BBY timeline (lore batch 20). Her elevation to Master is undated: recorded as "before 3,651 BBY". The c. 3,648 BBY Grand Master date is removed; D-306's and D-356's references to it are superseded. The rest of D-283 stands (a Knight in 3,661 and at the 3,653 peace talks; term ends c. 3,636). Canon data: her earlierRoles already end the Knight role at 3,652, so no data change.
- **D-393 Galaxy timeline slice 1 (c. 3,700–3,600 BBY): rulings on the 23 decisions (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 20; lore: SW_Timeline_3700_3600BBY_Part1–3.md, which replace SW_Legends_Galaxy_Timeline.md's rows for these years).** The rows in Parts 1 and 2 were edited to follow the rulings; Part 3 records them.
  - *1. Section order:* fine as it is; the bot need not ask again (noted in Part 3 and in the queue's timeline brief).
  - *2. Year labels* and *6. era ranges:* D-394.
  - *3. Downstream column:* dropped. It was a game value with no source; event priorities, if a system needs them, belong in game data.
  - *4. Added headings:* accepted (the GALACTIC WAR (SECOND) Overview, single-year headings 3,665–3,653, "3,676–3,672 BBY — Years 5-9", "Undated within the war (3,681–3,653 BBY)").
  - *5. New era block:* "LEGACY OF THE SITH — 3,624–3,600 BBY" accepted; the last dated event is c. 3,622 BBY and the rest of the range is empty on record.
  - *7. Date format:* the timeline keeps "3,681 BBY"; the parser reads both forms (D-396).
  - *8. Sim seeds and Legacy framework:* D-395.
  - *9. Blockade end:* 3,660 BBY (batches 14 and 15).
  - *10. Mandalore the Lesser:* took the title in 3,667 BBY; killed by Artus Lok c. 3,660 BBY (batch 15).
  - *11. Peace offer:* 3,654 BBY; the Alderaan conference and the Sacking are 3,653 BBY (batches 7 and 14).
  - *12. Zym:* killed by Braden on Coruscant after the Treaty, 3,653 BBY (batch 19).
  - *13. Janarus:* elected 3,653 BBY shortly after the Treaty (the *Encyclopedia*'s "immediately"; Am-Ris interim until then), re-elected 3,649 and 3,645 BBY, killed 3,640 BBY. A four-year term fits the elections listed for 3,653, 3,645 and 3,641.
  - *14. Satele as Grand Master:* 3,651 BBY ("by 3,645 BBY" is consistent; D-283 amended).
  - *15. Galactic War start:* the Treaty collapses midway through 3,641 BBY. The 3,642 BBY events (Balmorra freed, Taris invaded, the Maelstrom raid) are the Cold War's last flare-ups (batch 6); those rows moved into the COLD WAR block.
  - *16. Rhen Var:* the sources say only "the war's final years". Game default c. 3,654–3,653 BBY, tagged [inferred], matching the character files' "around 3,653"; not an active front in 3,661. The row moved from 3,660 to 3,654 BBY.
  - *17. Minos campaign:* fighting ends with the peace offer in 3,654 BBY; the Empire withdraws in 3,653 BBY. Both stand.
  - *18. Chiss alliance:* in place from the start of the war (the Empire's only true ally from 3,681, batch 18); the formal treaty terms were negotiated during the war. Both stand.
  - *19. Belsavis:* the Imperial invasion and the Dread Masters' release in 3,641 BBY; Darth Ekkage dies there in 3,641.
  - *20. Deaths at the war's start:* each follows its event. Ardun Kothe 3,641 (the Quesh war); Wyellett 3,641 (fighting on Hoth); Rycus Kilran 3,642 (the Maelstrom raid).
  - *21. Later SWTOR dates:* Iokath rediscovered 3,631 BBY, the war on Iokath 3,629; the Mek-Sha missions span 3,627–3,626; Plan Zero spans 3,642–3,641 (Power Structures' 3,643 corrected).
  - *22. Player-choice branches:* the version on Wookieepedia's year pages (Acina dies and Vowrawn becomes Emperor at Iokath; Malcom lives). The class protagonists' outcomes are background, by title only. Whether the player can take the KOTFE/KOTET leader role stays on the design backlog (batch 12).
  - *23. Minor dates:* Bomodon 3,674–3,673 (batch 14); First Bothawui 3,671 (batch 17); Elara Dorne born 3,669 (batch 19).
  - *Matching edits elsewhere:* the Era 09 character files and the canon roster (Kothe, Wyellett and Ekkage die 3,641; Kilran 3,642; Janarus elected 3,653, re-elected 3,649 and 3,645; Silara and Gaul Panteer die "several years after 3,653", the roster's death year an estimate of 3,650 in place of the generated 3,653); SW_SWTOR_Era_Power_Structures Part1 and Part2 (Plan Zero 3,642–3,641, Belsavis 3,641, Galactic War from mid-3,641); SW_Great_Galactic_War_Part1 (the Chiss timing note; the Minos Cluster Campaign row from 3,680, when the Jedi fleet arrived; Second Bothawui a Sith retreat, not a draw); SW_Great_Galactic_War_Part3 and SW_Republic_Military_GGW_Part1 (Rhen Var c. 3,654–3,653 [inferred]). The 3,661 statuses: Ngani Zho is active, taking Theron to Monastery c. 3,661 (was "not stated"); Theron's note adds it. Prince Shange and Lord Raxxus are not in the roster (the timeline row covers them); the Rim Campaign notes already say c. 3,660. Darth Malgus's unavailable window now ends 3,629 (revealed alive at Ossus c. 3,628, Part 2; it said "resurfaces ~3,629" from the old timeline). Source citations that named old timeline line numbers ("Timeline :NNN") in src/data and two docs now point to the slice or to the shifted lines.
  - *Not checked, by choice:* the old rows the bot skipped (the Senate accepting the peace offer over Jedi warnings, the Jedi returning to Coruscant, the "tribute" to Zakuul) stay unchecked in Part 3.
- **D-394 Timeline year labels and era ranges (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 20, rulings 2 and 6).** War years are counted Year = 3,681 − year: 3,681 BBY is "Year 0 (war begins)", 3,680 Year 1, 3,671 Year 10, 3,661 Year 20, 3,653 Year 28, which fits the "twenty-eight-year war" and the ATC count; the two old labels that broke the rule (3,681 = Year 1, 3,680 = Year 2) are fixed. The only code that reads the labels is the lore parser's "year N" dating of overview sentences (D-98), which maps any N, including 0 (tested). The era ranges no longer overlap: COLD WAR 3,653–3,642; GALACTIC WAR (SECOND) 3,642–3,636 (the Treaty collapses midway through 3,641); WAR AGAINST ZAKUUL 3,636–3,630; POST-ETERNAL EMPIRE / LATE SWTOR ERA 3,630–3,625 (its "~" dropped); LEGACY OF THE SITH 3,624–3,600. Rows moved to match: the 3,642 rows into the Cold War, and the 3,629–3,628 rows (Iokath, the Machine Gods, Theron's false defection, the Order of Zildrog) into the Post-Eternal block. Era slugs, and so the lore index's chunk ids, are unchanged.
- **D-395 The timeline's sim seeds and Legacy Change framework moved to spec 14 (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 20, ruling 8).** Both blocks left the lore file for docs-v2/spec/14-world-state-and-events.md ("Moved from the lore timeline"). No code reads either: the lore index skipped them as non-era sections, and nothing in src/ uses them; they wait for the galaxy simulation and Legacy Change specs. The faction seeds are re-dated from 3,660 to the 3,661 BBY start: the Hydian Way blockade active (`blockadeActive: true`), the Coruscant riots ongoing, Mandalore the Lesser leading the clans, the Rim Campaign starting. The unsourced comments ("Temple restaffing", "rationing easing") are dropped. The numbers are the old game values, unchanged and unreviewed. The framework text is unchanged; its CRITICAL tags were the dropped Downstream column (D-393 [3]).
- **D-396 The lore parser reads the timeline slices (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 20; src/lore/parse.ts).** `SW_Timeline_*_PartN.md` files join the timeline (file key `tl`, read after SW_Legends_Galaxy_Timeline.md as one timeline, so era ids stay stable and an era both files open, Old Republic Recovery, merges its overviews and rows). Table columns are found by header, so the old (Date | Event | Key Figures | Location | Downstream) and new (Date | Event | Type | Place | Who | Source) layouts both read. Nothing consumed the Downstream value (the parser never read the fifth column), so nothing changes when it is missing. Story text drops citations, research tags ([conflict], [inferred], [unclear], [BTS]), asides about the files ("(see … row)", "(SW_… gives …)", "(ruling 16)"), "[conflict] …" notes after a sentence, and overview sentences about the file itself ("the rows below", "the old file", "year pages"). A year's "State of the galaxy" paragraph becomes that year's first row. "Year 0" headings and "between X and Y BBY" dates read; years with or without the thousands comma were already accepted and are now tested. An overview sentence naming its era's own span dates to the era's start (D-98 refinement: "a war from 3,681 BBY to 3,653 BBY" is the era's description, not its end). Place cells drop their asides before splitting ("Balmorra (Empire → Republic)" is one place); " to " splits a route ("Brentaal IV to Denon"); "other" is not a place. Row text shows the place even when no one is named.
- **D-397 Rulings on the lore batch 20 questions (Jared, 2026-11-21).** (1) *Order seat 3,653–3,650 BBY:* the ruined Jedi Temple on Coruscant, flagged `relocating` in src/data/orderSeats.ts; Tython from 3,650 BBY per the timeline slice (it had Tython from 3,653). (2) *Xerender* dies in 3,641 BBY with Wyellett in the fighting on Hoth (ruling 20's principle; character files and roster). (3) *Silara and Gaul Panteer:* death year 3,650 BBY stands, tagged [inferred] with the reason ("several years after 3,653", read as about three) in the character files and the roster. The superseded rows stay in docs-v2/lore/superseded/.
- **D-398 Species files in the lore index, and the species data rebuilt (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 21; src/lore/parse.ts, src/lore/retrieve.ts, src/data/species.ts).**
  - *Parser:* `SW_Species_*_PartN.md` files get a file key each (sp-oldrepublic-p1, -p2, -p3). Each `###` entry with a "**Type:** species" bullet becomes a new `species` chunk kind, dated by the era in the H1 title, with citations stripped. Its names are the heading, its parenthesised names and plurals; plain "sith" is dropped, since it names the Order far more often, so the species answers to "Sith Pureblood(s)", "Sith hybrid(s)" and "Pureblood(s)". Species chunks have no base score and no place matching: they are sent only when the species is named (scene names or the player's words), so they never crowd out era lore. Part 3 and the reading notes add nothing. Index: 45 species chunks added, nothing else changed.
  - *Data:* src/data/species.ts goes from 12 to 45 species. Every lifespan and maturity has an `ageSource` tag (lore / game stat / house default, plus `unverified` for unchecked infoboxes) and a basis citing the species file (D-399 [1]–[6], [19]). `playable` marks the 38 offered at creation, and `knownFrom` is the era gate (Voss -3645). `ageingSigns` holds the three flavour signs. `speciesOfferedAt(year)` gives the creation list; computeCreation rejects other species for players, and the creation form lists only those offered for the start year. NPCs may be any species in the table. The 33 new species have no traits (traits are design, M8) and bodyScale 1.0.
  - *How the bands become the two numbers* (lifespan = the age at Human-equivalent 100; maturity = the age at Human-equivalent 18): the stated average lifespan when lore gives one, else the game book's venerable age or stated maximum, else the Human 100. Maturity is the book's adult age, else 18. This matches the earlier design's Togruta (95 = venerable 95+).
  - *Changed from the earlier design values:* Chiss 110/18 → 80/10; Zabrak 100 → 80; Miraluka 100 → 84; Cathar 100 → 90; Sith Pureblood 120 → 100. Human, Twi'lek, Mirialan, Togruta, Rattataki, Mon Calamari and Wookiee keep their numbers. Wookiee maturity stays at the earlier design's 60, tagged house default: the books' adult 18 and the prime at about 200 cannot both fit the linear age map (a question for Jared).
  - *NPC effect:* canon NPCs of species that were missing from the table aged as Humans (creationSpecies' fallback). They now use their own bands. The roster has Hutt ×3, Nautolan ×2, Duros, Cerean, Trandoshan and Jawa. The roster's speciesId still says 'other' for figures whose species is new to the table (Gand, Kaleesh, Talz, Epicanthix and others), because the NPC entity enum (entities.config.ts) does not list them.
  - *Era 09 data (rulings 9–13):* a `facts.conflicts` field added (types.ts), filled for Yuun, Xalek, Broonmark and Ula Vii, with Exal Kressh's note extended. The birth years and Exal Kressh's hybrid species already matched the rulings, so no value changed.
- **D-399 Species of the Old Republic era: rulings on the 17 decisions and the batch's own rulings 1–21 (Jared, 2026-11-21; lore batch 21; lore: SW_Species_OldRepublic_Part1–3.md).** The bracketed numbers in code and specs are the batch's ruling numbers.
  - *[1] Game-book age bands* (mostly UAA) are the default ageing tables where no lore exists, tagged `game stat`; a lore figure wins.
  - *[2] Human baseline:* the 15 species with no lifespan source get the Human bands (100 / 18) as a `house default`: Purebloods, Twi'leks, Mirialans, Rattataki, Mon Calamari, Rodians, Nikto, Devaronians, Houk, Sullustans, Voss, Gormak, Gektl, Echani and Sarkhai. Revisit if a source turns up (queued, low priority).
  - *[3] Falleen:* most live to about 250; 400 is the rare maximum.
  - *[4] Wookiees:* prime around 200; soft cap about 400, with rare cases beyond. Bowdaar, about 177 in 3,661 BBY, is in his prime.
  - *[5] Force-sensitive longevity* (up to about 200, about 300 with elite medicine) applies to Humans and Human-baseline species only, not to species with their own lifespan data (Hutts, Wookiees, Chiss, Falleen, Yoda's species, Epicanthix) until a scaled rule is designed. The 800-year-old Jedi is an exception. Design item: m6-integration-checklist.md item 33.
  - *[6] Zabraks:* the SWTOR codex average of 80, tagged `lore`; any maximum comes from game stats.
  - *[7]* Scourge's immortality and Khem Val's stasis are not species traits.
  - *[8] Ageing signs* (flavour, tagged): Trandoshans stop regrowing limbs at about 54 (game stat; mechanical when limb loss exists); Sullustan eyes fail after 30 (lore); old Purebloods mottle and their tendrils wither (lore).
  - *[9]–[13] Character files:* the character's own page wins. Yuun born 3,670 BBY (Gand page 3,668, a conflict); Xalek born 3,665 BBY (Kalee page 3,664, a conflict); Exal Kressh a hybrid (the species page's Pureblood line is a conflict); the Cold War Talz, Broonmark included, are kept (the "found around 15 BBY" line is a later rediscovery); the Cold War Imperial Epicanthix spies, Ula Vii included, are kept (the Palpatine-era "first contact" is the later Empire's).
  - *[14] Ryloth* is Hutt-controlled in 3,661 BBY [inferred]; tag it inferred wherever it appears.
  - *[15] Voss:* no Voss player characters before the rediscovery c. 3,645 BBY; a backstory exception is not offered at creation for the 3,661 start; Voss NPCs before 3,645 need a story reason.
  - *[16]* The Mother Machine origin of Twi'leks and Zabraks is an in-world rumour only.
  - *[17] Cerean:* the gray-window Force rate and language detail are accepted.
  - *[18] Homeworld control in 3,661:* left blank where the files don't state it; local colour may be invented, tagged `invented`, and must not contradict galactic control.
  - *[19] Aqualish and Gran ages:* kept, tagged `game stat`, "unverified".
  - *[20] Creation:* the 35 playable-ready species plus Mirialan, Rattataki and Voss (gated by [15]). Houk, Gormak, Gektl, Yoda's species, Sarkhai, Echani and Aqualish are NPC-only. Cyborgs are a variant; Zakuulans and rakghouls are not playable.
  - *[21] Era gating:* a species is offered only if its people are known to the side and era of the start; Voss is the only gated case for 3,661. Later start eras need the same check (23-species.md).
  - *The bot's section-order question (decision 17):* the template order is fine.
- **D-400 M7 begins: the playable 3,661 BBY start, first pass (Jared, 2026-11-21; docs-v2/spec/m7-start-checklist.md).** The checklist turns architecture §15's M7 start options and the 3,661 settlements of lore batches 12–21 into items. This pass builds the items that need no design decision:
  - *Default start (amends D-155's "no default year", per Jared's M7 brief; confirmation asked):* the new-campaign form opens on 1 January 3,661 BBY with a 14-year-old and the Great Galactic War era preselected (`DEFAULT_START_DATE`, `DEFAULT_START_AGE` in src/turn/campaign.ts). Only a prefill: any era or date can be chosen, and no rule favours a year.
  - *The board (src/engine/canonRoster.ts `canonOffBoard`, `canonBoardAt`):* a canon figure is on the board when the lore puts them there. That means "active" in the 3,661 snapshot (lore batch 19), or, with no stated status and in every other year, placed by birth, death and unavailable years. Children are on the board as children. The dead, unborn, imprisoned and out of sight are off it, and the snapshot wins over the dates where it says dead, unborn or imprisoned. The `npcCanonLinked` handler refuses a link to an off-board figure and gives the reason, so the NPC stays a story NPC.
  - *Data fixes for the board:* Wyellett is unavailable 3,665–3,643 (D-379: lost in 3,665; found in 3,642). The Hero of Tython and the Barsen'thor are unavailable 3,681–3,654 (D-378 [9]).
  - *Control and leaders (src/data/worldControl.ts):* 29 dated control spans over 27 places, plus the leaders in office, from the timeline slice and lore batches 12–21. Every entry has a source, readings the sources only imply are tagged inferred, and places the files leave unstated are left out (D-399 [18]). The narrator's state block gains a "Leaders:" line and, for the current location chain, a "Control:" line.
  - *Species at creation:* already built in lore batch 21 (D-399 [20], [21]).
  - *The spec 14 seed review* is in the checklist; no seed value changed (waiting for Jared).
  - *Harness:* the sweep and the lifetime suite were run. Neither reads non-Human species or the canon roster, so no gate can move; both pass (results in docs-v2/reports/m7-start-1.md).
- **D-401 Rulings on the M7 first-pass questions, and what was built (Jared, 2026-11-22; docs-v2/reports/m7-start-1.md questions 1–10, lore batch 21 questions 1–3).**
  - *[1] D-155 amended:* 3,661 BBY is the default start date (D-400 stands).
  - *[2] Start options are mechanical:* each sets location, side, rank and opening threads, with an editable premise text. No start puts the player in a SWTOR protagonist's role. Start locations are seeded as data. **Built:**
    - src/data/startOptions.ts: 17 starts for 3,661 (two Jedi, three Sith/Imperial-born, two Mandalorian, three underworld, a Coruscant lower-levels start, trooper, pilot, SIS agent, and three Imperial non-Sith), each from an architecture §15 note and its decisions. Places not on record are flagged `invented`.
    - computeCreation's choices are checked against the start (path, rank, archetype).
    - createCampaign seeds the places (outermost first, the character in the innermost) and the opening threads as engine events. `locationIntroduced` and `threadOpened` now accept the engine source.
    - The start is recorded on the character (`start`: option, side, Imperial-born). The new-campaign form has a start picker, which fills the premise.
  - *[3] Pre-placement:* only figures whose 3,661 location is on record. **Built** as src/data/canonPlacements.ts, a data table rather than NPC records (an NPC record would show the figure as already met): Tremel, Spindrall and Ragate on Korriban; Karagga on Nal Hutta; Ngani Zho and Theron on Monastery; the Dread Masters on Belsavis. Karagga is placed on Nal Hutta, not Nar Shaddaa, because the lore says he "ruled from a palace on Nal Hutta"; Nar Shaddaa has only his statue (SW_NarShaddaa_HuttSpace_GGW_Part1). Ragate is placed by name, as he is not in the roster.
  - *[4] The narrator's view of the board:* only the figures at the current location ("Canon figures here"), plus a "Board warning" line for each story NPC named after an off-board canon figure (at most three). No full roster. **Built** in the state block.
  - *[5] Imprisoned figures:* a canon link is allowed when the scene is at their prison, and the NPC gets the new status `imprisoned`. **Built:** `prisonOf` and `canonOffBoard` read the placements' prison spans in any year (the Dread Masters 3,668–3,642), ahead of the generic unavailable spans. Revan (the Emperor's prisoner) has no prison place on record, so his link stays refused.
  - *[6] SWTOR protagonists:* the Emperor's Wrath, Darth Nox, Cipher Nine, the Hunter (Grand Champion) and Havoc's commander are kept out of 3,681–3,653 (D-378 [9] reasoning); companions such as Theron stay. **Built** as unavailable spans 3,681–3,654. Havoc's commander is `lore:meteor`; the M7 report's question 6 had wrongly labelled Voidhound, the Smuggler, as Havoc's commander. Voidhound gets the same span under the ruling's intent, all SWTOR protagonists.
  - *[7]* The blockade applies as standing state at the start (D-400). The event chain (the blockade broken in 3,660, the Rim Campaign) goes to M8: docs-v2/spec/m8-world-checklist.md item 1.
  - *[8] Era rules:* built in M7 as src/data/eraRules.ts. Nine rules from the topical rulings (Coruscant shortage, security and places; the Nar Shaddaa districts; Tatooine; Jedi life; the Korriban Academy; the Mandalorians; the Imperial forces), each sent only when its place is in the location chain or its faction is in play. The factions in play are the character's start side (else the path's) and those of the NPCs present.
  - *[9] Seeds:* the five proposed values and the three additions are accepted and written into spec 14.
  - *[10] Sith Purebloods* need an Imperial origin at the 3,661 start: Imperial, or an Imperial-born defector or underworld character. Republic and Jedi starts are refused. **Built:** each start's `pureblood` rule (`allowed`, `imperialBornOnly`, `refused`), the Imperial-born mark on the form, and for a free start in 3,681–3,653 a Pureblood needs the Sith path.
  - *[11] Wookiee maturity* stays 60 (house default); separate "adult" and "prime" fields go to M8 (m8-world-checklist.md item 2).
  - *[12] Body scales* for clear size outliers, tagged house default (`bodyScaleSource`): Hutt 1.5, Talz 1.25 (2–2.5 m), Houk 1.25 (2.2 m), Jawa 0.6 (1 m), Yoda's species 0.5 (under 0.7 m), with the Wookiee's 1.25 at 2.1 m as the yardstick. The rest stay 1.0 until the M8 traits (m8-world-checklist.md item 3).
  - *[13] NPC species list* extended with the 21 species of the table it lacked (entities.config.ts), and the ten figures now carry their species: Nadia Grell Sarkhai, Darth Karrid Falleen, Khem Val Dashade, Xalek Kaleesh, Tanno Vik Weequay, Yuun Gand, Fideltin Rusk Chagrian, Ula Vii Epicanthix, Broonmark Talz, Gault Rennow Devaronian.
- **D-402 Planets, routes and travel of the Old Republic era: rulings 1–18 (Jared, 2026-11-22; lore batch 22; lore: SW_Planets_Travel_OldRepublic_Part1–6.md).** The bracketed numbers in code and specs are the batch's ruling numbers.
  - *[1] Battle on the Hydian Way:* 3,660 BBY, confirming D-393 [9]. The blockade runs 3,661–3,660; "3661" on the battle page is a recorded conflict.
  - *[2] Serenno* falls c. 3,660 BBY in the Rim Campaign; "about 3661" (the Serenno page) is a conflict. In 3,661 Serenno is Republic-held, with Ven Zallow's base: added to the control table and as a canon placement.
  - *[3] Travel table:* approved as a design item, tagged `invented`. Travel follows lanes as a sum of hops at Class 1 base times; class multiplies; off-lane costs time and risk; plotting takes hours; climb-out adds sublight time; hazards (Deep Core slow, Maelstrom sublight, Ruhnuk cut off from 3,663, asteroid fields); the Hydian Way south of Devaron is closed at the 3,661 start, and smugglers can run it at high risk. The draft numbers are in m7-start-checklist.md §F, **not wired into play until Jared approves them.**
  - *[4] Hyperdrive classes:* the *Ascendant Spear* at Class 0.5 is lore; the KOTOR Campaign Guide's Class 2 for the *Hammerhead*, *Aurek*, *Foray* and *Kandosii* may be used in 3,661 (`game stat`); unrated ships take a `house default` class from the draft.
  - *[5] Dromund Kaas in 3,661:* the Republic knows the name (from 3,668) but has no usable coordinates; safe from Republic reprisal through the war (first Republic assault 3,640). Republic characters can't travel there at the 3,661 start without a reason (capture, a stolen Imperial navicomputer); Imperials travel there normally.
  - *[6] Quesh:* not on public charts in 3,661; at most in Republic survey records, unsettled, no Hutt interest; important c. 3,643.
  - *[7] Gated at the 3,661 start,* each with its opening year: Tython (after 3,653, Satele's route), Voss and Denova (after the Treaty), Belsavis (Republic-secret; the Empire learns of it in the Galactic War; Republic characters only with a story reason), the Maelstrom station (Imperial-secret until 3,642), Sebaddon (secret, from 3,658), Rishi (the Rishi Maze), Nathema (routes erased), Zakuul (3,636), Odessen and Iokath (c. 3,631), Obus VI (uncharted), Mek-Sha (after 3,638), Port Nowhere (Cold War or later), Ruhnuk (cut off from 3,663), Yavin 4 (remote; story reason only).
  - *[8] Ilum:* a Republic and Jedi staging world on difficult lanes, on the Republic's charts only.
  - *[9] Odacer-Faustin:* no Imperial academy placed there at the 3,661 start (first dated student 3,655); Imperial space with no academy named (an era rule).
  - *[10] Kaleesh (amends D-399 [21]):* the Kalee page reads "Not stated; Kaleesh only recently contacted by the Galactic War" (SW_Planets_Travel_OldRepublic_Part2, Kalee). That does not put contact before 3,661, so the gate stands: Kaleesh are NPC-only at the 3,661 start, gated like the Voss (`knownFrom` 3,641, the Galactic War; the year is a design reading, flagged).
  - *[11] SWTOR-era places on open worlds* may be used, tagged "unconfirmed in window", unless they exist only because of a later event (the Taris resettlement, the Tython Temple, Mek-Sha, Vaiken Spacedock). The looser Kaas City and Nar Shaddaa rule now applies to every world; Coruscant keeps its strict rule. (An always-on era rule.)
  - *[12] Population figures* are background tagged with their era; the narrator gives only orders of magnitude. (Dropped from the planet text sent to the narrator; same always-on rule.)
  - *[13] Mention-only worlds* are background map nodes with control blank; travel there is allowed; local colour may be invented (`invented`) without contradicting galactic control. Worlds whose only grid is Canon-only stay off the map, but can be mentioned.
  - *[14] Control "not on record"* stays blank; Part A's [inferred] tags are kept.
  - *[15] Routes:* the B1 routes are the trunk network; the B2 routes are drawn, flagged unattested, existing in 3,661 only if founded earlier; the Pabol Hutta (Canon) is left out.
  - *[16] Devaron and the Hydian Way:* the blockaded stretch is "between Devaron and the Minos Cluster", a stretch of space, not a stop list; Devaron stays on the Corellian Trade Spine.
  - *[17], [18] Developer files:* SW_Dromund_Kaas_KaasCity_OldRepublic_Part1 and Part2 move grid R-5 out of "Canon only" (the Legends page gives R-5, Essential Atlas 2009); Part2's Ord Radama loses "Seat of the Empire" (Outer Rim / Radama Void, Esstran sector, Q-5; a Republic world in 3,661, its "Imperial governorship" from later sources). Each file gains a "Changes from the previous file" section naming batch 22 (neither had one).
- **D-403 The lore parser reads the planets files (Jared, 2026-11-22; lore batch 22; src/lore/parse.ts).** `SW_Planets_Travel_*_PartN.md` get a file key each (pl-oldrepublic-p1..p6). Each `###` planet entry ("Type: celestial body" or a numbered "1. Location" field) becomes a new `planet` chunk, located at the planet, so it is sent when the scene is there or the planet is named. Each route entry ("Endpoints", "Founded" or a hyperroute type) becomes a `route` chunk, sent only when named. Planets and routes keep their own locations (the index-wide location tagging skips them, since an entry names many places). Citations, cross-reference pointers, population figures ([12]) and the big tables (master table, mention-only index, B2 list, the 725 sources) are left out. Each event line is dated by its own leading date (`datedEventPieces`: a sentence opening with "3653 BBY:" or "c. 3643 BBY:" takes that year, a range its start; undated fields stay visible), so a 3,661 game never sees the Sacking in the Coruscant entry (D-98's mechanism, not monotonic). Index: 192 chunks added (182 planets, 10 routes), nothing else changed.
- **D-404 The galaxy map data, the closed worlds and the travel draft (Jared, 2026-11-22; lore batch 22; src/data/galaxyMap.ts, galaxyMap.generated.ts, scripts/gen-galaxy-map.mjs).** Map data didn't exist as such before this batch; the M7 start places and control table are location data, so the batch's "add the planets" branch was taken.
  - *Generated* (re-run the script when the files change): 182 master-table worlds with region, grid, sector, system, routes, the 3,661 control statement (field 2 in full, blank when not on record), the 3,653 change where Part A records one, role code, gating note and full or thin; 407 mention-only background nodes (20 Canon-only-grid worlds kept off the map); 22 attested routes (the Hydian Way flagged for the blockade); 73 unattested infobox routes.
  - *Curated:* `WORLD_GATES` (rulings 5–8) with opening years and who each is closed to; `closedWorlds(side, bby)`; the state block lists the closed worlds for the character's side (start faction, else path) and gives the reason when the scene is at one. Era rules added: the always-on places-and-populations rule ([11], [12]) and Odacer-Faustin ([9]).
  - *Travel:* the draft table (base hop times by region, class multiplier, Deep Core ×3, off-lane ×4 plus a 3-hour plot, 1-hour climb-out each end, asteroid ×1.5, the Maelstrom sublight only, the blockade closed) and house-default classes are in m7-start-checklist.md §F, all `invented`, waiting for approval; checklist item 26 tracks the travel system.
  - *Spellings:* no planet in the files is spelled differently from the roster, the character files, the timeline or the control table. Two worlds named elsewhere are not in the planets files: Varn (Guss Tuno's birthplace, SW_Characters_09_GGW_ColdWar_Part3a) and Kintan (the Nikto homeworld, SW_Species_OldRepublic_Part2).
- **D-405 Rulings on the lore batch 22 questions, travel wired into play, era rank names, the map fix, and the rest of early access in M8 (Jared, 2026-11-22).**
  - *[1] Kaleesh gate:* 3,641 stays, tagged [inferred] (species.ts note, 23-species.md).
  - *[2] Travel:* approved with two changes, all numbers still `invented`.
    - **Hop time:** the region value is the minimum, plus time per grid square crossed beyond the first: Core 3 h, Colonies and Inner Rim 4 h, Expansion and Mid Rim 6 h, Outer Rim and Hutt Space 8 h, Wild Space and Unknown Regions 15 h. A hop across regions uses the slower rate.
    - **Classes:** common freighter 2, modified smuggler ship 1.5, civilian transport 3; the rest as drafted.
    - **Built (m7-start-checklist.md item 26):**
      - src/engine/travel.ts (pure; the grid distance moves one square in any direction, diagonals included) and src/data/travel.config.ts;
      - src/turn/travel.ts: a "travel" intent from the current location's map world to a targeted, referenced or named map world takes the engine's time, which passes through the normal span path. A requested span is overridden and noted.
      - Refused, with no time passing: a world closed to the character's side (D-402 [5]–[8]), or a routine trip across the blockade.
      - The blockade corridor is read as grid columns L–N, rows 14–21, from Devaron's square to the Minos Cluster (an invented reading of "between Devaron and the Minos Cluster"). Mandalorians and Imperials pass; running it for anyone else is M8 (m8-world-checklist.md item 4).
      - A destination not on the map leaves the time to the narrator, as before.
      - The ship is a common freighter until ships are data (M8), and the smuggler start's ship is a modified smuggler ship.
      - The narrator gets a "Travel:" line in the turn results.
    - **Anchor, re-run:** Coruscant (L-9) to Alderaan (M-10) is one square, a single Core hop at the 4-hour minimum plus 2 hours of climb-out. That is 10 h in a common freighter, 8 h at Class 1.5 and 6 h at Class 1, against the Imperial-era guide of about 16 h. The guide's route clips the Deep Core, which a hop between two Core Worlds doesn't see.
  - *[3] The galaxy map generator:* Varn (O-6, Quelii sector) and Kintan (S-9, Si'Klaata Cluster) are in the mention-only index (Part 4). The generator ran the citation stripper on the name cells, and every index name is a link, so it erased them.
    - **The damage:** no row was dropped (all 427 were kept, which is why the count test passed), but 398 mention-only names and all 20 off-map names came out empty.
    - **The fix:** names now keep their link text (`linkText`).
    - **Duplicates:** the file also lists two worlds in both the master table and the index (Malachor V, Mandellia); the master-table entry now wins.
    - **Result:** 182 worlds, 406 mention-only nodes and 19 off-map names, every name non-empty (tested; Varn and Kintan checked). D-404's spelling note, "not in the planets files", is superseded: both are in the index. The lore index was unaffected (it doesn't read the tables).
  - *[4] Era rank names (m7-start-checklist.md item 15):* built as specified in architecture §15. src/data/eraRanks.ts holds the Jedi and Sith ladders by era from the cross-era tables (D-298, D-299, D-308; SW_Jedi_Training_AllEras, SW_Jedi_Ranks_Councils_Roles_AllEras, SW_Sith_Training_AllEras), keyed by year. Overlaps are allowed (Vitiate's Empire alongside Kun's Brotherhood). The state block gets a "Rank names in this era" line.
  - *[5] M8, the rest of early access* (docs-v2/spec/m8-world-checklist.md):
    - world events: the 3,661–3,660 chain, plus the 3,653 Sacking of Coruscant and the Treaty of Coruscant as scheduled events (the control table already changes on its dates; the events give the narrator the scene);
    - running the Hydian blockade (the Visz storyline);
    - economy and prices (research due Monday), then medicine and bacta, then droids;
    - species traits and bodies, and separate adult and prime ages (already listed).

- **D-406 The Hydian Way blockade narrowed (Jared, 2026-11-22; overnight run item 1).** Amends D-405 [2]'s blockade reading.
  - **Refused** (3,661–3,660; Mandalorians and Imperials pass): a routine trip with an end on the Hydian Way inside the strip (grid columns L–N, rows 14–21), or a trip along the Hydian Way through it. The Hydian Way worlds come from the map's route data: planets whose route list names it, plus stops named in the route's endpoints text (`worldsOnRoute`). In the strip that is Eriadu (M-18) and Malastare (N-16).
  - **Reachable:** the strip's other worlds (for example Sullust, M-17; Eliad, M-20; Thyferra, L-14), with the hop time × 1.5 for the detour (`invented`). Mandalorians and Imperials take no detour.
  - **The narrator:** a trip with an end in the strip in those years gets a "blockade zone: patrols, shortages, scarce passage" line, whoever travels.
  - **Unchanged:** the anchor (Coruscant–Alderaan, 10 h in a common freighter); running the blockade stays in M8 (m8-world-checklist.md item 4).
  - *Defaults taken (reversible, for review):*
    - [a] "Along the Hydian Way through the strip" means both ends are Hydian Way worlds and the strip's rows lie between them (inclusive); a trip between two non-Hydian worlds whose straight line crosses the strip is not refused, since the map has no routing.
    - [b] The zone line goes to everyone; the detour only to those who don't pass.
    - [c] The detour multiplies the hop (before climb-out), like the other hazard factors.

- **D-407 Alien Imperial starts enforced (Jared, 2026-11-22; overnight run item 2; m7-start-checklist.md item 20; D-388 [10]).**
  - **Rule:** before the Battle of Ilum (3,640), a non-Human can't take the regular Imperial military or naval starts. Each Imperial start option carries an `alien` rule:
    - `imperial-soldier`: refused (Humans only; Chiss too, since the liaison route is naval or Intelligence);
    - `imperial-officer`: a Chiss liaison only;
    - `imperial-agent`: a Chiss agent, or any other alien as an informer or asset outside the formal ranks;
    - `korriban-slave`: any species (the Force-sensitive slave route).
  - **Creation** refuses with a message that names the three routes. An alien who passes gets the role as an opening story thread ("A Chiss liaison", "A Chiss agent", "An informer, not an officer"), so the narrator knows it.
  - **Purebloods** follow D-401 [10], not this rule.
  - *Defaults taken (reversible, for review):*
    - [a] Only the species `human` counts as Human; near-Humans (Miraluka, Rattataki, Echani and the rest) count as aliens.
    - [b] The Sith Academy acolyte and the Imperial-born start on Dromund Kaas are left open to any species: they aren't the military, and the request named only the military and naval starts. D-388 [10] lists the slave route as the alien way to Korriban, so these two may need the same rule.
    - [c] The lapse year is 3,640 (D-388 [10]: "formal admission comes after Ilum"); every start option is 3,661-only today, so it matters only for later starts.

- **D-409 The 3,661 era lifetime profile (overnight run item 4; tests/lifetime/era3661.test.ts).**
  - **The profile:** the Average Jedi Guardian with Padawan at 13. They start as a 4-year-old initiate in 3,671 and are a 14-year-old Padawan in 3,661. The run uses the real game from 3,671 to the start of 3,649, on the spread's 20 seeds. It is part of `npm run test:lifetime` (and `test:all`); `ERA_SEEDS=n` gives a quicker look.
  - **The harness:** runLifetime takes a start date and a daily hook (tests/support/lifetime.ts). The defaults are unchanged, so every other profile runs exactly as before.
  - **The probe:** from 3,661, at each new year, on the live state:
    - the state block's Order-seat, leaders and control lines;
    - a canon link for every roster figure, made the way the pipeline makes one (a new NPC by the exact name, the lore index's match for the year, then `npcCanonLinked`);
    - `planTravel` to every closed map world;
    - a travel turn through `resolveTurn` to a closed world, which must pass no time;
    - whether Tython can be reached.
    The character lives in the Coruscant Temple, then moves with the Order to Tython in 3,650.
  - **Gates, all met on 20 of 20 seeds:** no crash; no canon link to an off-board figure (each year links exactly the board, 155–164, and refuses the rest); no trip to a closed world (15–18 tried a year); the seat, Tython, control, leaders and board changes land on their dates (the test lists them).
  - **World events:** none exist yet (M8). The report lists where they would fire: 3,660 (the Hydian Way battle, the Lesser's death, the Rim Campaign), 3,653 (the Sacking, the Treaty), 3,652 (Alderaan, Ord Mantell), 3,651 (Tython rediscovered), 3,650 (the Order moves), 3,649 (Janarus re-elected).
  - **Fixed, found by the profile:** M1-4X's estimated build year was 3,668, so he was on the board in 3,662 and 3,660 around the snapshot's "not built" in 3,661. The lore says built "before 3,643" and not built in 3,661 or 3,653. The estimate is now 3,645. A test now catches any figure that flickers around the snapshot.

- **D-412 Date-agnostic refactor, stage 1: the canon roster as dated spans (Jared's brief, 2026-11-22; docs-v2/spec/date-agnostic-refactor.md §1, §4).** 3,661 is the default start, not the frame for the data.
  - *[1] The shape (src/data/canon/types.ts):* the single `role`, `earlierRoles`, `unavailable` and the `status3661`/`status3653` snapshots are replaced by dated spans, each carrying its source wording and an `estimate` tag where the sources don't date it:
    - **ranks:** rank, role and office spans (Padawan, Jedi Knight, Jedi Master, Grand Master; acolyte, apprentice, Sith Lord, Darth, Dark Councilor; Overseer; military ranks; Supreme Chancellor, Supreme Commander, Mandalore, Sith Emperor). A span can carry its own `faction` (Karrid, Sajar, Praven, Bengel Morr, Parkanas Tark, Ako Domi, Ardun Kothe, Jaesa Willsaam, Elara Dorne, Harron Tavus, Broonmark, Andronikos Revel). Where spans overlap, the later-starting one (then the shorter) is the rank that year, so an office inside a rank wins.
    - **status:** active (a cited fact), imprisoned, stasis, missing, captured, exiled, offRecord. Imprisoned is off the board except at the prison; stasis, missing, captured and offRecord are off it; exiled stays on.
    - **locations:** where the lore puts a figure. Only `placed` spans (the D-401 [3] placements) go to the narrator; other location facts are data.
    - The old 3,661 and 3,653 status wording is kept as cited facts in one-year `active` spans ("status in 3,661 BBY (master index, lore batch 19)").
  - *[2] The data:* src/data/canon/era09Timeline.ts holds every figure's spans (183), merged into the roster entry; era09Status.ts is gone. The leaders table's offices are on the holders' rank spans. Placements (canonPlacements.ts) are now built from the placed location spans plus Overseer Ragate, who is not in a roster.
  - *[3] Typical rank ages (src/data/canon/rankAges.ts; invented, tagged):* Initiate 4, Padawan 13, Knight 25, Master 40, High Council 45, Grand Master 55; acolyte 15, apprentice 20, Sith Lord 22, Darth 35, Dark Councilor 45, Overseer 35; soldier 18, officer 25, general 40, Supreme Commander 50, senator 35, Chancellor 50, official 25, minister 45, bounty hunter, smuggler and criminal 20, crime lord and Mandalore 35. Never below the role's minimum age. A rank whose start isn't on record starts at its typical age; lore boundaries (Satele a Knight to 3,652, Malgus a Darth c. 3,667, Thanaton from 3,678, Bela Kiwiiks a Master from 3,663) are kept.
  - *[4] Birth estimates:* batch 19's "25 at the earliest dated year" rule is dropped. Each estimated birth is re-derived from the figure's rank facts with the typical ages, and its basis is written in `birthBasis`. A test checks every rank starts at an age the role allows (Thanaton and the Barsen'thor are on record younger) and that a first Padawan or acolyte span starts by 30 (Sajar and Praven are adult converts). Examples: Darth Vich 3,703 (the old 3,686 had him born after his death), Decimus 3,698 (promoted to the Council in 3,653), Rictus 3,725 (older than Vowrawn), Nok Drayen 3,696.
  - *[5] The brief's §4 corrections:*
